The intervals are code constants, not operator configuration, at V0. *Rejected: a fixed interval (no backoff) — V-Reliability names retry with backoff as the standing pattern, and a 1 s floor gives a just-started instance a fast first success while the 30 s cap bounds the load on a shared instance; an attempt limit — ruled out by the stay-up ruling; re-checking on every readiness probe — couples check load to probe cadence an orchestrator sets, and a probe is a read, never a trigger.* *(Binary-observable: with the instance down, the `instance_check` records are spaced 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30, … seconds (±10 %) between `completed_at` and the next `run_at`, with `trigger: "schedule"`; with a wrong password the spacing reaches and holds at 300 s; with the instance up, records are spaced 30 s; `ALTER ROLE … SET synchronous_commit = off` is reflected as `ready: false` within 30 s plus one check duration.)* [V-Reliability — retries with backoff; V-Simplicity — three constants, no configuration surface]
- R-0118-d (forced retry and the operator verbs): The forced-retry trigger is **`SIGHUP`** to the host process: on `SIGHUP` the host SHALL re-read the R-0119 password file, re-run the check (coalesced per R-0118-a) with `trigger: "signal"`, rebuild the serving pool (R-0117-d), and reset the backoff. The admin CLI SHALL expose three verbs with distinct jobs. **`mnemra check-db` is the operator's pre-flight and nothing else** — never a probe body: it runs the same check (R-0118-a) **out-of-process** against the configured instance as the serving role, prints the per-clause results and the obligations in human-readable form, and exits by precedence `2` (any clause unverifiable, or the instance unreachable) over `1` (any clause unmet) over `0` (every hard clause met); it does not signal the running host. `mnemra check-db --setup-sql` prints the SQL an operator applies once with their own privileges: `CREATE ROLE` for the owner role and the serving role with the R-0117-c attribute set, the database, `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC`, `CREATE SCHEMA mnemra AUTHORIZATION <owner>`, `GRANT CONNECT` (and no `CREATE` on the database) to both, `GRANT USAGE` on the mnemra schema to both (and `CREATE` to neither — the owner creates by ownership), `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA` plus the matching `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE <owner>` to the serving role, the [P-0020](../src/adrs/P-0020-report-execution-context.md) report role when the reporting engine is enabled — and **no password literal**: each role is created `PASSWORD NULL` and the output ends with the instruction to set each password through `psql`'s `\password <role>`, which hashes client-side under `password_encryption` (R-0117-k) so no secret lands in SQL history or server logs. `mnemra check-db --obligations` prints the R-0117-j list. `mnemra ready` wraps `GET /ready` and `mnemra health` wraps `GET /health`: each reads `MNEMRA_HEALTH_PORT`, uses its own client timeout code constant (`2 s`, below the probe timeout the deployment document tells an orchestrator to set, `≥ 3 s`), and exits `0` on `200`, `1` on `503`, `2` when the listener refuses the connection or does not answer within the timeout — so an orchestrator can tell "not ready" from "no listener". *Rejected: a `POST /ready/recheck` route on the loopback listener — it adds a mutating route to a listener R-0004-g makes `GET`-only and unauthenticated, and a mutating unauthenticated route is a control surface the design has no reason to open; an admin-token-gated HTTP endpoint — no admin HTTP surface exists at V0 and control-plane operations route through the CLI (R-0010-g); making `check-db` signal the host — it would couple an out-of-process verb to process discovery, and `kill -HUP` already exists on every supported platform; passwords in the setup SQL — they would sit in `psql` history, the operator's shell history and the server log.* *(Binary-observable: `kill -HUP <pid>` produces an `instance_check` record with `trigger: "signal"` within 1 s of the in-flight check completing, regardless of the backoff position; `mnemra check-db` against a superuser URL prints `R-0117-c unmet role_overprivileged { attribute: "superuser" }` and exits `1`; against a stopped instance it exits `2`; against a stopped instance with the superuser URL it still exits `2` (precedence); `--setup-sql` output contains no `PASSWORD '` literal, and applied by a superuser to a fresh instance followed by `\password` for both roles yields a serving role for which `check-db` reports `R-0117-c met`; `mnemra ready` with no listener bound exits `2` within 2 s; with a `503` it exits `1`.)* [V-Simplicity — one signal, no new surface; P-LeastAuthority — the only principal that can force a retry is one that can already signal the process; V-Security — no secret in SQL history; R-0010-g]
- R-0118-e (liveness, `GET /health`): `GET /health` SHALL answer the question "is the process alive and not wedged" and nothing else: `200 { "alive": true, "pid", "uptime_s" }` when the host's main async runtime answers an in-process ping within 1 s, `503 { "alive": false }` otherwise. The handler SHALL run on a thread that is **not** the async runtime (the listener already has its own thread, `libs/mnemra-host/health.rs`), send the ping into the runtime, and wait with a `recv_timeout` of 1 s, so a wedged runtime is observed rather than waited on. It SHALL NOT open a connection to the instance, read the check results, or depend on readiness in any way, so an instance outage of any length cannot turn it `503`. **The implication between the two is one-directional by design: `ready` ⇒ `alive`, never the reverse.** `/health` `200` with `/ready` `503` is the normal not-ready state (the process is fine, the instance or its configuration is not); `/ready` `200` with `/health` `503` cannot occur, because a runtime that does not answer the liveness ping cannot have completed a check or be serving. The maintainer asked at the gate whether health can be good while ready is not (activity 7787); the answer is yes, and it is the designed state. An orchestrator's **liveness probe** points here, and its **startup probe**, if any, points here too — a startup probe on readiness would fail for the whole of an instance outage at boot and reach the orchestrator's restart threshold, which is the loop this requirement prevents. **Stated honestly: a startup probe on `/health` verifies only that the listener bound and the runtime answers; the real start-up signal is the first completed instance check**, visible as the first `instance_check` record and as `last_run_at` becoming non-null in `/ready` (R-0118-f). *(Binary-observable: with the instance stopped for ten minutes, `GET /health` returns `200` on every probe; with the host's runtime deliberately blocked (a `test-hooks` injection that parks the runtime), `GET /health` returns `503 { "alive": false }` within 1 s and the listener thread is still answering; a SQL-observation seam shows no statement issued by the `/health` handler.)* [V-Reliability; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — liveness checks liveness; the restart-loop ruling]
- R-0118-f (readiness, `GET /ready`): `GET /ready` SHALL answer "can the host serve a call right now": `200 { "ready": true, "last_run_at", "age_s", "checks": [ … ] }` iff every hard clause of the most recent **completed** check is `met` (or `not_applicable` where this requirement permits it — see below), that check completed no longer ago than the **staleness bound**, where the `checks` entry for R-0117-f (and any clause with an open R-0120-h entry) carries `known_gaps: [ <XFAIL names> ]` generated from the single-sourced XFAIL list — so an operator reading `met` on R-0117-f also reads that two spoofing mechanisms are known failures while they are; `mnemra check-db` prints the same list under the clause — **and** the MCP server is accepting requests (the `mark_ready` gate); otherwise `503 { "ready": false, "reasons": [ { clause, status, reason, detail?, guidance? } ], "next_retry_s", "last_run_at", "age_s", "checks": [ … ] }`. **The staleness bound is derived from the current schedule, not a fixed number**: 2 × the current scheduled interval + the 10 s deadline — 70 s while serving at the 30 s cadence, and 2 × the current backoff + 10 s while not ready, so a 300 s `auth_failed` backoff (R-0118-c) is not masked by a `stale_check` and the operator keeps seeing the true reason. A check older than the bound is the `stale_check` reason: the checker has stopped reporting and readiness is withdrawn rather than carried on a stale `met`. **`not_applicable` is permitted on exactly two clauses** — R-0117-e while the RLS manifest is empty, and the P-0020 report-role presence check while the reporting engine is disabled (R-0013-e as amended); a `not_applicable` on any other clause is a check defect and SHALL be treated as `unmet { reason: "unexpected_not_applicable" }`. The body carries the full most-recent `instance_check` results under `checks`, so an operator reads one endpoint for the why; **detailed reasons appear here and on `mnemra check-db` only** (both loopback-or-local), never on the MCP surface (R-0118-h). During a graceful drain (SIGTERM received) readiness is `503 { "ready": false, "reasons": [ { "reason": "draining" } ] }` while `/health` stays `200` until exit (R-0118-l). An orchestrator's **readiness probe** points here. `ready` ⇒ `alive` always (a `200` here implies `/health` is `200`); the converse never holds (R-0118-e). The listener stays loopback-only (R-0004-g), so at V0 an orchestrator reaches both probes by **exec** (`mnemra health`, `mnemra ready`), not by HTTP from outside the pod; the non-loopback bind, and the status-code-only response to unauthenticated callers it would require, is R-0004-g's existing tripwire, re-stated as a deferral in Out of Scope. **The status code is a function of the clause statuses and nothing else**: a property test SHALL drive the route over every combination of clause status (`met`, `unmet`, `unverifiable`, `not_applicable`), staleness and the `mark_ready` gate, and assert `200` iff all hard clauses are `met` (or `not_applicable` on exactly the two permitted clauses), fresh under the schedule-derived bound, and the gate is open — seeded with the embedded-era defect (`libs/mnemra-host/health.rs:627-629` at `57cfeca` returned `200` for `overall: "down"`) and with a `not_applicable` on a non-permitted clause as the pinned failing cases. *(Binary-observable: before `mark_ready`, `503` with `reason: "starting"` and `last_run_at: null`; after, with all hard met, `200 { "ready": true, "age_s": < 70 }`; with R-0117-i unmet, `503` whose `reasons[0].clause` is `"R-0117-i"` and whose `next_retry_s` equals the current backoff; **with a wrong password held for 120 s, every `GET /ready` in that window returns `503` with `reasons[0].reason = "auth_failed"` and never `"stale_check"`**; with the checker parked by a `test-hooks` injection for 80 s while serving, `503` with `reason: "stale_check"` and `age_s ≥ 70`; after SIGTERM, `503` with `"draining"`; the property test passes and fails on both seeded cases; `checks[R-0117-f].known_gaps` is non-empty exactly when the XFAIL list is non-empty — a test seeds an empty list and asserts the key is `[]`, and seeds one entry and asserts it is named.)* [P-ReliabilityTarget — the indicator is read from the consumer's side; V-Observability — the why is machine-readable and a missing signal is detected; P-TrustworthySignal]
- R-0118-g (probe mapping, stated once): liveness probe → `GET /health` (exec `mnemra health`); readiness probe → `GET /ready` (exec `mnemra ready`); startup probe → `GET /health` or none, never `/ready`; pre-flight before first start, and the operator's diagnostic when `/ready` says no → `mnemra check-db` (never wired as a probe: it opens its own connection and runs the full check, which is the wrong cost and the wrong semantics for a probe cadence). If two of these contracts share an implementation, the shared part is the `InstanceFacts` probe and the check (R-0118-a); the three surfaces SHALL stay distinct in route, body and semantics, because merging them is exactly what makes a database outage restart a process. *(Binary-observable: the operator deployment document carries this mapping verbatim and the docs test of R-0117-j covers it.)* [V-Consistency — the same question gets the same endpoint across deployments]
- R-0118-h (MCP on stdio while not ready — coarse code, no detail): While readiness is false, the MCP server SHALL still complete `initialize` and answer `tools/list` (so a client sees a live server and the verbs it will have), and SHALL answer every `tools/call` and every other verb-dispatching request with the structured JSON-RPC error `host_not_ready` (custom code; API Contract error table) whose `data` carries **only** `kind: "not_ready" | "draining"` and `next_retry_s`. It SHALL NOT carry the clause list, reasons, details or guidance: while the instance is unreachable the token lookup (`admin_tokens`, in the database) cannot run, so every stdio caller is **unauthenticated** at that moment, and an unauthenticated caller learns nothing about the instance's configuration from the error; the detailed reasons live on `GET /ready` (loopback) and `mnemra check-db` (local), R-0118-f, and the error's `data` carries a fixed `hint: "run mnemra ready or mnemra check-db on the host"` so an operator reading a client log knows where to look. **Reconciliation with R-0010-c** (`DF-auth-check` on every incoming request before routing to any builtin or plugin handler): while not ready, `initialize` and `tools/list` are answered from the server's static state (protocol capabilities and the manifest-declared verb names, which are public in the repository's signed manifests) **without routing to any builtin or plugin handler**, and every other request is refused with `host_not_ready` **before** routing; so no request reaches a handler without the auth check, and R-0010-c's "before routing" clause holds — the two protocol requests are not routed at all. R-0010-c carries an amendment note to this effect. No host-fn executes, no `WorkspaceCtx` is constructed for the call, and no plugin instance is invoked. The host SHALL NOT close the stdio transport or exit because readiness is false: a client that treats a closed transport as a dead server re-spawns the host, which is the stdio form of the restart loop. *(Binary-observable: a client connected over stdio to a host whose instance is down completes `initialize`, receives a `tools/list` naming `echo.create`, and receives `host_not_ready` with `data = { kind: "not_ready", next_retry_s }` and no other key on `echo.create`; the same client, without reconnecting, receives a success on `echo.create` within one retry interval plus the hysteresis of the instance coming up.)* [maintainer ruling 2026-10-19; R-0010-f — error classes are distinguishable; V-Security — no configuration detail to an unauthenticated caller; V-Reliability]
- R-0118-i (no soft class at V0; the first entrant's design, with the mechanism that fires it): **No soft class, no `capability_unavailable` error, no `degraded` field, gate or test exists at V0** (maintainer ruling 2026-10-19, activity 7771: "build it when needed"). Recorded once, as design content for the first soft entrant and not as a contract: a soft clause would declare, in the R-0117 table, the closed set of capabilities it gates (MCP verbs, host-fns or subsystems by name); on drift those capabilities would refuse with a structured error naming the clause and reason while everything else serves, and readiness would report `200` with a `degraded` list naming them; a clause whose capability set cannot be stated stays hard. The first expected entrant is R-0117-b (pgvector), gating the embedding-write and dense-retrieval capabilities, once the retrieval cluster's degraded-mode matrix ([P-0014](../src/adrs/P-0014-retrieval-architecture.md)) is live. **What fires the decision** (P-Defer DF1, at the last cheap point): a test in the migrations suite SHALL fail when a migration introduces the first column of type `vector` while R-0117-b is still classified hard with no capability set — the failing test names this clause and forces the classification before the column lands. *(Binary-observable: no V0 verb returns an error naming a soft clause; `GET /ready`'s `200` body carries no `degraded` key; the migrations-suite test fails on a seeded `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN embedding vector(1536)` and passes without it.)* [maintainer ruling 7771; P-Defer DF1 — the trip-wire is a test that fires on the migration, not a memory; V-Simplicity]
- R-0118-j (not-ready is visible: metrics, alert, log hygiene): The host SHALL emit, on the R-0004 OTel surface, the gauge `host_ready` (`1`/`0`) with a `clause` label naming the first unmet clause (or `none`), the counter `instance_check_total{trigger, status}` (one increment per run, `status ∈ { met, unmet, unverifiable }` for the run as a whole), the histogram `instance_check_duration_seconds`, and the gauge `seconds_since_last_ok`. The deployment document SHALL carry one documented alert: `host_ready == 0` for longer than **5 minutes** (the default; the maintainer may change it, AE4) — "the host is up and has not served for N minutes; read `/ready` or run `mnemra check-db`". Log records for readiness SHALL be emitted at `INFO` **on state change only** (`ready → not ready` with the first reason; `not ready → ready`); the steady state — a check that repeats the same result — SHALL NOT produce a log line per run, only the metrics, so a day-long outage is one line and a gauge, not 2 880 lines. *(Binary-observable: with the instance stopped for ten minutes, the metrics surface shows `host_ready{clause="R-0117-i"} 0`, `seconds_since_last_ok` rising past 600, `instance_check_total{trigger="schedule",status="unmet"}` incrementing, and exactly one `INFO` readiness log line for the transition; the deployment document's alert rule names `host_ready` and `5m`.)* [V-Observability — a surface that is up but not serving must be visible as such, and an alert is the instrument; R-0004-a/-d; Bolt H5]
- R-0118-k (supervision models and stdin): At V0 the only MCP transport is stdio (R-0010-a/-e), so the host is **always a child of the agent session that spawned it**: stdin EOF is a clean stop (R-0121-a), SIGTERM is a clean stop, the agent's process supervisor is the host's supervisor, and the exec probes are run by whatever manages that session. **A daemon model — the host under systemd, docker or Kubernetes with no stdio client — has no transport to serve at V0 and is therefore not supported**, stated here so the container snippets in the deployment document are read as "the operator's Postgres plus a stdio host", not as a service unit. The daemon model's design content is recorded for the **HTTPS shared-service transport amendment, tracker #4127** (maintainer rulings 2026-10-19, activities 7787–7790: one long-running mnemra service that every agent session reaches over streamable-HTTP MCP, HTTPS/TLS only, localhost included, one code path local and remote — "change the model so its acts like remote even when local"; it amends R-0010-a/-e, which stay as written in this amendment). #4127 is the trigger — a filed task, the next amendment, not a generic future event: the daemon's stop is SIGTERM only and stdin EOF SHALL NOT exit it; `sd_notify` `READY=1` is sent on the first completed check with every hard clause met and `WATCHDOG=1` is fed from the liveness ping (so a wedged runtime stops feeding it); `ExecReload=kill -HUP $MAINPID`; `Restart=on-failure` is safe only because a not-ready host exits `0` on SIGTERM and never on a check result; readiness for a unit without `sd_notify` is `ExecStartPost` polling `mnemra ready`. The deployment document SHALL carry a compose snippet for the operator's Postgres+pgvector with the host as a stdio child — with the host service's `stdin_open: true` (and `tty: false`), because a container started with a closed stdin delivers EOF at once and R-0121-a's EOF-is-a-clean-stop would drain the host at start — and SHALL state the daemon model as unsupported until #4127 lands; the CI fresh-machine job (R-0120-g) exercises the compose snippet, which is how a missing `stdin_open` would show up. *(Binary-observable: the deployment document's supervision section states exactly the two models and marks the daemon one unsupported at V0; a host spawned with its stdin closed exits `0` after a clean drain; the CI fresh-machine job runs the documented compose file.)* [R-0010-a/-e; V-Honesty — a model with no transport is not claimed; P-Defer — the daemon content is recorded with a self-announcing trigger]
- R-0118-l (graceful drain): On SIGTERM or stdin EOF the host SHALL: mark readiness `503 { "draining" }` at once (R-0118-f); answer every new verb-dispatching request with `host_not_ready { kind: "draining" }` (R-0118-h); let in-flight verbs complete up to a **drain deadline** code constant of `10 s` (below the 30 s interval), after which each still-running verb is cut and recorded as R-0004-a `outcome = "error"`; ignore `SIGHUP` during the drain; cancel an in-flight instance check and start no new one; close the serving pool; and exit `0` when every in-flight verb completed, non-zero when the deadline cut any. A **second SIGTERM** during the drain SHALL exit immediately, non-zero. `/health` stays `200` until exit. The embedded-era `/health` drain scenario in this spec ("`/health` degraded and down states") is superseded by this clause. *(Binary-observable: a host with one in-flight slow verb receiving SIGTERM answers a new `echo.get` with `host_not_ready { kind: "draining" }`, returns the slow verb's result, and exits `0` within 10 s; with a verb that never completes it exits non-zero at 10 s with an `outcome = "error"` metric for that verb; a `SIGHUP` during the drain produces no `instance_check` record; a second SIGTERM exits non-zero within 1 s.)* [V-Reliability — graceful degradation with a bound; R-0121-a; Bolt M2]
- R-0118-m (database loss while serving): A pool acquire or driver error on a host-fn path SHALL surface to the caller as the distinct JSON-RPC error `storage_unavailable` (custom code; API Contract error table), never as a generic internal error and never as `host_not_ready` (the host *was* ready when the call was accepted); pool acquire is bounded by an **acquire timeout** code constant of `5 s`. An observed pool or driver error SHALL trigger an immediate out-of-cycle instance check with `trigger: "error"`, rate-limited to one such trigger per backoff floor (1 s) and coalesced per R-0118-a; if the check finds R-0117-i unmet, readiness goes false and subsequent calls get `host_not_ready`. A write cut by a connection loss after the host sent `COMMIT` but before the reply arrived is **unacknowledged** to the caller (`storage_unavailable`), and R-0121-a makes no claim about it. *(Binary-observable: killing the instance while a verb's query is in flight yields `storage_unavailable` to that caller within 5 s, an `instance_check { trigger: "error" }` record, and `503 /ready` with `R-0117-i unreachable`; a verb issued after that gets `host_not_ready`; the mid-write kill scenario under R-0121.)* [V-Reliability — a dependency failure mid-call is designed, not discovered; R-0010-f — error classes are distinguishable; Bolt M3]

### R-0119 — Connection configuration and the credential at rest (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018; anchors [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md) §Amendment 2026-10-19; replaces R-0116-e's no-stored-secret design for an instance the host does not own)

*R-0116-e dissolved the credential question by owning the cluster and reaching it over a peer-authenticated socket. The operator's instance may be remote, so a credential can exist; this requirement bounds where it lives and keeps the no-secret shape available where it still fits. ⚠ The Postgres client library the host uses parses far more than it inspects (Warden, MEASURED in sqlx-postgres 0.9.0 `options/parse.rs:69-111`): `?password=`, `?host=` / `?hostaddr=` overrides, `options=…`, multi-host lists, `PG*` environment defaults, an always-run `~/.pgpass` lookup, and unknown query keys logged **with their values**. A URL denylist cannot close that; the host therefore never hands the URL to the driver. Keystone anchors: **V-DataMinimization** — a credential that need not exist is not held, so peer authentication over a socket stays the recommended local shape; **V-Security** — a secret never travels in the environment, the command line or a log, and the connection options are built from an allowlist; **P-SecurityLayered** — the control enumerates the permitted, and a control that errors denies; **V-Consistency** — the file-under-the-host-uid shape is the one R-0008-e and R-0014-d already use.*

- R-0119-a (connection options are built explicitly from an allowlist; the driver never sees the URL or the environment): The host SHALL read `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL`, parse it itself, and construct the driver's connection options **field by field** (`PgConnectOptions` or its successor: user, one host **or** one socket directory, port, database, `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `application_name = "mnemra-host"`, the password from R-0119-b when and only when R-0119-d permits one); it SHALL NOT call the driver's URL parser and SHALL NOT let the driver read any `PG*` environment variable. The URL grammar the host accepts is a **closed allowlist**: scheme `postgres` or `postgresql`; userinfo with a user and **no password**; exactly one host component — a literal IPv4/IPv6 address, a hostname, or empty with `host=/absolute/socket/dir` in the query; an optional port; a database path; and query keys from exactly `{ host, port, sslmode, sslrootcert }`. Any other query key (`password`, `hostaddr`, `options`, `sslkey`, `sslcert`, `passfile`, unknown keys), a multi-host list, a `hostaddr`, or a password in userinfo SHALL be refused with `config_rejected { reason }` naming the offending key, before any connection is attempted and **without logging the offending value**. At process start the host SHALL scrub every `PG*` variable (`PGPASSWORD`, `PGPASSFILE`, `PGHOST`, `PGHOSTADDR`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGSSLMODE`, `PGSSLROOTCERT`, `PGOPTIONS`, `PGSERVICE`, `PGSERVICEFILE` and the rest of the libpq set) from its own environment, so neither the driver nor a child process inherits one — and because `std::env::remove_var` is `unsafe` under edition 2024 (the workspace's edition; it races any concurrent reader of the environment), the scrub SHALL run in `main` **before the async runtime or any other thread starts**, as the first statement after argument parsing, with a source check asserting that ordering. Local-versus-remote (R-0117-f, R-0119-d) SHALL be classified from the **parsed** options — socket directory present, or host is a literal loopback address — never from the URL text. An unset or empty `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` yields `connection_not_configured`. All of these are hard, non-exiting refusals (R-0118-b). The host SHALL log the connection as user, host-or-socket, port and database only (R-0004-h). *Rejected: a password inside the URL or a `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD` variable — the process environment is readable by every same-uid process and appears in crash dumps and diagnostics; a denylist of URL keys — the driver's grammar is wider than any list the host maintains, and an omission fails open.* *(Binary-observable — **one seeded test per bypass**: `postgres://u:secret@h/d` → `config_rejected { reason: "password_in_url" }` and `secret` in no record; `postgres://u@h/d?password=secret` → `reason: "query_key_forbidden", key: "password"` and `secret` in no record; `?hostaddr=10.0.0.9` → `key: "hostaddr"`; `?options=-c%20…` → `key: "options"`; `postgres://u@h1,h2/d` → `reason: "multi_host"`; `?frobnicate=1` → `key: "frobnicate"`; with `PGPASSWORD=secret` and `PGHOST=evil` in the spawn environment the host's own `/proc/self/environ` (Linux) carries neither after start and the connection goes to the configured host; with a seeded `~/.pgpass` line matching the configured host and user, a peer-auth socket connection sends no password (R-0119-e); the options object handed to the driver has `application_name = "mnemra-host"`.)* [P-SecurityLayered — enumerate the permitted; P-GuaranteeByMechanism; V-Security; Warden H1] *(Amended 2026-10-25, #4119 (maintainer ruling 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8289, Peter, by AskUserQuestion in the design-lane session: fold the host's repeated-key refusal into the #4119 amendment): **a repeated query key is refused.** A query key that appears more than once SHALL be refused with `config_rejected { reason: "duplicate_key", key }`, naming the key and never a value, before any connection is attempted, whether or not the repeated values agree. The allowlist is checked first: a key outside `{ host, port, sslmode, sslrootcert }` is refused as `query_key_forbidden` whether or not it repeats. As approved, this clause was silent on a repeated allowlisted key, so which of two values the host keeps fell to the implementation, and an operator who wrote `sslmode=verify-full&…&sslmode=require` has stated two transport floors; the driver this clause keeps the URL from applies the last of a repeated key (Warden, MEASURED in #4119 round 3, `options/parse.rs:50-109`). The rule is part of the grammar, so it binds every mode of the one parser R-0120-a(7) calls, `host` included. *Rejected: amending at the next V0 amendment — the test modes of R-0120-a(7) already refuse a repeated key, so one parser would carry two answers to one input until an amendment that has no date; leaving the clause silent because the host classifies and builds its options from one parse, so a repeated key cannot split its check from its use — true, but the ambiguous configuration would still be admitted under whichever value the implementation keeps, and a closed allowlist admits each permitted key once.* *(Binary-observable, added: `postgres://u@127.0.0.1/m?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/p&sslmode=require` → `config_rejected { reason: "duplicate_key", key: "sslmode" }` and no connection attempted; `…?sslmode=verify-full&sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/p` (equal values) → the same; `…?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/p&sslrootcert=/zzqq-dup-value` → `key: "sslrootcert"`, and `zzqq-dup-value` appears in no emitted record; `…?password=zzqq-pw-one&password=zzqq-pw-two` → `reason: "query_key_forbidden", key: "password"`, and neither `zzqq-pw-one` nor `zzqq-pw-two` appears in any emitted record; admitted, each key once: `postgres://u@127.0.0.1/m?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/p` with a password file connects (R-0119-d), and `postgres://mnemra@/mnemra?host=/var/run/postgresql` reaches `R-0117-i met` (R-0119-c).)* [P-SecurityLayered — enumerate the permitted, each permitted key once; V-Consistency — one grammar across the four modes of R-0120-a(7); maintainer ruling 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8289])*
- R-0119-b (the password file — read safely, re-checked on every read, stay-up on failure): When R-0119-d permits a password, the host SHALL read it from the file named by `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` (default `~/.config/mnemra/db-password`, beside the admin token file, R-0008-e). **Every read** — at boot, on `SIGHUP`, and by `mnemra check-db` — SHALL open the path with `O_NOFOLLOW` (a symlink is refused), `fstat` the **opened descriptor** (never a path-based `stat`, so there is no check-then-open window), and require: a regular file; no group or world **write** bit and no world **read** bit; and owner = host uid with no group read bit (the bare-host shape, `0600`) — **or**, only when the operator has set `MNEMRA_CREDENTIAL_MOUNT=1` **and** the process observes container evidence — any of `/.dockerenv`, `/run/.containerenv`, a `/proc/1/cgroup` line naming a container runtime, or an orchestrator environment marker (`KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST`, or `container` as set by podman and systemd-nspawn); the orchestrator marker is needed because under cgroup v2 with containerd or Kubernetes `/proc/1/cgroup` is typically `0::/` and neither marker file exists, so file evidence alone would refuse the primary mounted-Secret shape — *all of these markers INFERRED from general knowledge, not measured; the integration test under the CI container is the measurement* — owner = root with the file readable through a group the host belongs to (the Kubernetes Secret-mount shape: root-owned, `fsGroup`-grouped, `defaultMode: 0440`). The relaxation is opt-in and evidence-gated so a bare host never silently accepts a root-owned group-readable secret; the container section of the deployment document sets the variable, a root-owned file without the variable reports `credential_file_rejected { reason: "owner" }` whose guidance names the variable, and the variable without container evidence reports `reason: "not_a_container"`. *Residual, labelled: the evidence is a marker file, a cgroup string or an environment variable, any of which a root-level writer on a bare host could plant; the gate is against misconfiguration, not against a root-level adversary, and the deployment document says so. The Kubernetes shape itself is **latent at V0**: a stdio host has no transport to serve under an orchestrator (R-0118-k), so the orchestrator marker becomes load-bearing only with the HTTPS shared-service transport amendment (#4127), and it is written now so that amendment does not re-open this clause.* A failed check SHALL NOT exit: it reports `credential_file_rejected { path, reason, guidance }` as a hard not-ready reason (maintainer ruling 2026-10-19, activity 7762), **drops any previously loaded secret** from memory so a later successful read cannot be shadowed by a stale one, and the `guidance` names `chmod 0600` for a file the operator owns and `defaultMode: 0400`/`0440` plus `fsGroup` for a mounted Secret. The file's trailing newline, if any, is stripped. The host SHALL re-read the file on every forced retry (R-0118-d), so the operator's rotation procedure is: set the new password on the instance (`\password`, R-0118-d), write the file, `SIGHUP` the host; the pool is rebuilt and new connections use the new value. No automatic rotation exists; rotation is the operator's and this is its reload path. **The password-file shape is the primary shape for containers** (a mounted Secret), while peer authentication over a socket (R-0119-c) is the primary shape for a bare host; the deployment document SHALL present each first in its own section. MEASURED at `57cfeca` (Warden M3): the existing 5-pre check wires only `check_admin_token` (`libs/mnemra-host/mnemra_host.rs:521`), checks no ownership (`libs/mnemra-host/startup/file_mode_check.rs:67`) and is path-based (`:106`); this clause does **not** reuse it. *(Binary-observable: a password file at mode `0644` yields `503 /ready` with `reason: "credential_file_rejected"` and `guidance` naming both remedies, the process alive; a symlink at the path yields `reason: "symlink"`; a root-owned `0440` file in a group the host holds is rejected with `reason: "owner"` on a bare host, rejected with `reason: "not_a_container"` when `MNEMRA_CREDENTIAL_MOUNT=1` is set without container evidence, and accepted with the variable set inside a container (a `test-hooks` injection supplies the marker); a root-owned `0400` file is rejected with `reason: "unreadable"` either way; after a rejected read following an earlier good one, the pool's next connection attempt uses **no** password (the old secret is gone) and R-0117-i reports `auth_failed`, never a silent success; after `ALTER ROLE … PASSWORD`, writing the file and `kill -HUP`, the next `instance_check` reports `R-0117-i met` and the pool's new connections authenticate; no emitted record contains the file's contents.)* [maintainer ruling 7762; V-Security; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the check is on the descriptor the read uses; V-Consistency — the R-0008-e / R-0014-d file shape; P-LeastAuthority]
- R-0119-c (peer authentication, no secret, server-side identity checked first): When the options name a unix socket directory, the host SHALL use peer authentication and no password (a password file is not read), and before sending the first byte on the socket SHALL perform the R-0117-f(ii) peer-uid and socket-directory check; this is the **recommended** shape for a bare-host local instance and the operator document SHALL present it first in the bare-host section. The ident map is the operator's (R-0117-j item 5). *(Binary-observable: with `postgres://mnemra@/mnemra?host=/var/run/postgresql`, `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` unset and no default file present, the check reports `R-0117-i met` and the host serves; a socket pre-created by a test in a directory owned by another uid is refused with `transport_unauthenticated` and zero bytes written.)* [V-DataMinimization — a credential that need not exist is not held; V-Simplicity; maintainer ruling 7771 — local spoofing is mitigated, not accepted]
- R-0119-d (the transport floor is enforced on the parsed options): From the parsed options the host SHALL classify the connection as **socket** (socket directory present) or **TCP** (any host, loopback included). TCP SHALL require `sslmode = verify-full` and a `sslrootcert`; any weaker or absent `sslmode` is refused with `config_rejected { reason: "sslmode_below_floor" }` before connecting. A password (R-0119-b) is permitted **only** on a TCP connection that satisfies that floor; a socket connection with a password file configured is refused with `config_rejected { reason: "password_over_socket" }`, and a TCP connection without a password file is refused with `connection_not_configured { missing: "password_file" }` (because `trust` is banned by R-0117-j item 6 and `peer` does not exist on TCP). *(Binary-observable: `postgres://u@127.0.0.1/m` with a password file → `sslmode_below_floor`; `…?sslmode=require` → the same; `…?sslmode=verify-full` without `sslrootcert` → `config_rejected { reason: "sslrootcert_missing" }`; `…?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/p` with a password file → connects; `postgres://u@/m?host=/run/pg` with a password file configured → `password_over_socket`.)* [P-SecurityLayered — the floor is a configuration-time control ahead of the runtime check; R-0117-f; Warden H2]
- R-0119-e (`.pgpass` is closed by mechanism): The driver's `~/.pgpass` / `PGPASSFILE` lookup runs on every connect where no password was set (Warden, MEASURED: `apply_pgpass()` always runs in sqlx-postgres 0.9.0). The host SHALL make that lookup inert — by the mechanism the implementation chooses (an explicit empty-password sentinel the driver treats as set, pointing the lookup at a path that cannot exist, or a driver option if one lands) — such that **no password reaches the wire except the one R-0119-b supplied**. *(Binary-observable: with a seeded `~/.pgpass` whose line matches the configured socket, user and database, and no password file configured, the peer-auth connection sends no password message (driver trace or a fake-server harness) and succeeds; with the same `~/.pgpass` and a TCP configuration whose password file is wrong, R-0117-i reports `auth_failed` rather than succeeding on the `.pgpass` value.)* [P-GuaranteeByMechanism — the second path is closed by a test, not a sentence; V-Honesty — the earlier "SHALL NOT depend on it" is replaced by a measured fact and a mechanism]
- R-0119-f (pool size, connection lifetime and the connection arithmetic): The serving pool's maximum size SHALL be the operator-configurable `MNEMRA_DB_POOL_SIZE` (default `8`, range `1..=64`, a value outside the range refused with `config_rejected`), with the acquire timeout of R-0118-m and a **maximum connection lifetime** code constant of `5 min` (R-0117-d). The host's connection budget on the instance is: steady state pool size + 1 (the instance check's **dedicated** connection, R-0118-a); during a pool rebuild (R-0117-d) transiently up to 2 × pool size + 1; and + 1 while `mnemra init` runs under the owner role. R-0117-j item 4 states the `max_connections` arithmetic the operator sizes against with the 2× factor. *(Binary-observable: with `MNEMRA_DB_POOL_SIZE=3` and three verbs held in flight, a fourth verb's acquire waits and fails with `storage_unavailable` after 5 s; `pg_stat_activity` for the serving role never exceeds 4 rows in steady state and never exceeds 7 during a `SIGHUP` rebuild; a pooled connection's `backend_start` is never older than 5 min plus one acquire; `MNEMRA_DB_POOL_SIZE=0` and `=65` are refused.)* [V-Reliability — bounded resources; V-Observability — the budget is a stated number the operator can size to]
- R-0119-g (the owner credential — held only by `mnemra init`): `mnemra init` SHALL connect as the owner role using `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` (same grammar and allowlist as R-0119-a) and `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_PASSWORD_FILE` (same rules as R-0119-b) or peer authentication; the serving host SHALL NOT read either variable and SHALL NOT hold the owner credential at any time. **The owner credential SHALL be absent at serve time**: a serving process started with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` or `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_PASSWORD_FILE` set, or with the default owner password-file path present and readable by the host uid, reports the hard not-ready reason `owner_credential_present { guidance }` and does not serve — the serving uid can read any file it can read, and the two-role split stops SQL injection but not a compromised host process, so the owner secret must not be reachable from the serving process's uid at all (supplied to `mnemra init` only, on the operator's session, and removed or made unreadable afterwards; the ident map must not map the serving uid to the owner role, R-0117-j item 5). **The limit of this check, named:** `owner_credential_present` sees exactly the two variables and the default owner password-file path; a copy of the owner secret anywhere else the serving uid can read is invisible to it, so "the owner credential is unreachable from the serving uid at all" stays an **obligation** (R-0117-j item 5), not a verified clause. The two credentials may name the same instance and database; they SHALL name different roles, and `mnemra init` SHALL refuse (`config_rejected { reason: "owner_equals_serving" }`) when the owner URL's user equals the serving URL's user. *(Binary-observable: `mnemra init` with the serving role's URL in `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` is refused; the serving host spawned with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` set reports `503 /ready` with `owner_credential_present`; spawned with the default owner password file present at mode `0600` under the host uid it reports the same; no serving-path code reads either variable (a source check); after `init`, the serving role's `pg_class.relowner` and `nspowner` scans (R-0117-c) report `met`.)* [P-LeastAuthority — the authority to change the schema is never standing in the serving process; maintainer ruling 7771]

### R-0120 — Test substrate: how test fixtures obtain a Postgres, locally and in CI, with no embedded engine (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018; anchors [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md) §Amendment 2026-10-19, R-0018-b, the CI tier-2 spec `docs/specs/2026-07-02-ci-flake-tier2-shared-engine.md` R-0026 / R-0027 / R-0032 / R-0033)

*R-0018-b stands: integration tests run against a real Postgres with the real extension, never a mock. The tier-2 spec's fixture shape — one acquisition entry point, one boot per binary, a fresh uniquely named database per test, an application-role pool and a named privileged provisioning path, a guard lint and a membership enumerator — transfers almost unchanged; what changes is where the engine comes from. ⚠ That spec is `approved` and names `EmbeddedEngine::start` / `postgresql_embedded` as its guarded symbols (R-0032) and "boots the binary's engine" (R-0026); this amendment falsifies those words, which is raised for that spec's own amendment (P-0010 §Amendment, "Records this amendment falsifies"), not edited here. Keystone anchors: **P-TrustworthySignal** — a missing substrate fails the test loudly and never skips; **P-TestableByDesign** — the fixture takes its server as an injected input; **V-Consistency** — one mechanism for local, CI and smoke.*

> **Amended 2026-10-20, #4119 (maintainer ruling 2026-10-20, #4119 activity 7849: fold the R-0120 corrections into the CI-spec amendment; CI and local tests connect over TLS exactly as production does, with no test-only transport bypass).** The round-1 reviews of the CI-spec amendment (Bolt, Charon, Warden) found clauses of R-0120 that a conforming implementation could not satisfy or that fail open: the superuser URL was confined by *reader* while exported to every process in the gate; the host-under-test could not connect in CI at all under R-0119-d (no TLS, no password file); "ours" was decided by name prefix with `LIKE`; a failed teardown was silent; the exception set the guard lint inherits was wider than this clause stated. Each correction is appended to its clause as *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119: …)*, the original text retained, following this spec's own #4018 precedent. **Revised after the round-2 reviews and maintainer ruling 2026-10-22 (#4119 activity 7867):** the CI container is started by a job step under six stated conditions (R-0120-c(1)); the superuser credential takes R-0119's own shape, a URL with no password plus a `0600` password file, and the leak check admits its one holder by compile-time identity (R-0120-a(1)); the harness role's attributes are checked at acquisition (R-0120-a(6)); the sentinel exception is a predicate over the URL, not only a byte-exact literal (R-0120-f(ii)); the `schema_init` and `coordination_schema` exceptions retire under three conditions (R-0120-f). **Revised again 2026-10-22, in the one scoped pass maintainer ruling 2026-10-22 ordered (#4119 activity 8037), after the #4119 threat model, the PostgreSQL 16.4 measurements of activities 8047 and 8064, and the rulings of activity 8067:** every test URL goes through R-0119-a's parser in one of four modes, and `PG*` variables are refused in test processes (R-0120-a(7)); the harness check is an allowlist in R-0117-c's query shape, keyed on `session_user` (a(6)); the production-store guard is a name allowlist with no cache (a(8)); the PostgreSQL 16 self-grant, the template's `IS_TEMPLATE` flag and the version floor are stated (a(9)); one run owner tears down on every exit path (a(5)); run-scoped passwords are set as SCRAM verifiers (a(2)); the superuser location is named and the gated recipe never skips (a(1)); the gated cell counts only when every chain `just ci` runs, locally or in CI, reaches it (tier-2 R-0033); and the fresh-machine job runs as its own job (R-0120-g). **Amended 2026-10-25 (maintainer ruling 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8289, Peter, by AskUserQuestion in the design-lane session):** R-0119-a, approved under #4018, refuses a repeated query key with `duplicate_key`; the note is appended to R-0119-a, and the four modes of R-0120-a(7) inherit it. What this pass does not mitigate is recorded as accepted risks `AR-0014` to `AR-0019` in the overview register; the threat model's remaining controls are tasks #4162 to #4166 and #4153. R-0120-h is unchanged. The CI specs this clause anchors (`docs/specs/2026-07-02-ci-flake-tier2-shared-engine.md`, `docs/specs/2026-06-26-ci-flake-tier1-serialize.md`, `docs/specs/2026-07-15-ci-coverage-shard.md`) are amended in the same change and cite these corrections by clause. The `.bom.toml` sidecar is re-pinned at this amendment's exit gate (ruling 7849 item 3), not here.

> **Amended 2026-10-28, #4363 (drafted on the design lane; apart from the direction the maintainer ruled, every lock it adds is a draft until his exit approval).** What the maintainer ruled:
> - on the cross-lane `ci-reap` kill, *"i consider this a defect"* (Peter, 2026-10-28, recorded in #3575 activity 8906, which also records that the fix direction was not ruled);
> - of the options then put to him, "Test two lanes in #4161" (#3575 activity 8909);
> - later the same day, only "Separate server per lane (Recommended): each lane gets its own Postgres server and port, so lanes cannot interfere"; not selected were "one harness role per lane (D2), keep sharing and patch in round 3, cut scope and park" (#4363 activity 9016, Puck's record of his selection; the option wording was Puck's);
> - for the lane's identity, "Declared lane name" (#4363 activity 9037, Puck's record; the option wording was Puck's).
>
> R-0120-a had stated that "two lanes may share one server". This amendment supersedes that premise with **one server per lane, enforced by a lane claim** (R-0120-i(a)). It adds **R-0120-i**, which keeps the concurrency guarantee for concurrent runs within one lane, and it makes the corrections to locked text that R-0120-i needs. Those corrections are the #4363 draft's, not rulings. They are appended to R-0120-a, R-0120-c, R-0120-d and R-0120-e as *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363: …)*, with the original text retained, and each is put to the maintainer by name in R-0120-i(h).
>
> The same change also:
> - amends tier-1 R-0024 (`docs/specs/2026-06-26-ci-flake-tier1-serialize.md`) for #4361's interim reap;
> - appends notes to tier-2 R-0027 and R-0033 (`docs/specs/2026-07-02-ci-flake-tier2-shared-engine.md`);
> - amends accepted risk `AR-0016` (`docs/src/architecture/overview.md`) in place, retiring its acceptance of lanes sharing one server and one harness role, and keeping its acceptance for runs within one lane; and notes on the same page why the data-flow entity `EE-other-lane` keeps its name;
> - adds `## Amendment 2026-10-28` to ADR P-0031 (`docs/src/adrs/P-0031-test-substrate-isolation.md`), whose context said lanes "may share one server".
>
> **The copies of the sharing premise this change reconciles** were found by a phrase-level sweep, `grep -r -n -o -i -E` over `docs/src`, `docs/specs` and `docs/intent`, for the phrases "a server two lanes (may) share", "(may) share(d) one server", "two lanes may share", "two-lane server", "shared with another lane", "other lane's run / included / live", "across lanes" and "lanes sharing a / one server". It counts phrases rather than lines, because R-0120-a is one line of about 69 KB, and a line-level search counts it once whatever it holds. Each copy is reconciled in this change: R-0120-a and R-0120-b in this spec (#4363-C8); tier-2 R-0027, R-0028, and its §Amendment row on the harness role; `AR-0016` and the `TB-test-postgres` row in the overview; P-0031; and `docs/intent/postmaster-ownership-marker.md`, whose line-8 banner carries a dated #4363 note below it, in that locked intake's own form. *Superseded (round 3 draft):* "Besides this spec, tier-2 R-0027, `AR-0016` and P-0031, the one other copy is `docs/intent/postmaster-ownership-marker.md`". The line-level grep had matched tier-2 R-0028 and left it unclassified (Charon R4-1, round 4). Hits that are not this premise: tier-1 R-0024's and P-0010's cross-lane postmaster reap, which is about the embedded engine, and the ingestion spec's and Frame's unrelated Lanes A and B.
>
> No R-NNNN identifier is added (the series maximum is R-0121 at `0e82b3e`). The `.bom.toml` sidecars are re-pinned at this amendment's exit gate, not here.

- R-0120-a (two inputs, three roles, never a skip): The shared fixture entry point SHALL obtain its server from exactly two environment variables. `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` names the **harness role**: `LOGIN CREATEDB CREATEROLE`, not a superuser, no `BYPASSRLS`; it provisions per-test databases and, per run, creates a **run-scoped owner role** and a **run-scoped serving role** (`mnemra_test_<run>_owner`, `mnemra_test_<run>_serving`, with the R-0117-c attribute set) — so the role the host-under-test connects as is never the harness role, which holds `CREATEDB` and would fail R-0117-c (Charon C7); the harness role's `CREATEROLE` is what lets the drift scenarios run `ALTER ROLE <serving> SET …` and `RESET`. `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` names a superuser on the same server and SHALL be read by **one gated test binary only** (`tests/instance_check_privileged.rs`, the R-0117 negative cases that need a superuser connection or a `BYPASSRLS` grant); a source check SHALL fail when any other test file reads it. Both variables SHALL be required: an absent or unreachable variable fails the first acquiring test with a message naming the variable and the one-line remedy from the deployment document; the fixture SHALL NOT skip, SHALL NOT fall back to a default URL, and SHALL NOT start a server. **Before any provisioning the fixture SHALL refuse a server that holds a non-test mnemra database, failing closed**: for every database in `pg_database` other than `template0`, `template1` and `postgres`, and other than `mnemra_test_*` names, the fixture connects and looks for the mnemra migrations table — a database it **cannot connect to** (no `CONNECT` privilege, `datallowconn = false`) is treated as a possible production store and refused with `cannot verify <db>` (never skipped), a database carrying the table is refused with `refusing to run tests against a server holding <db>`; the marker is the migrations table, not the name, and there is no ignore list. **`<run>` is defined as the whole gate invocation**: the value of `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID`, which the `just ci` recipe exports once as `<hosthash>_<pid>_<pid-start-epoch>` of the `just` process (alive for the whole gate, so every one of the 42 test binaries shares one run id and one cache), and which a test binary started without the variable generates for itself from its own pid and start epoch (a standalone run is its own invocation); the fixture SHALL refuse a value that does not parse to the three fields. `<hosthash>` is the first 8 hex characters of a hash over the hostname, the machine boot id and the container id when one is present (`/proc/self/cgroup` or `/proc/1/cpuset`) — so two containers that share a hostname get different hashes and one never sweeps the other's live run, whose pids it cannot see (`kill(pid, 0)` does not cross a pid namespace; stated limit). Postgres truncates identifiers to **63 bytes silently**, so every generated name SHALL be built to fit (`mnemra_test_` 12 + hash 8 + pid ≤ 7 + epoch 10 + 3 separators = 40, leaving 23 for `<binary>_<n>`, with `<binary>` truncated to fit) and **parsed from the right** (`<n>`, `<binary>`, epoch, pid, hash are fixed-width or delimiter-terminated from the right, so a truncated binary name cannot shift the fields). At fixture start the sweep SHALL remove: (a) every database and role under the fixture's **own** run prefix (a restart of the same pid-and-epoch is impossible by construction), and (b) every `mnemra_test_<hosthash>_<pid>_<epoch>_*` database and role whose `<hosthash>` equals this process's and whose `<pid>` is **dead** (`kill(pid, 0)` → `ESRCH`) or whose live process's start epoch differs from `<epoch>` — the SIGKILLed run that never reached teardown. It SHALL NOT touch another `<hosthash>`'s prefix or a live same-hash run (two lanes may share one server, and a global sweep is the cross-lane reap trap in a new form). **Cost of the fail-closed production-store scan, stated:** one `pg_database` read plus one connect attempt per non-test database, which at 42 Postgres test binaries per `just ci` would run 42 times; the fixture therefore **caches the scan result per run** in a file under the OS temporary directory named by `<run>` (the gate-level id above, so the 42 binaries of one `just ci` share it), and the scan runs once per gate invocation while each later binary reads the cache; a standalone binary run has its own id and scans once. **Reboot sweep (age threshold):** because `<hosthash>` includes the boot id — kept, since it is what separates two containers that share a hostname and show no container id (cgroup v2 `0::/`) — the pre-reboot orphans of a SIGKILLed run on a persistent machine would never match a post-reboot hash; so the sweep SHALL also remove every `mnemra_test_*` database and role whose `<epoch>` (already in the name) is older than a code constant of **24 h**, whatever its hash, on the stated assumption that no gate or local run lives that long (two lanes sharing a server cannot have a live 24-hour-old run). *Rejected: skip-when-absent — the SKIP-with-no-assertion class this repository has already been burned by (`CLAUDE.md`, the former `postgres_engine.rs` trap); a fixture-started disposable cluster from the operator's `initdb`/`pg_ctl` — it re-creates a host-driven lifecycle the engine decision rejected (activity 7091, option 2), and packaging who supplies those binaries is #4019's question; one role for harness and host — it cannot satisfy R-0117-c.* *(Binary-observable: with `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` unset, `cargo test -p mnemra-host --test schema_init` fails (not passes, not skips) with the variable's name in the first failure; `grep -rn '#\[ignore\]' libs/mnemra-host/tests/` returns no hit that mentions a database; `grep -rln MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL libs/mnemra-host/tests/` returns exactly the gated binary and the fixture; the smoke child's `instance_check` shows `R-0117-c met` while the harness role, checked by `mnemra check-db`, reports `role_overprivileged { attribute: "createdb" }`; a server seeded with a database `mnemra` holding the migrations table makes the fixture refuse before creating anything; a seeded database with `CONNECT` revoked from the harness role makes it refuse with `cannot verify`; a seeded `mnemra_test_<thishash>_<deadpid>_<epoch>_x` database is swept at the next fixture start while a seeded `mnemra_test_<otherhash>_…` one and a live same-hash run's databases survive; a generated name for a 40-character binary is exactly 63 bytes and round-trips through the right-to-left parser; with `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` exported by `just ci`, the production-store scan's connect attempts appear once per gate run, not once per binary, and two binaries in the run observe the same `<run>` in their database names; a seeded `mnemra_test_<anyhash>_<pid>_<epoch 25 h old>_x` database is swept while a seeded 23-hour-old one with a live same-hash pid survives.)* [P-TrustworthySignal; P-TestableByDesign; P-LeastAuthority — superuser authority is confined to one binary; Warden M6; Charon C7] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119 — nine corrections; (1), (2), (4), (5) and (6) revised and (7)–(9) added on 2026-10-22 in the one scoped pass maintainer ruling 2026-10-22 ordered (#4119 activity 8037), after the #4119 threat model and the PostgreSQL 16.4 measurements of activities 8047 and 8064, under the rulings of activity 8067. **(1) The superuser credential is confined by holder, takes R-0119's shape, and the leak check admits its one holder by compile-time identity.** "Both variables SHALL be required", the clause title's "two inputs", its opening "SHALL obtain its server from exactly two environment variables" and the observable "`grep -rln MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL libs/mnemra-host/tests/` returns exactly the gated binary and the fixture" are superseded. `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is required of every Postgres test binary. The superuser credential is **two** variables in the shape R-0119-a/-b give the host's own credential: `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` names a superuser role on the same server with **no password in its userinfo**, `sslmode=verify-full` and the run's `sslrootcert` (parsed in the `superuser` mode of (7); anything else is refused naming the reason), and `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD_FILE` names a regular file of mode `0600` owned by the test uid, opened `O_NOFOLLOW` and checked on the opened descriptor exactly as R-0119-b checks the host's file, its contents never printed. Both are required of exactly one execution cell, `tests/instance_check_privileged.rs`, which runs as its **own full-binary standalone cell**, and both SHALL be **absent from every other process**: that cell's recipe builds the binary with neither variable set and places both in the environment of the built test executable's own process only — never in a `ci.yml` workflow, job or `GITHUB_ENV` environment, never in the environment `just ci` runs in, and never on a cargo, rustc or build-script process — reading the two values from **the superuser location**, a directory and not an environment variable: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/mnemra/test-superuser/` (`~/.config/mnemra/test-superuser/` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is unset), beside the host's own default credential path of R-0119-b, holding a file `url` (the password-less superuser URL) and a file `password` (mode `0600`). The recipe sets `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` to the contents of `url` and `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD_FILE` to the path of `password`. The location is a constant of the recipe computed from exactly two inputs, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `HOME`; no other variable and no recipe parameter changes it. The recipe SHALL refuse, exiting non-zero, printing `GATE instance_check_privileged FAIL` and naming the variable, an `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` that is set, non-empty and not an absolute path, and, when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is unset or empty, a `HOME` that is unset or not an absolute path (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382; Bolt L1). *Rejected: ignoring a relative `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, as the XDG base-directory convention does — the recipe would then read a location other than the one its environment names, and say nothing (P-TrustworthySignal).* The directory SHALL be owned by the test uid at mode `0700`. The CI setup step (R-0120-c(1)) and the documented local setup (R-0120-d) both write it. **The gated recipe never skips** (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 8067 item 3: a local `just ci` runs the gated cell). When the location is absent, is not a `0700` directory owned by the test uid, or lacks a readable `url` or `password` file, the recipe SHALL exit non-zero, printing `GATE instance_check_privileged FAIL` and a line naming the path and the deployment-document section that creates it. It SHALL NOT exit `0` on any path on which the built binary did not run to completion and pass. **It SHALL pin the binary's exact passed count (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382; Bolt M4).** The recipe holds that count as a literal and prints `GATE instance_check_privileged PASS` only when the binary's output carries `test result: ok. <N> passed; 0 failed;` with exactly that `N`; otherwise it prints the FAIL line, naming the pinned and the reported count, and exits non-zero — so a run in which a test was ignored, filtered out or removed cannot exit `0`. The literal changes only in the same diff as a test added to or removed from the binary. `N` is the same on every platform: a platform-specific assertion (R-0120-e(1)) sits inside a test that exists on every platform, never in a test compiled on one platform only. (Anchors: P-TrustworthySignal — read what a check reported, never just its status; V-Consistency — the `verify-signing-root` recipe's `1 passed` pin.) *Superseded: "a location that is not an environment variable (illustrative, not normative: a `0700` directory whose path the recipe takes as an argument)" — an unnamed location left CI with no stated way to hand it over and every local contributor with no stated place to put it (Bolt R3-2, R3-9(b)). Rejected: a recipe argument — the `ci` recipe's one parameter is `chain` (`justfile:937` at `65e58e0`), so every caller, CI included, would have to pass a second one, and a forgotten argument is the skip; a variable naming the directory — a new variable inherited by every process under `just ci`, beside a preflight that refuses the superuser variables by name; a CI-only cell with a `NOT-RUN` line locally — rejected by ruling 8067 item 3.* (Anchors for the location: V-Consistency — R-0119-b's default credential path under `~/.config/mnemra/`; V-Simplicity — no new variable or parameter crosses an environment; P-TrustworthySignal — a missing credential fails, never skips; ruling 8067 item 3.) The gated binary reads them through an accessor in a `#[path]` module that only it includes; the accessor reads each variable at exactly one site, opens the password file under the R-0119-b checks, constructs the one superuser pool over `verify-full`, and passes the URL and the file path unchanged to a host it spawns as `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` and `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` (the R-0117-c superuser scenario). **Every test-side spawn of the production binary** SHALL start from `env_clear()` and pass an explicit variable list, so no `MNEMRA_TEST_*` variable reaches a host process (R-0120-e(1)). The set is measured, not assumed: at `65e58e0`, `grep -rn 'Command::new' libs/mnemra-host/tests/` returns 7 sites, and exactly one spawns the production binary (`libs/mnemra-host/tests/smoke_e2e.rs:196`); the gated binary adds a second. A spawn site added later is covered by this rule, and its detector is #4165 (threat model C4119-5(c); Charon C3-3): a count-pinned allowlist of `Command::new` sites under `tests/` in the R-0032 lint, with `env_clear()` required on every spawn of the production binary. That task is an acceptance criterion of the R-0120 implementation plan and fires at the R-0032 lint change. *Superseded: "by no detector until its test carries the `/proc` assertion of R-0120-e(1) — a named residual, not a guarantee" — a computable violation left without a detector (P-Defer DF1).* **Fail closed on leakage:** at acquisition the shared fixture SHALL fail the first acquiring test with `superuser_url_leaked { variable }` when either superuser variable is set in a process whose **compile-time** test-target name — `env!("CARGO_CRATE_NAME")` evaluated in the `#[path]`-included fixture, never a value read from the environment or `argv`, which a leaked export would carry along — is not `instance_check_privileged`; in that one binary the fixture SHALL instead require both variables and fail naming the missing one. `just ci`'s preflight SHALL refuse, naming the variable, when either superuser variable is set in its own environment, before any gate runs. *That Cargo sets `CARGO_CRATE_NAME` to the target name of an integration-test crate is INFERRED from Cargo's documented build environment, not measured here; the seeded admit-and-reject pair below is the measurement.* *Rejected: reader-only confinement by source check — the environment is inherited by every child (build scripts, proc-macros, the smoke child), so a source check on `tests/` confines nothing; the superuser URL with its password in one environment variable (the round-1 shape) — the scenario's spawned host needs a password file under R-0119-d anyway, so the secret would sit in the environment and on disk at once, and R-0119-a already rejects a password in the environment because every same-uid process and every crash dump can read it; a leak check that fires on any process holding the variable (the round-2 shape) — the gated binary is itself a fixture consumer, so the check fired on the one permitted holder; admitting the holder by an environment marker — a leaked export carries the marker with it.* [P-LeastAuthority — the one process that needs superuser authority is the only one whose environment holds it: the credential is not inherited through any environment, while a same-uid process can still read it (accepted risk `AR-0015`); V-Consistency — the credential shape of R-0119-a/-b and of the run-scoped role files in (2); P-CheckCoversTheClaim — identity on a hit, keyed on an attribute the leak cannot set; maintainer ruling 7867 item 1 — no superuser URL in the job or workflow environment; Warden H5, R2-H2(i); Bolt H2, N1] **(2) TLS, local included (ruling 7849 item 2).** The harness URL SHALL be a TCP URL carrying `sslmode=verify-full` and `sslrootcert=<path to the run's CA certificate>`; the fixture SHALL refuse — failing the first acquiring test, naming the reason — a socket URL, a weaker or absent `sslmode`, or a missing `sslrootcert` (the `harness` mode of (7)). Every message the fixture or the accessor prints about a connection SHALL carry only the fields (7) parses — user, host, port and database — and never URL text. *Superseded: "and SHALL redact userinfo from every message it prints" — a `?password=` or `?options=` value was still printed (Warden R3-W2(d)).* The harness password MAY travel in the URL's userinfo (it is the harness's own throwaway credential and never the host's). The **run-scoped roles** receive **random passwords per run** — 32 bytes from the OS CSPRNG, hex-encoded, generated by the fixture, the plaintext never written to any log or test output — stored in files of mode `0600` under a private per-run directory created `0700`, which every later acquirer of the run and its owner find (its location and the controls on opening it are task #4164's, threat model C4119-3(a) and (e), carried into the R-0120 implementation plan as acceptance criteria; revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382, Warden L3 — superseded: "a per-run directory `<OS temp dir>/mnemra_test_<run>/`", a location #4164 places elsewhere), and the host-under-test (`verify-smoke`'s child, R-0120-e; `startup_run_full`) is given exactly R-0119's inputs: `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` with **no** password, `sslmode=verify-full`, the same `sslrootcert`, and `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` naming the serving role's file; `mnemra init` likewise as the owner role. **The run-scoped passwords are set on the server as client-computed SCRAM-SHA-256 verifiers.** The fixture computes `SCRAM-SHA-256$<iterations>:<salt>$<StoredKey>:<ServerKey>` itself (16 random salt bytes; the iteration count a code constant, `4096`). It issues `CREATE ROLE … PASSWORD '<verifier>'` and `ALTER ROLE … PASSWORD '<verifier>'`, so no statement it sends contains a plaintext password. MEASURED on PostgreSQL 16.4 (#4119 activity 8064, I-18): the server stores such a verifier byte for byte and authenticates the plaintext against it. A failing `CREATE ROLE` logs the verifier and not the plaintext (`grep -cF` over the server log: 1 for the verifier, 0 for the plaintext). The verifier may therefore appear in a server log, and through `docker logs` in a public CI log, on a red run: accepted risk `AR-0019`. *Rejected: a plaintext `PASSWORD '<hex>'` (round 3) — with the default `log_min_error_statement`, a failing statement writes it to the server log's `STATEMENT:` line (MEASURED, #4119 activity 8047 Q3), and `docker logs` publishes that log on a red CI run; masking as the only control — the fixture generates these passwords in-process, so the runner never sees them to mask (threat model C4119-4(e), a second layer this pass does not take).* (Anchors: V-Security — no secret in SQL or a log; P-SecurityLayered.) **There is no test-only transport bypass**: no cfg, feature or environment variable lets the host accept a weaker transport in tests (R-0119-d, R-0117-f). *Rejected: a socket/peer path for tests — peer authentication maps the OS user to one role name, and the run-scoped roles are per run; a test-only `sslmode=disable` switch in the host — the switch is itself the bypass.* [R-0119-d; R-0117-f; P-SecurityLayered; ruling 7849] **(3) Names parse or they are not ours.** The generated grammar is fixed: databases `mnemra_test_<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>_<binary>_<n>`, roles `mnemra_test_<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>_owner` and `…_serving`, and the one exempt name `mnemra_test_template`. The sweep and every drop path SHALL consider only names that **fully parse** right-to-left into those fields; a `mnemra_test_*` name that does not parse (a developer's `mnemra_test_scratch`) SHALL be reported once per run as `unparsed_test_name { name }` on stderr and never dropped. "Ours" is **field equality after parsing** — never a prefix test and never `LIKE`, whose `_` is a wildcard and which cannot tell `admin_token` from `admin_token_behavior` or pid `123` from `1234`. Before any `DROP DATABASE` the fixture SHALL make a second identity check: `pg_database.datdba` resolves to a role whose name parses to the same `<run>`. The 24-hour age sweep compares the name's epoch with the fixture process's clock and assumes the server and the fixture are on synchronised clocks (local, or a shared server under NTP); the deployment document SHALL state this. *Rejected: `LIKE 'mnemra_test_<run>_%'` — matches across the delimiter; name-only identity — a developer could create a parseable name by hand.* [P-CheckCoversTheClaim; Warden M5; Bolt M1, L2] **(4) Two processes, one server.** `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE mnemra_test_template` fails with SQLSTATE `55006` while another session holds the template; the fixture SHALL serialise the clone across processes with `pg_advisory_lock(<one code-constant key>)` on the harness connection for the clone's duration and SHALL retry a `55006` at most 3 times at 500 ms before failing the test by name. *Superseded by (8): "The production-store scan cache file SHALL be created atomically (write to a temp name, rename) with mode `0600` inside the `0700` per-run directory" — under the name allowlist there is no cache.* [V-Reliability; Bolt M3] **(5) Teardown is loud, has one owner, and runs on every exit path (Bolt R3-1, R3-7, R3-12).** A failed drop or sweep SHALL print `fixture_teardown_failed { object, error }` to the process's real stderr, and a sweep error at acquisition SHALL fail the acquiring test. **The run owns its roles and its directory; a binary owns only its databases.** The **run owner** is the process that generated `<run>`: the `ci` recipe; the one helper recipe of R-0120-c(4), when a shard recipe is invoked directly — it generates and exports `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` **only when the variable is unset**, and owns the run only when it generated it; or a test binary started without the variable. The first acquirer of a run creates the run-scoped roles, their password files and the per-run directory, and every later acquirer of the same run reuses them; only the run owner removes them. Each binary's exit hook (tier-2 R-0028) drops exactly the databases that binary created (R-0120-b as amended) and nothing else, except a binary that is itself the run owner, whose exit hook then also runs steps (ii) and (iii) below for its run. **Sweep (a) does not run inside a run the process does not own.** A binary that inherited `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` leaves every object of its run to the owner, which names a leftover in step (i) before dropping it in step (ii); a process that generated its own `<run>` has nothing under it to sweep. Sweep (b) and the 24-hour age sweep are unchanged. **The `ci` recipe, and the helper when it owns the run, SHALL set an `EXIT` trap** — it fires on normal completion, on a failing gate and on `INT`/`TERM` — that runs, after the chain, in order: **(i)** the leftover check: every database that parses to the run is one a binary's exit hook should have dropped (a failed per-binary drop, or a binary killed by `SIGKILL`), and each is named; **(ii)** the end-of-run teardown: drop every database that parses to the run (`WITH (FORCE)`), then the run's two roles, then the per-run directory; **(iii)** the post-teardown check: any database or role that parses to the run, or the per-run directory still existing, is named. The trap prints the executor's report line, when the executor printed exactly one, and then exactly one `GATE leaked-objects` line, chosen by the closed outcome set under the teardown executor below. On a FAIL, the apparatus line included, the recipe exits non-zero even when every gate of the chain passed; when the chain failed, the recipe exits with the chain's status. A database with live connections is dropped `WITH (FORCE)` after the fixture's own pools are closed. **The teardown executor (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382, Bolt M3; revised again 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8452: one pass on Warden's scoped re-check M1–M3 and L-a; landing-prep pass 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8494: Warden's final re-check M1, M2, L1 and L4).** Steps (i)–(iii) of the trap SHALL be run by one dedicated executable built from this workspace: a non-default workspace binary, never the production `mnemra` binary and never a test binary. It SHALL decide which objects are the run's with the name parser of (3), and parse its connection URL with the parser of (7) in `harness` mode — the same functions the fixture calls, included or linked, never copied. It SHALL connect as the **harness role**, the role that created the run's databases and roles and holds `ADMIN` and `SET` on the two run roles (9). It SHALL take the harness URL from `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and the run id from `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` in its inherited environment, and SHALL take **no command-line argument**. **Inputs are refused before any connection.** The executor SHALL exit non-zero, before it opens any connection, when it is given any argument (which it does not echo); when `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` is absent, empty, or does not fully parse right to left under (3) into the run fields `<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>`; when `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is absent or empty; when any variable whose name begins `PG` is set (`pg_env_present { variable }`, as (7) refuses it in a test process); and when the harness URL fails the parse of (7) in `harness` mode. It SHALL NOT generate a run id and SHALL NOT call the fixture's run-id source: a run it was not told is not a run it may tear down. Its `main` is its own, so it MAY also apply R-0119-a's `PG*` scrub before any thread starts; the refusal is required either way. **The fixture's credential rules bind it.** Every message it prints about a connection, the reason it gives for a refusal or a failure included, SHALL carry only the fields (7) parses and never URL text, under (2); its driver SHALL receive an options object built field by field from the parse result of (7), never a URL string, under (7); and on its harness connection, which names the database `postgres`, it SHALL run the four checks of (6) through the fixture's decision function before its first query of step (i), exiting non-zero on the first that fails. **Identity before a drop.** Before each `DROP DATABASE` it SHALL apply the second identity check of (3) — `pg_database.datdba` resolves to a role whose name parses to the same `<run>` — through the function the fixture calls, included or linked, never copied. A database whose name parses to the run but which fails that check is not dropped, and step (iii) names it. **The per-run directory.** It SHALL find the per-run directory through the locator the fixture uses to create it, included or linked, never copied. That locator SHALL be computable from `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` and the environment the executor inherits, or SHALL read the location from a record whose own location is so computable. Which of the two applies, and the controls on opening the directory, are task #4164's ((2)). **Identity before a removal.** Before it removes the directory its locator resolves, it SHALL check that directory's identity: its basename carries the same `<run>`, compared as the run fields of (3) (`<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>`, field equality after parsing, never a prefix test), it sits under the parent the locator expects, and any location record the locator read is owner-only and was opened without following a link. A directory that fails that check is not removed, and step (iii) names it. The check's mechanism and its seeds are task #4164's, carried into the R-0120 implementation plan as acceptance criteria (Warden's final re-check M2). A directory that exists and cannot be removed is named by step (iii).
