Because the first acquirer creates the run's roles and the per-run directory together, a run whose roles existed when step (ii) began but whose directory the locator does not find is also named, as `missing`: an absent directory passes only when the run never created its roles. **One report.** On completing steps (i), (ii) and (iii), the executor SHALL print exactly one report as the last line of its stdout and exit `0`, whether or not the report names anything: `teardown_report { leftover: [<names>], remaining: [<names>], run_dir: removed | absent | missing | not_removed }`, where `leftover` lists step (i)'s names and `remaining` lists step (iii)'s databases and roles. On every path that does not complete all three steps it exits non-zero. **The trap's outcome set is closed.** The trap SHALL print `GATE leaked-objects PASS` only when the executor exited `0`, its stdout holds exactly one `teardown_report` line, that line is its last, and the report names nothing (`leftover` and `remaining` empty, `run_dir` either `removed` or `absent`). It SHALL print `GATE leaked-objects FAIL` followed by the named objects only when the first three conditions hold and the report names something. It SHALL print `GATE leaked-objects FAIL (apparatus — <reason>)` on every other outcome: a non-zero exit, death by a signal, no report, more than one report, a report that is not the last line, or an executor that is missing or not executable. `<reason>` names the outcome (`executor exited <status>`, `executor killed by signal <n>`, `executor printed <k> reports`, `teardown executor unavailable: <reason>`) and repeats no executor output. **Order.** The run owner SHALL run its preflight, then build the executor, then set the `EXIT` trap, then start the chain. That is the `ci` recipe before it invokes the chain, and the helper of R-0120-c(4), when it owns the run, before the recipe it wraps. So the preflight's refusal of the superuser and `PG*` variables covers the build's environment (cargo, rustc, build scripts and proc-macros), and the executor exists on every path on which a test binary may have acquired. When the build fails, the recipe SHALL set no trap, run no gate, print `GATE leaked-objects FAIL (apparatus — teardown executor unavailable: <reason>)` and exit non-zero. **A test binary that is itself the run owner** (a standalone run) runs steps (ii) and (iii) in its own exit hook, in process, through the fixture's own code and not through the executor. It does not run step (i), because a database its own hook failed to drop is already printed as `fixture_teardown_failed` under this item's first sentence, and it prints no `GATE` line, because a standalone binary is not a gate: what (iii) finds is printed as `fixture_teardown_failed` on its real stderr. *Rejected: `psql` in the trap — it would take the harness password on its command line, readable through `ps` by any local user, or through a `PG*` variable, the form (7) refuses under the gate, and the run's name grammar would be re-implemented in shell, the second grammar (7) already rejects; an executor built by `_gate-build` (the prerequisite of `verify-smoke`) — it runs after `verify-test` and `verify-test-hooks` in both chains, so a red test gate leaves objects behind with no executor to remove them; a test-binary executor run by the trap — inside a full-binary cell it would tear the run down mid-chain, and outside every cell tier-2 R-0033 clause (b) counts it 0; teardown in the production `mnemra` binary — the shipped binary would carry a drop-every-run-database capability its role does not need; `PASS` whenever steps (i) and (iii) name nothing, beside a list of named apparatus cases (missing, not executable, cannot connect or query) — a run in which the executor evaluated nothing also names nothing, so an exit `2` with no output printed `PASS` (Warden, scoped re-check M1); an executor that generates a run id when none is set, as a test binary does — it would check an empty run while the real run's objects remained (M1); a driver connect on the URL string in the executor — the driver applies the last of a repeated key and seeds options from `PG*`, which is how the fixture's refusals would be bypassed (M2); name-only identity in the executor — (3) already rejects it, and the executor drops `WITH (FORCE)` (M3); the build ordered only before the chain — the preflight runs before any gate, and the build is not a gate, so a leaked superuser variable could reach build scripts (L-a).* (Anchors: V-Security as R-0119-a's keystone applies it — a secret never travels on the command line; ruling 7867 item 3 — the harness URL is the test credential the environment carries; V-Consistency — one grammar, the rejection (7) records; P-TrustworthySignal — a teardown that could not run reports so, never PASS; P-LeastAuthority — the production binary gains no drop authority, and the preflight runs before the build. Revision of 2026-10-25, M1–M3 and L-a: P-SecurityLayered — a control that errors denies rather than passes through, so the trap enumerates the outcomes that pass and every other outcome fails; P-TrustworthySignal — a red that traces to the apparatus is labelled as such; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — identity on a hit (the `datdba` check), and a zero given a positive control (`run_dir: removed`; the listener's count of 1 below); V-Consistency — the fixture's rules bind the executor through the fixture's own functions.) *Superseded (round 3): "the gate carries a final recipe step **`GATE leaked-objects`** that FAILS when any database or role on the server parses to the current `<run>`, or any file remains under the run's directory, and the `just ci` `INT`/`TERM` trap runs the same end-of-run teardown". Nothing removed the roles and the directory on a passing run, so that step was red on every green run, or else every binary removed them and the next binary rebuilt them. A chain prerequisite also never runs after a red gate (`justfile:885`, `:898` at `65e58e0`), and a trap on `INT`/`TERM` alone missed the failing-gate path the retired reap covered (`justfile:949-950` at `65e58e0`). Also superseded, from the #4018 text: sweep "(a) every database and role under the fixture's **own** run prefix (a restart of the same pid-and-epoch is impossible by construction)" — under a gate-level run id one binary's start would drop the run's shared roles and a peer's leftovers alike, and a database a failed hook left would vanish before the owner's check could name it.* *Rejected: a single report after teardown — teardown drops what a failed per-binary hook left, so the report could never fire on it, which is why step (i) runs first; each binary removing the roles and directory at its exit — the next binary recreates them with new passwords; sweep (a) narrowed to databases — a database a failed hook left is still dropped silently by the next binary.* [P-TrustworthySignal — the check can fail and can pass; P-GuaranteeByMechanism; P-LeastAuthority — one owner per object; Bolt R3-1, R3-7, R3-12] **(6) The harness role is checked against an allowlist, in R-0117-c's query shape (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 8037: the harness-role check is an allowlist, matching R-0117-c).** On the harness connection, which names the database `postgres` under (7), after the parse of (7) and before the store guard of (8), the fixture SHALL run four checks in this order. The first check that fails fails the first acquiring test; a query that errors fails the test the same way, naming the error, and never passes. The decision is a pure function over the facts the queries return, so each case below can be shown over a facts record as well as on a server. **Step 1, version:** `server_version_num` SHALL be ≥ `160000`, else `harness_server_unsupported { found }`, because the PostgreSQL 16 `CREATEROLE` rules are what bound the harness (9). **Step 2, one identity:** `session_user` SHALL equal `current_user`, else `role_session_mismatch { session_user, current_user }`. A startup `role` setting — `options=-c role=…` or `PGOPTIONS` (MEASURED, #4119 activity 8047 Q4), or a per-role default from `ALTER ROLE … SET role` (INFERRED) — makes `current_user` a different role from the one that authenticated, and `RESET ROLE` returns to `session_user`. **Step 3, attributes:** of the seven `pg_roles` booleans of `session_user`, only `rolcanlogin`, `rolinherit`, `rolcreatedb` and `rolcreaterole` MAY be true. `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` or `rolreplication` true reports `harness_overprivileged { attribute }`. **Step 4, memberships:** `pg_has_role(session_user, r.oid, 'MEMBER')` SHALL be evaluated over every row of `pg_roles` except `session_user` itself. That is R-0117-c's query, keyed on `session_user` where R-0117-c uses `current_user`, because step 2 has made the two equal and `session_user` is the key a startup setting cannot move. It is checked against an allowlist: a role is admitted **only** when its name fully parses (3) to a run-scoped role, `mnemra_test_<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>_owner` or `…_serving`, of **any** run, **and** its own `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` and `rolreplication` are false. Every other membership reports `harness_overprivileged { attribute: "membership", via: <role> }`. No predefined `pg_*` role is admitted, `pg_signal_backend` and `pg_database_owner` included. MEMBER is the key because it is true for every membership whatever its options: a `WITH SET FALSE` grant and an `ADMIN`-only grant both report MEMBER true and SET false (MEASURED, 8047 Q2). **Why any run:** the harness holds `ADMIN` on every role it ever created (8047 Q1) and `SET` and `INHERIT` on each run role it self-granted under (9). On a server two lanes share, or one holding a stale run's roles that await a sweep, an allowlist keyed to the current run would refuse a correct harness. **Why the database is `postgres`:** `pg_database_owner` is reported true under MEMBER, USAGE and SET for a role that owns the database it is connected to, directly or through a membership, and false on `postgres` (MEASURED, #4119 activity 8064 I-20). `postgres` is owned by the bootstrap superuser in CI and in the documented local setup, so on that connection the membership does not exist, and a harness that owns `postgres` is refused as over-privileged. The case every acquisition must admit is the CI-created harness: `LOGIN CREATEDB CREATEROLE`, no memberships at its first acquisition, and later `ADMIN`, `SET` and `INHERIT` on run-grammar roles of this run and of others. *Superseded (round 3): "read `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` and `rolreplication` for `current_user` and for every role `current_user` can `SET ROLE` to (`pg_has_role(current_user, r.oid, 'SET')`)" and "a transitive member of `pg_read_server_files`, `pg_write_server_files` or `pg_execute_server_program`, the three predefined roles R-0117-c already treats as over-privilege for the serving role". The check was keyed on `current_user`, which a startup `role` setting moves. The `SET` key missed a `WITH SET FALSE` grant, whose `INHERIT` still conveys the role's privileges, and an `ADMIN`-only grant, through which the harness can grant itself `SET`. The three-role list was a denylist that admitted `pg_read_all_data`, `pg_write_all_data`, `pg_signal_backend` and any predefined role a later major adds, and it misattributed the three to R-0117-c, which uses an empty allowlist (Warden R3-W2(b), R3-W3, R3-W8; Charon C3-2).* *Rejected: admitting `pg_database_owner` with a stated reason — it puts a predefined role on the allowlist when fixing the connection's database makes the membership false by measurement; an allowlist keyed to the current run — it fires on a correct two-lane server; a name-only allowlist — a hand-made role with a parseable name and `BYPASSRLS` would pass; editing R-0117-c's text to call a shared check — R-0117-c is a #4018-approved clause outside this amendment, and sharing one function at implementation time edits no locked text.* [P-LeastAuthority; P-SecurityLayered — the control enumerates the permitted and fails closed on error; V-Consistency — R-0117-c's query shape, with one divergence (the key) and its reason; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — key on the authority actually reachable; maintainer ruling 8037; Warden R2-H3, R3-W3] **(7) One URL-to-options path (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 8037: every test URL goes through R-0119-a's existing closed-allowlist parser).** Every connection URL a test process handles SHALL be parsed by the parser R-0119-a specifies for the host — the same function, not a copy — called with an explicit **mode**. That covers the harness URL, the superuser URL, the URL a test hands a spawned host, and every sentinel literal of R-0120-f(ii). The driver never receives a URL string: the options object is built field by field from the parse result, as R-0119-a builds it, so the classification of a connection and the options it uses come from **one** parse result. In every mode the grammar is R-0119-a's: scheme, userinfo, one host component, an optional port, a database path, and query keys from exactly `{ host, port, sslmode, sslrootcert }`, with every other key, a multi-host list and a `hostaddr` refused, naming the key and never the value, and a query key that appears more than once refused with `config_rejected { reason: "duplicate_key", key }` (R-0119-a as amended 2026-10-25). The modes differ only in these rows. **`host`:** R-0119-a, as amended 2026-10-25, and R-0119-d exactly; used by the host itself, in tests to check a URL before it is handed to a spawned host, and for the fixture's own connections as the run-scoped owner and serving roles, which take R-0119's shape (a password-less URL plus a password file, (2)). **`superuser`:** TCP only, so a socket directory (an empty host with `host=` in the query) is refused `config_rejected { reason: "socket_url" }`; `sslmode=verify-full` and an `sslrootcert` required; a password in userinfo refused (`password_in_url`), because the password comes from `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD_FILE` under (1). **`harness`:** as `superuser`, except that a password in userinfo is **permitted** (ruling 7867 item 3, the one stated divergence: the harness's own throwaway credential), and the database path SHALL be `postgres`, else `config_rejected { reason: "harness_database" }` ((6) states why). **`sentinel`:** evaluated by the R-0032 lint and never connects. Userinfo with or without a password is admitted, since the pool never authenticates; the host is exactly the literal `127.0.0.1`; the port is exactly `1`; the database name is one or more of `[A-Za-z0-9_]`; there is **no query key at all** and no fragment. This mode is the target predicate of R-0120-f(ii) as amended: the lint calls the parser in this mode on the entry's literal. A **repeated query key** is refused in every mode, `host` included, because the refusal is R-0119-a's own grammar rule (maintainer ruling 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8289, Peter, by AskUserQuestion in the design-lane session), naming the key and never a value. sqlx applies the last of a repeated key (Warden, MEASURED round 3, `options/parse.rs:50-109`), so `?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/ca&sslmode=require` would otherwise pass a check and connect unverified. **`PG*` variables are refused in test processes, not scrubbed.** The shared fixture and the superuser accessor SHALL fail the first acquiring test with `pg_env_present { variable }` when any environment variable whose name begins `PG` is set in the test process, naming the first. `just ci`'s preflight, and the helper of R-0120-c(4), SHALL refuse the same set, naming the variable, before any gate runs. sqlx seeds its options from `PGHOST`, `PGHOSTADDR`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGSSLMODE`, `PGSSLROOTCERT`, `PGSSLCERT`, `PGSSLKEY`, `PGAPPNAME` and `PGOPTIONS`, and appends to a `PGOPTIONS`-seeded value rather than replacing it (threat model, MEASURED in `sqlx-postgres-0.9.0/src/options/mod.rs:55-97` and `:436-452`), so no field-by-field builder can clear what the environment seeded. A test binary's `main` belongs to libtest, which runs tests on spawned threads (INFERRED), so the `remove_var` scrub R-0119-a orders before any thread starts cannot be placed soundly in a test process. The host keeps its scrub (R-0119-a), and a spawned host starts from `env_clear()` under (1). That the hosted CI runner sets no `PG*` variable is INFERRED; the preflight's refusal is the measurement, and it is loud. *Rejected: checks on the URL text beside the driver's own parser (round 3) — the driver parses more than any text check inspects (a repeated key, `?host=`, `?options=`, `PG*` seeding), so each check could be bypassed through the parser (Warden R3-W2); scrubbing `PG*` in the fixture — unsound under edition 2024 once libtest has started threads; a second, test-only parser — two grammars drift, and the host's is the one under review.* [P-SecurityLayered — enumerate the permitted, and a control that errors denies; V-Simplicity — one parser, four modes; V-Consistency — the host's own grammar; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — classify the parsed options, never the text; Warden R3-W2] **(8) The production-store guard is a name allowlist (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 8067 item 2; threat model D1(b)).** Before any provisioning, on the harness connection to `postgres`, the fixture SHALL read every `datname` in `pg_database`. It SHALL refuse the server, failing the first acquiring test closed, when any database is named other than `template0`, `template1`, `postgres`, or a name that begins with the literal bytes `mnemra_test_` — a byte comparison in code, never `LIKE`, whose `_` is a wildcard. The refusal is `refusing to run tests against a server holding <db>`, naming every such database. On the same connection the fixture SHALL check the `postgres` database itself for the mnemra migrations table (R-0117-h), and refuse with the same message naming `postgres` when the table is present. A query that errors fails closed, naming the error. No database is exempted by name beyond those four entries; that includes the database `mnemra init` creates in the R-0120-g job, which never shares a server with a test binary (R-0120-g as amended). The guard is one catalog read and one table lookup per acquisition, so there is **no cache** and no connect to any other database. A `mnemra_test_*` name admitted here is not thereby "ours": ownership for any drop is still the parse-and-`datdba` rule of (3). The cost, stated: a developer's server that also holds unrelated databases is refused, which the deployment document already forbids (R-0120-d, "dedicated to tests"). *Superseded, from the #4018 text: "for every database in `pg_database` other than `template0`, `template1` and `postgres`, and other than `mnemra_test_*` names, the fixture connects and looks for the mnemra migrations table — a database it **cannot connect to** (no `CONNECT` privilege, `datallowconn = false`) is treated as a possible production store and refused with `cannot verify <db>` (never skipped), a database carrying the table is refused with `refusing to run tests against a server holding <db>`; the marker is the migrations table, not the name, and there is no ignore list"; "every one of the 42 test binaries shares one run id and one cache" (its "and one cache"); and the paragraph "**Cost of the fail-closed production-store scan, stated:** … the fixture therefore **caches the scan result per run** in a file under the OS temporary directory named by `<run>` …". Also superseded: (4)'s cache-file sentence, quoted there.* *Rejected: the marker scan, hardened (threat model D1(a)) — a server whose other databases carry no migrations table, such as another application's on a developer's shared server, passed it, so "dedicated to tests" was prose only (T4119-19); it exempted `postgres`, so a store installed there was not seen (T4119-20); its per-run cache was a file in a shared temporary directory that a forged or stale copy could turn into a skipped guard (T4119-27, -29); and it cost one connect per database per binary.* [control-integrity CI2 and P-SecurityLayered — enumerate the permitted; V-Simplicity — one query and no cache; P-TrustworthySignal; ruling 8067 item 2] **(9) What PostgreSQL 16 requires of the harness and the template, stated (MEASURED on 16.4, #4119 activity 8047 Q1 and Q5; the version change DOCUMENTED there from the 16.0 release notes).** The server SHALL be PostgreSQL 16 or later, and the fixture refuses an older one ((6) step 1). From 16.0 a `CREATEROLE` role receives only `ADMIN` on a role it creates and can no longer grant itself an arbitrary non-superuser role; below 16 the harness's `CREATEROLE` would reach `pg_execute_server_program`. In 16, `ADMIN` alone lets the harness neither create a database `OWNER` a run-scoped role nor drop one that role owns ("must be able to SET ROLE", "must be owner of database", 8047 Q1). So, after creating each run-scoped role — the owner role and the serving role — the fixture SHALL issue `GRANT <run role> TO <harness role> WITH SET TRUE`, unless a membership with `SET` already exists. An operator-set `createrole_self_grant` that includes `set` creates that membership at `CREATE ROLE`, which is the same end state. The resulting membership also carries `INHERIT`: the grant names `SET` only, and the stored row has `inherit_option` true (8047 Q1). That inheritance is what lets `DROP DATABASE … WITH (FORCE)` terminate the run's own serving-role and owner-role sessions, and (6) step 4 admits it because both names parse to the run grammar. The harness SHALL NOT be granted `pg_signal_backend`, for this or any purpose, and (6) refuses it. Each test database SHALL be created `CREATE DATABASE <name> OWNER <run owner role> TEMPLATE mnemra_test_template`, so the `datdba` identity check of (3) has a run-grammar owner to resolve. The template SHALL carry `IS_TEMPLATE true`, set by the setup step as superuser (R-0120-b as amended): a non-owner, non-superuser `CREATEDB` role is refused `permission denied to copy database` on a template whose `datistemplate` is false, and only the template's owner or a superuser can set the flag (8047 Q5). *Rejected: harness-owned test databases — `datdba` would never parse to the run, so (3) would refuse every drop and everything would leak; `GRANT pg_signal_backend TO <harness>` to make FORCE work — it lets the harness terminate any non-superuser backend on the server, the operator's and the other lane's included (threat model T4119-18).* [P-LeastAuthority — the harness reaches only the run roles it created; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the `datdba` check needs an owner it can parse; Bolt R3-6] *(Binary-observable, added; superseded rows are quoted at the end: with either superuser variable set in the environment `just ci` starts in, `just ci` exits non-zero naming it before any `GATE` line, and a shim named `cargo` placed first on `PATH`, counting its invocations, records 0 (the preflight runs before the executor's build), while the same shim records 1 or more on a run with neither variable set (the positive control); with `PGOPTIONS`, or `PGSSLMODE=disable`, set in that environment, `just ci` exits non-zero naming the variable before any `GATE` line and the same `cargo` shim records 0, and with either set on the invocation of one Postgres binary, that binary's first acquiring test fails with `pg_env_present { variable }` naming it; with either superuser variable set on the invocation of one non-gated Postgres binary, that binary's first acquiring test fails with `superuser_url_leaked { variable }`; the gated binary run by its recipe with the superuser location populated acquires and passes (the admit case), and run directly with either variable withheld fails naming the missing one; the gated recipe run with the superuser location absent, at mode `0755`, or without its `password` file exits non-zero, prints `GATE instance_check_privileged FAIL` and names the path, and exits `0` in none of these cases; in the `justfile`, the gated cell's recipe names neither superuser variable on any line that invokes `cargo`, and names them only on the one line that executes the built test binary; under the R-0032 token rule (executable-code tokens only — comments, doc attributes and string literals excluded), evaluated for this observable over every `.rs` file under `libs/mnemra-host/tests/` with the fixture module included (the lint itself skips that module), the reads of either superuser variable are in exactly the accessor module and the fixture module, and a seeded doc comment naming `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` in another test file leaves that result unchanged while a seeded `std::env::var("MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL")` in another test file changes it; the accessor refuses a superuser password file at mode `0644`, a symlink at that path, a superuser URL carrying a password in its userinfo (`password_in_url`), and a superuser URL carrying `?password=x` (`query_key_forbidden`, `key: "password"`, with `x` in no output), each naming the reason; in `harness` mode the parser refuses `…?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/ca&sslmode=require` with `duplicate_key`, `key: "sslmode"`, refuses `…&options=-c%20role%3Dx` with `key: "options"`, refuses `postgres://h:pw@/postgres?host=/tmp` with `socket_url`, and refuses a harness URL naming a database other than `postgres` with `harness_database`; it admits the CI harness URL `postgres://<harness>:<hex>@127.0.0.1:<port>/postgres?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=<CA path>`, admits in `sentinel` mode the three sentinel literals of R-0120-f(ii) present at `65e58e0`, and admits in `host` mode the smoke child's `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL`; a harness URL ending `&frobnicate=secretvalue` fails naming `frobnicate`, and `secretvalue` appears in no output; a harness URL naming the superuser fails acquisition with `harness_overprivileged { attribute: "superuser" }`; after `ALTER ROLE <harness> BYPASSRLS` and after `ALTER ROLE <harness> REPLICATION`, acquisition fails naming `bypassrls` and `replication` respectively; after each of `GRANT pg_write_all_data TO <harness> WITH SET FALSE`, `GRANT pg_signal_backend TO <harness>`, `GRANT pg_execute_server_program TO <harness>`, `GRANT <a superuser role> TO <harness>`, and a grant of a `BYPASSRLS` role `WITH ADMIN TRUE, INHERIT FALSE, SET FALSE`, it fails with `attribute: "membership"` and `via` naming the granted role (the first of these passes under the superseded `'SET'` key and fails under this one); a hand-made role named `mnemra_test_<hash>_<pid>_<epoch>_owner` holding `BYPASSRLS` and granted to the harness fails acquisition naming it; a harness URL naming a superuser role to which `ALTER ROLE <superuser> SET role = <harness>` has been applied fails with `role_session_mismatch`; the decision function given `server_version_num = 150013` reports `harness_server_unsupported`; the CI harness passes acquisition after a full `just ci` in which it created and self-granted the run roles of this run and of an earlier one; a harness URL with `sslmode=require`, or a socket URL, fails the first acquiring test naming the reason; a server holding a database `mnemra` with no tables, a database `otherapp`, or a database with `CONNECT` revoked from the harness is refused naming each, before anything is created; a server whose `postgres` database holds the mnemra migrations table is refused naming `postgres`; a fresh CI container holding only `template0`, `template1`, `postgres` and `mnemra_test_template`, plus a seeded `mnemra_test_scratch`, is admitted; on a fresh CI container the harness creates both run roles and self-grants each (for each role and the harness, `pg_auth_members.set_option` and `inherit_option` are true), creates a database `OWNER` the run owner role from the template (its `datdba` parses to the run), and drops it `WITH (FORCE)` while a serving-role session is connected to it, each step succeeding; `SELECT datistemplate FROM pg_database WHERE datname = 'mnemra_test_template'` returns `t` after the setup step; the serving role's password file is mode `0600` under a `0700` directory; every statement the fixture issues that sets a password (`CREATE ROLE … PASSWORD` or `ALTER ROLE … PASSWORD`; the drift scenarios' `ALTER ROLE … SET` statements set none) carries a `PASSWORD 'SCRAM-SHA-256$4096:` literal and no substring equal to that role's plaintext password; with a seeded failing `CREATE ROLE` for the serving role (the role pre-created), the captured server log (`docker logs <container>` in CI) gives `grep -cF -- "<plaintext>"` = 0 and `grep -cF -- "<verifier>"` = 1, with the positive control `printf 'x%sy' "<plaintext>" | grep -cF -- "<plaintext>"` = 1, and the same `grep -cF` for the plaintext over every test binary's captured output is 0; the smoke child authenticates as the serving role with the plaintext from its file (`R-0117-i met`); `psql "<harness URL with sslmode=disable>"` is refused by the server; the smoke child's `instance_check` shows `R-0117-f met`; a seeded `mnemra_test_scratch` database survives a sweep and appears once as `unparsed_test_name`; a seeded `mnemra_test_<hash>_123_<epoch>_x_1` survives a run whose pid is `1234`; two processes cloning the template concurrently both succeed; a passing `just ci` prints `GATE leaked-objects PASS` exactly once, after the chain's last `GATE` line, and afterwards no database or role on the server parses to its run and the per-run directory does not exist; a run whose chain passes but in which one binary's drop of one of its databases is made to fail (the harness's privilege on it revoked) prints `GATE leaked-objects FAIL` naming that database, and `just ci` exits non-zero; a run with a seeded failing gate prints its `GATE leaked-objects` line after the failing gate's line, exits with the failing gate's status, and leaves no object parsing to its run; `kill -INT` of `just ci` leaves no object parsing to its run; a binary started without `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` leaves, once it exits, no database or role parsing to its own run and no per-run directory; with `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` exported by `just ci`, two binaries of the run observe the same `<run>` and the second does not recreate the run's roles (their `oid`s are equal across the two); a binary killed with `SIGKILL` mid-run inside `just ci` leaves databases that the `EXIT` trap names under `GATE leaked-objects FAIL` and then drops. `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` unset; set to the empty string; set to `garbage`; `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` unset; a harness URL repeating `sslmode` (`duplicate_key`, `key: "sslmode"`); a harness URL ending `&frobnicate=zzqq-exec`, which names `frobnicate` while `zzqq-exec` appears in neither its stdout nor its stderr; with the built executor replaced by a stub that exits `0` printing exactly one `teardown_report { leftover: [], remaining: [], run_dir: bogus }` as its last line, a run whose gates all pass prints no `GATE leaked-objects PASS`, prints a line beginning `GATE leaked-objects FAIL`, and exits non-zero (`run_dir` passes only as `removed` or `absent`, an allowlist); no executable line of the `justfile` (comments excluded) invokes `psql`; a binary started without `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` prints no `GATE` line; the gated recipe prints `GATE instance_check_privileged PASS` on the unmodified binary, and prints `GATE instance_check_privileged FAIL` naming the pinned and the reported count, and exits non-zero, with one of the binary's tests marked `#[ignore]` and again with one test removed; the gated recipe run with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=relative/x` exits non-zero, prints `GATE instance_check_privileged FAIL` and names `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and run with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset and the location populated under `$HOME/.config/mnemra/test-superuser/` it runs the binary. Superseded rows: "`grep -rln MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER libs/mnemra-host/tests/` returns exactly the accessor module and the fixture module" — raw text matches comments and the R-0032 lint's own literals, so a correct tree failed it (Warden R3-W9); "`grep -r` of the run's logs for its contents returns nothing" — the run's logs are not a file, and the password was plaintext in SQL; "a binary killed with `SIGKILL` mid-run leaves objects that the next fixture start sweeps, and a run left with a seeded leftover database turns `GATE leaked-objects` red naming it" — under (5) the owner's trap names and drops them; and, from the #4018 block, "a seeded database with `CONNECT` revoked from the harness role makes it refuse with `cannot verify`" and "the production-store scan's connect attempts appear once per gate run, not once per binary" — the guard of (8) connects to no other database and keeps no cache.)*)* *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363 — the corrections R-0120-i needs. They are drafted by #4363, not ruled, and each is put to the maintainer in R-0120-i(h).* **#4363-C1, sweep drop outcomes ((5), narrowed for one class of outcome):** "a sweep error at acquisition SHALL fail the acquiring test" stands for every outcome except the two that R-0120-i(c) enumerates. A drop that returns SQLSTATE `3D000` (`DROP DATABASE`) or `42704` (`DROP ROLE`) counts as removed. A drop that returns one of R-0120-i(c)'s transient SQLSTATEs is retried before it is classified. **#4363-C2, executor step (ii) ((5), extended):** in step (ii), a drop that returns `3D000` or `42704` counts as removed only while the run's owner reads dead or reused under R-0120-i(b). While the owner reads live, the object is named in a new report field, so the report reads `teardown_report { leftover: [<names>], remaining: [<names>], vanished: [<names>], run_dir: … }`. The trap's `PASS` condition, "the report names nothing", now also requires `vanished` to be empty. A report without the `vanished` field is a malformed report, an apparatus outcome of the trap's closed set. **#4363-C3, an identity failure on the sweep path ((3)):** a database that parses to a run the sweep would remove, but fails the `datdba` check, SHALL NOT be dropped and SHALL NOT be counted removed. It SHALL be reported once per run as `identity_mismatch { name }` on stderr, as `unparsed_test_name` is. Before this note only the executor stated an outcome for a failed check. **#4363-C4, the sweep observables' seeds, read in the grammar of (3):** the seeded objects those observables name are "`mnemra_test_<thishash>_<deadpid>_<epoch>_x`", "`mnemra_test_<otherhash>_…`" and "`mnemra_test_<anyhash>_<pid>_<epoch 25 h old>_x`". Each is read as a database whose name fully parses under (3) into `…_<binary>_<n>` (for example `…_seed_1`), created `OWNER` a role seeded in the same run's grammar (`…_owner`). The harness holds `SET` on that role, self-granted as (9) does for run roles. Read literally, a seed ending `…_<epoch>_x` carries no `<binary>_<n>` pair, so the right-to-left parse does not yield that run, and a seed the harness owns fails the `datdba` check. Either way a correct implementation keeps the seed, and the row fails. **#4363-C5, superseded:** "while a seeded 23-hour-old one with a live same-hash pid survives". Sweep (b) removes a same-hash name whose pid is live when that process's start epoch differs from the name's `<epoch>`. So a 23-hour-old epoch paired with any live process that did not start 23 hours earlier is removed by (b), not by age. R-0120-i's other-hash pair observes the age threshold instead. **#4363-C6, no observable mutates a shared role ((6), (9)):** several observables run statements against `<harness>` and the superuser: `ALTER ROLE <harness> BYPASSRLS`, `ALTER ROLE <harness> REPLICATION`, each `GRANT … TO <harness>`, the grant to the harness of a hand-made `…_owner` role holding `BYPASSRLS`, and `ALTER ROLE <superuser> SET role = <harness>`. Each is read as a case of (6)'s decision function shown **over a facts record**, which (6) already permits ("each case below can be shown over a facts record as well as on a server"). R-0120-i(d) states the rule, the two on-server controls that keep the query layer covered, and the detector. As written, `<harness>` is the placeholder this clause uses for the one harness role both lanes share. Run on a server, those rows would make every concurrent run's acquisition fail closed for their duration. `GRANT <a superuser role> TO <harness>` would also put superuser authority in every process that holds the harness URL, in both lanes. **#4363-C7, liveness and the run id's binding (sweep (b), (5)):** "whose `<pid>` is **dead** (`kill(pid, 0)` → `ESRCH`) or whose live process's start epoch differs from `<epoch>`" is read with R-0120-i(b). That gives one source for every start epoch, a tolerance, a zombie read as dead, and an unreadable value read as keep. A run id not bound to its acquirer is refused at acquisition. (Anchors: P-CheckCoversTheClaim for #4363-C4 and #4363-C5 — a correction is checked by the predicate it corrects, here the parse and `datdba` predicates of (3), which #4119 added after those rows were written, and #4363-C5's replacement keys the check on the branch it claims; P-SecurityLayered for #4363-C3 — a control that cannot confirm identity denies the drop; P-LeastAuthority for #4363-C6 — superuser authority stays confined to the one gated binary of (1). #4363-C1, #4363-C2 and #4363-C7 are anchored at R-0120-i(b) and (c).) *(Binary-observable, added for #4363-C2: with the built executor replaced by a stub that exits `0` and prints exactly one `teardown_report { leftover: [], remaining: [], vanished: [x], run_dir: removed }` as its last line, a run whose gates all pass prints no `GATE leaked-objects PASS`, prints `GATE leaked-objects FAIL` naming `x`, and exits non-zero. With the stub printing `teardown_report { leftover: [], remaining: [], run_dir: removed }`, which has no `vanished` field, the same run prints `GATE leaked-objects FAIL (apparatus — …)` and exits non-zero.)*)* *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363, for maintainer ruling 9016 — #4363-C8 to #4363-C10. Drafted by #4363 and put to the maintainer in R-0120-i(h).)* **#4363-C8, one server per lane (the sweep paragraph, (6) and the executor).** *Superseded:* "(two lanes may share one server, and a global sweep is the cross-lane reap trap in a new form)" and "(two lanes sharing a server cannot have a live 24-hour-old run)". Ruling 9016 gives each lane its own server, and R-0120-i(a) enforces it with a lane claim, which every acquisition, the teardown executor and the gated cell check before anything else. The sweep still SHALL NOT touch a live same-hash run, which on a lane's own server is a concurrent run of that lane. The age assumption reads "no run of a lane lives that long". In (6), "On a server two lanes share, or one holding a stale run's roles that await a sweep" reads "On a lane's server with concurrent runs, or one holding a stale run's roles that await a sweep"; the reason it gives for admitting the run roles of any run stands. In (6)'s order, the lane claim check of R-0120-i(a) runs after step 4 and before the store guard of (8). Three rejected-alternative reasons in this clause and R-0120-b still name a second lane, and are read the same way. They stand as reasons, because each holds for concurrent runs of one lane: (6)'s "an allowlist keyed to the current run — it fires on a correct two-lane server" reads "… on a correct lane server with concurrent runs"; R-0120-b's "two serial DDL statements per test on a server shared with another lane" reads "… shared with concurrent runs of the lane"; and (9)'s "the operator's and the other lane's included (threat model T4119-18)" reads "the operator's and a concurrent run's included". The sweep that found them is named in the heading of R-0120. **#4363-C9, the store-guard rows over (8)'s decision function ((8)).** The rows "a server holding a database `mnemra` with no tables, a database `otherapp`, or a database with `CONNECT` revoked from the harness is refused naming each, before anything is created; a server whose `postgres` database holds the mnemra migrations table is refused naming `postgres`" are read as cases of (8)'s decision, shown over a facts record: the `datname` list and the result of the migrations-table lookup. (8)'s guard is one catalog read and one lookup, so it is a pure decision as (6)'s is. The admit row, a fresh server "holding only `template0`, `template1`, `postgres` and `mnemra_test_template`, plus a seeded `mnemra_test_scratch`, is admitted", stays on the server. Run on a lane's server, a seeded `otherapp` would refuse every concurrent run of the lane while it existed, and a kill would leave it behind, refusing every later run until someone removed it by hand (Warden N1, round 2). **#4363-C10, the superuser location per lane ((1)).** Each lane's server has its own superuser, so the superuser location gains a lane component: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/mnemra/test-superuser/<lane id>/` (`~/.config/mnemra/test-superuser/<lane id>/` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is unset), where the lane id comes from the one function of R-0120-i(a). "The location is a constant of the recipe computed from exactly two inputs, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `HOME`" reads "from exactly three inputs: `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `HOME` and that function's lane id"; no variable and no recipe parameter supplies the lane id. Both directory levels, `test-superuser/` and `<lane id>/`, SHALL be directories owned by the test uid at mode `0700`, and the recipe refuses a symlink at either level, naming the path. It reads the `url` file under the same `O_NOFOLLOW` and descriptor checks that R-0119-b applies to the password file. *Superseded (round 4 draft):* "refuses a lane component that is a symlink", which covered the lower level only, while the `url` file was read by path (Warden R4-5, round 4). (Anchors: maintainer ruling 9016; P-GuaranteeByMechanism for #4363-C8; V-Consistency for #4363-C9 — the shape #4363-C6 gives (6); P-LeastAuthority for #4363-C10 — one superuser credential per server, held for that server only.) *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363, for maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9046 — #4363-C11 and #4363-C12. These are drafted by #4363 under that ruling, and are a draft until the maintainer's exit approval of #4363.)* **#4363-C11, a swept run's directory is removed with it (the sweep paragraph and (5)).** Sweep (b)'s and the age sweep's removal of "every … database and role" also removes the run's per-run directory, under R-0120-i(i), in R-0120-i(i)(3)'s order, and the executor's step (ii) takes the same order. In (5), "That locator SHALL be computable from `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` and the environment the executor inherits, or SHALL read the location from a record whose own location is so computable. Which of the two applies, and the controls on opening the directory, are task #4164's ((2))" is settled: the locator reads the record on the run's owner role, under R-0120-i(i)(1), and the controls of R-0120-i(i)(2) apply to every removal. The rest stays #4164's. **#4363-C12, the age sweep skips same-host runs.** "the sweep SHALL also remove every `mnemra_test_*` database and role whose `<epoch>` (already in the name) is older than a code constant of **24 h**, whatever its hash" reads "… whose `<hash8>` differs from the sweeper's and whose `<epoch>` … is older than … 24 h". A same-hash name is decided by sweep (b) alone, and a run past 22 h refuses to acquire (R-0120-i(j)). (Anchors: ruling 9046; P-CheckCoversTheClaim and V-Consistency for #4363-C11, one locator keyed on the run being removed; V-Reliability for #4363-C12.)
- R-0120-b (per-test database from a template): Each test SHALL receive a fresh database named `mnemra_test_<run>_<binary>_<n>` created `TEMPLATE mnemra_test_template`, where the template is a database the operator or CI setup created once, as a superuser, with `CREATE EXTENSION vector` applied — so no test needs the privilege to install the extension and R-0117-b is exercised for real (the tier-2 R-0027 "per-database `CREATE EXTENSION vector`" clause becomes "inherited from the template"). The fixture SHALL drop each database at teardown and SHALL NOT drop any database whose name lacks the **current run's** prefix. The teardown assertion ("no test database remains") SHALL be scoped to the run's own prefix. The `--test-threads 1` posture of the Postgres binaries (#1852) is unchanged by this amendment. *(Binary-observable: two tests in one binary observe distinct `current_database()` values, neither can read the other's rows, and `\dx` in each lists `vector`; after the run `SELECT datname FROM pg_database WHERE datname LIKE 'mnemra_test_<run>_%'` returns no row while a seeded `mnemra_test_otherrun_x` database survives; a seeded `DROP DATABASE postgres` through the fixture's drop path is refused by the prefix check.)* [tier-2 R-0027 carried; P-LeastAuthority — tests hold no extension-install privilege; Bolt M5] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119: "at teardown" is **the binary's process exit**, not the end of each test — a test database lives for its binary's life (the tier-2 R-0027 lifetime, carried), and the exit hook drops **exactly the set of names this process created**, which it keeps in memory, so a database it did not create is never in the drop set whatever its name. The `LIKE` observables above are superseded by the parse-and-compare rule of R-0120-a(3): read as `every pg_database row whose name parses to this run and binary` / `no such row remains`. The `DROP DATABASE postgres` observable stands and gains two seeds: `mnemra_test_template` and an unparseable `mnemra_test_scratch` through the drop path are refused. *Rejected: per-test drop — two serial DDL statements per test on a server shared with another lane, for no isolation gain (the per-test database is already fresh).* **Revised 2026-10-22 (#4119; R-0120-a(9)):** the template is created once by the setup step, as a superuser, with `CREATE EXTENSION vector` applied **and `IS_TEMPLATE true`**. Without the flag, the harness, which is neither the template's owner nor a superuser, is refused `permission denied to copy database` (MEASURED, #4119 activity 8047 Q5). Each test database is created `OWNER` the run owner role, so its `datdba` parses to the run. *(Binary-observable, added: `datistemplate` is `t` for `mnemra_test_template` after setup; every test database's `datdba` parses to its own run's owner role.)* [tier-2 R-0028; Charon routed; Warden M5; Bolt R3-6])*

- R-0120-c (CI): The CI workflow SHALL provide the server as a GitHub Actions service container running the pgvector project's official PostgreSQL 16 image, **pinned by image digest** in the workflow, with a `pg_isready` wait before any step uses it, and a setup step that creates the harness role, the template database and the extension from the service's superuser and exports both URLs to the job. **The digest pin needs a firing bump mechanism because Dependabot is disabled on this repository** (`CLAUDE.md`): a scheduled weekly workflow SHALL resolve the image tag's current digest and fail when it differs from the pinned one, naming both — the failure is the trigger to review and bump. The same workflow SHALL fail when the pinned image's `server_version_num` is below the R-0117-a floor (`160015`) or its pgvector version is below the R-0117-b floor (`0.8.7`), and when the latest 16.x minor on the PostgreSQL security page or the latest pgvector release on OSV exceeds the recorded floors — so the image can never lag the floors silently and the floors can never lag the releases silently (one job, four conditions, each named in its failure). **The workflow fails closed**: when the security page or OSV cannot be fetched, or the fetched content does not parse to at least one 16.x version / one pgvector release, the job fails with `source_unavailable { source }` — an empty or unparseable result is never read as "no newer release" — and the same holds for the image inspection. Detection is proven by a seeded known-newer release (the parser is run over a fixture that lists a version above the floor, and over an empty fixture, in a unit test the job runs first). **Where a failure is delivered:** the job opens a GitHub issue labelled `floor-drift` carrying the condition and both versions (one open issue per condition — it comments on an existing one rather than duplicating), and the implementation lane files the tracker task from that issue; the tracker is not reachable from CI. **Residual, named:** this is a scheduled, non-required workflow, and GitHub disables scheduled workflows in a public repository after 60 days without repository activity, so its silence would read as "no drift". It is therefore **paired with a required gate check**: a step inside the required "CI gates" check reads the floor-drift workflow's last successful run through the GitHub API and fails when it is older than **14 days** or when the API cannot be read — a keep-alive commit was rejected because it manufactures activity to keep a detector alive and still leaves the schedule as the only observer, whereas the required check makes the detector's silence red on every pull request. With that pairing, "a mechanism, not a cadence" holds: nothing here depends on a reviewer remembering the release calendar. A CI step SHALL run one Postgres test binary with both variables **unset** and assert that it fails naming the variable (the fail-not-skip proof, P-TrustworthySignal). `just ci` SHALL preflight-refuse, naming the two variables, when either is unset, before any gate runs. The `~/.theseus` caches, the `GITHUB_TOKEN` requirement and the `verify-cold-start` gate (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:119-123`, `:191-195`, `:305-309`; `justfile:756-811`, MEASURED at `57cfeca`) are retired with the embedded engine. *(Binary-observable: `ci.yml` declares a `services:` block whose image reference contains `@sha256:` and a step invoking `pg_isready`; a scheduled workflow exists whose job fails on a seeded stale digest, on a seeded known-newer 16.x or pgvector release, and on an empty or unparseable source fixture (`source_unavailable`); the "CI gates" check carries a step that fails when that workflow's last successful run is older than 14 days or the API read fails; the env-unset step's log shows the named failure; `grep -n theseus .github/workflows/ci.yml justfile` returns nothing; `just ci` with `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` unset exits non-zero naming it before any `GATE` line.)* [P-SecurityLayered — supply chain: the image is pinned by digest and the pin has a firing bump; P-TrustworthySignal — the skip class is proven absent; V-Simplicity — one service, no download gate; Bolt M5] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119 — four corrections. **(1) The server is started by a job step, not a `services:` block, so it can carry a per-run certificate (ruling 7849 item 2).** A GitHub Actions service container starts before any step runs, so no step can generate a certificate it will present. "Provide the server as a GitHub Actions service container", "from the service's superuser and exports both URLs to the job", "with both variables **unset**", "naming the two variables, when either is unset" and the observable "`ci.yml` declares a `services:` block" are superseded. The job SHALL instead, in job steps (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 7867 item 1, each of its six conditions carried by a sentence here): generate a throwaway CA and a server certificate with `openssl` on the runner, whose **subjectAltName contains the host component of the harness URL** — an IP SAN `127.0.0.1` in CI, because the port is published on loopback only — and delete the CA private key after signing; place the server key so that inside the container it is **owned by the uid the server runs as, mode `0600`** (PostgreSQL's `ssl_key_file` rule, which also admits a root-owned key at `0640`; the image's server uid is read from the image, not hard-coded — that the pgvector image runs the server as its `postgres` user is INFERRED, not measured); start the container from the **same digest-pinned image** with its superuser named `postgres` and `POSTGRES_DB` unset or `postgres`, so that the image creates no database beyond `template0`, `template1` and `postgres` and the store guard of R-0120-a(8) admits the fresh container (that the official image creates a database named by `POSTGRES_DB`, defaulting to the superuser's name, is INFERRED; the fresh-container admit observable of R-0120-a is the measurement), and with its port **published on `127.0.0.1` only**, the certificate and key mounted, and the server configured for `ssl = on`, `password_encryption = scram-sha-256` and an authentication policy that accepts TCP connections only over `hostssl` with `scram-sha-256` and rejects every non-TLS TCP connection; wait with `pg_isready`; generate the superuser and harness passwords at random in the step and emit `::add-mask::` for each before its first use; run the setup step (harness role, template database created with `IS_TEMPLATE true`, extension; R-0120-a(9)) through the superuser; export to the job **only** the harness URL (R-0120-a(2) shape, naming the database `postgres`, R-0120-a(7)) and the CA certificate path; write the superuser URL and password file for the gated cell to the superuser location R-0120-a(1) names, which is outside every environment — **no superuser URL or superuser password appears in the `ci.yml` workflow `env:`, any job `env:`, or `GITHUB_ENV`**; and run `docker logs <container>` in a step guarded `if: failure()`. The fail-not-skip step runs one Postgres test binary with `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` **unset** and asserts it fails naming that variable. `just ci`'s preflight refuses, naming the variable, when `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset, **or** either superuser variable is set (R-0120-a(1)), **or** any variable whose name begins `PG` is set (R-0120-a(7)), before any gate runs. The digest pin, the drift workflow and its required-check pairing are unchanged. *Rejected: binding the port on all interfaces with a certificate for the container's hostname (the round-2 text) — job steps on the runner reach the container through the published loopback port, so verify-full against a hostname SAN fails, and an all-interface bind exposes a superuser-holding server to the runner's network; exporting the superuser URL to the job — every step and every child of `just ci` would hold it.* **The image reference** SHALL be `pgvector/pgvector:<tag>@sha256:<digest>` — repository, a tag (the drift job resolves the tag; its server SHALL satisfy R-0117-a's predicate — the supported major and the minor floor — which the drift job checks as stated below and which also clears the harness floor of R-0120-a(6) step 1, and the exact version is held in the workflow, never in this spec — maintainer ruling 2026-10-25 (#4249 activities 8296, 8303)), and the digest — held **once** in the workflow and used **byte-identically** by every job that runs a `PG_TEST_FLAGS` cell or `verify-smoke`: at `65e58e0` those are the `ci` job ("CI gates") and `coverage-pg` ("Coverage — PG shard"), and the R-0120-g fresh-machine job uses the same reference; `coverage-rest` SHALL start no Postgres (a Postgres consumer that drifts into it then fails loudly under R-0120-a, a second detector beside the coverage-shard spec's R-0096). *Rejected: `services:` with the certificate baked into a derived image — the private key would live in a published layer; passing certificates between jobs by artifact — two new third-party Actions (coverage-shard spec R-0097) for a secret that should not leave the runner.* [R-0119-d; P-SecurityLayered; P-LeastAuthority — the superuser credential never enters a shared environment; ruling 7867 item 1; Bolt M6; Warden M7] **The drift job reads the reference from the workflow and checks it with the host's own predicate (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382; Bolt M1, M2).** It SHALL take the tag and the digest from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with the pattern this clause's observable uses, `pgvector/pgvector:[^@ ]+@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}`, and fail `source_unavailable { source: "ci.yml" }` when the file holds no match or more than one. The drift workflow SHALL hold no image reference and no server version literal of its own. It SHALL evaluate the started image's `server_version_num` with R-0117-a's version decision — the same function and the same supported-major and minor-floor constants the host's check reads, called from a test the job runs, never a copy or a second literal — and fail naming the found version and the part of the predicate it misses, the major or the floor. This extends the first of the drift job's four conditions above from the floor alone to R-0117-a's whole predicate. So an image bump is an edit to `ci.yml` alone, and raising the floor is an edit to R-0117-a and its constants. *Rejected: the floor alone (the round-4b text) — a tag on the next major passes it, and the failure surfaces two jobs away, at the host's `pg_version_unsupported` in R-0120-g; a version literal in the drift workflow — a second copy of R-0117-a's constants, which drifts from the host's; a reference the drift workflow holds itself — a bump would need two edits, and a missed one checks an image no job runs.* (Anchors: V-Consistency — R-0117-a's own "one declared set, one check"; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the claim "the CI image serves the host" is checked with the host's predicate, on the image the jobs run.) **(2) The floor-drift read inside the required check needs a permission the workflow does not grant.** `ci.yml` sets workflow-level `permissions: contents: read` (`:34-35` at `65e58e0`), which leaves `actions: none`; the `ci` job SHALL carry a job-level `permissions:` override adding `actions: read` and nothing else, so the step that reads the floor-drift workflow's last successful run can do so. [P-LeastAuthority — the narrowest scope that lets the detector read; Bolt M6] **(3) What `verify-cold-start`'s retirement does and does not replace.** `verify-cold-start` was the one gate proving that a tampered `postgres` binary or pgvector library is refused; **it has no successor**: the digest pin covers only the CI image, and the provenance of an operator's locally installed binaries is **NOT VERIFIED BY MNEMRA** — it is R-0117-j item (3), the operator's, and the deployment document SHALL say so beside the install commands rather than implying the pin covers it. The retirement is **tied** to R-0120-g: the change that removes `verify-cold-start`, `postgres_engine_cold_start`, its exception entry in the guard lint, the `~/.theseus` cache steps, the `ci` job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` env (`ci.yml:55-56`) and `just ci`'s token refusal SHALL be the same change that adds the R-0120-g job that gates the required check (R-0120-g as revised 2026-10-22: its own job, which the gates job `needs:`; superseded wording: "inside the required check"), and that CI run SHALL show the R-0120-g steps green. [P-CheckCoversTheClaim; V-Honesty; Bolt M5] **(4) The shard jobs do not pass through `just ci`.** `verify-coverage-pg` (and any recipe a CI job calls directly) SHALL export `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` **only when it is unset**, and run the preflight itself, through the one helper recipe `just ci` uses; when the helper generated the id it is the run owner and runs the `EXIT`-trap sequence of R-0120-a(5) around the recipe, and when it found the id already set (under `just ci`) it owns nothing. *Superseded (round 3): "SHALL export `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID`" unconditionally — under `just ci` the local full chain would have carried two run ids, and the coverage phase's objects would be invisible to the outer run's leftover check (Bolt R3-12); and "so a shard job runs one production-store scan, not one per binary" — under R-0120-a(8) there is no scan to share.* [V-Consistency; P-LeastAuthority — one owner per run; Bolt L3, R3-12] *(Binary-observable, added: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` contains exactly one image reference matching `pgvector/pgvector:[^@ ]+@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}` and the `ci` and `coverage-pg` jobs and the R-0120-g job all resolve to it while `coverage-rest` references no Postgres image; `ci.yml` declares no `services:` block; from a job step `psql "host=127.0.0.1 port=<port> sslmode=disable …"` is refused and `sslmode=verify-full sslrootcert=<CA>` to `127.0.0.1` connects; `openssl x509 -noout -ext subjectAltName` on the server certificate prints `IP Address:127.0.0.1`; `docker port <container>` prints only `127.0.0.1:` bindings; `docker exec <container> stat -c '%u %a' <key path>` prints the server's uid and `600` (or `0 640`); each generated password appears in an `::add-mask::` line before any other use; as the masking's positive control, a seeded dummy value (never a real credential) masked the same way and then echoed shows as `***` in the job log; the workflow carries a step with `if: failure()` running `docker logs`; no `env:` block at workflow or job level and no `GITHUB_ENV` write names a `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER` variable or a superuser password; `just ci` with `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` set exits non-zero naming it before any `GATE` line; the `ci` job's `permissions:` block lists `actions: read` and `contents: read` and nothing else; `grep -n 'cold-start\|theseus\|GITHUB_TOKEN' justfile .github/workflows/ci.yml` returns nothing in the commit that adds the R-0120-g steps and in no earlier commit; a `coverage-pg` run's log carries exactly one `GATE leaked-objects` line and the run leaves no object parsing to it (superseded: "a `coverage-pg` run's log shows one production-store scan"); the drift job, given a seeded fixture of a server exactly at R-0117-a's minor floor on its supported major, passes; given a seeded fixture of a below-floor server on the supported major, fails naming the floor; given a seeded fixture of a server on a major R-0117-a does not support, fails naming the major; run over a seeded `ci.yml` holding no image reference, and over one holding two, it fails `source_unavailable { source: "ci.yml" }`; the drift workflow file contains no `pgvector/pgvector:` string; and a seeded change to R-0117-a's minor-floor constant alone, with no workflow edit, changes the drift job's verdict on the same image.)*)* *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363, for maintainer ruling 9016 — CI under one server per lane. Drafted by #4363 and put to the maintainer in R-0120-i(h).)* CI already runs one container per job: the `ci` job, the `coverage-pg` job and the R-0120-g job each start their own, and no two jobs share a server. One server per lane therefore changes two things here. First, a step before the setup step builds the lane-id tool and runs `just test-lane declare ci`, in an environment that holds no superuser credential. Second, the setup step of (1) takes the lane id from `just test-lane show`, and writes the lane claim of R-0120-i(a) as that clause states, with `psql` variables. Nothing else in this clause changes. *(Binary-observable, added: the job's log shows `just test-lane declare ci` before the setup step, and the claim written before any test step; no workflow line holds a lane-id literal other than that declaration's `ci`; the CI refusal row of R-0120-i(a) runs on the job's own container.)* (Anchors: maintainer ruling 9016; P-GuaranteeByMechanism.) *(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291, for the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-29 (#4291 activities 9171 and 9325): the CI images follow R-0117-a and R-0117-b as amended, which hold the supported majors, their floors and the pgvector floor as one host constant. **Superseded, retained above as the record:** "the pgvector project's official PostgreSQL 16 image" (one image, of one major); and, from the 2026-10-20 text, the digest "held **once** in the workflow and used **byte-identically** by every job that runs a `PG_TEST_FLAGS` cell or `verify-smoke`", "raising the floor is an edit to R-0117-a and its constants", and the observable "`.github/workflows/ci.yml` contains exactly one image reference matching … and the `ci` and `coverage-pg` jobs and the R-0120-g job all resolve to it". **Read as the host constant's values, not superseded:** the floors the 2026-10-19 text quotes, "below the R-0117-a floor (`160015`) or its pgvector version is below the R-0117-b floor (`0.8.7`)", read as a floor in R-0117-a's table and R-0117-b's floor; and "R-0117-a's predicate — the supported major and the minor floor —", "the same supported-major and minor-floor constants", "on its supported major", "on the supported major" and "R-0117-a's minor-floor constant" read as R-0117-a's table, a key and that key's floor.**Merge point with the 2026-10-28 note (#4363), read per key, not superseded:** "CI already runs one container per job: the `ci` job, the `coverage-pg` job and the R-0120-g job each start their own, and no two jobs share a server" means: no job has more than one live container at a time, and no two jobs share a server; the `ci` job starts one container per chain leg, one after another, each stopped before the next starts ((5)), and each fresh-machine job and `coverage-pg` starts its own. "the setup step of (1) … writes the lane claim" and "the claim written before any test step" hold per container: each leg's setup step writes the claim on that leg's container before that leg's first test step, and the job's one `just test-lane declare ci` serves every leg. "no workflow line holds a lane-id literal other than that declaration's `ci`" reads: other than `ci` in each job's lane declaration. At one key, the V0 state, each of these reads as written. **The floor-drift workflow and the freshness heartbeat: deferred to #4444 (maintainer ruling 2026-10-29, #4291 activity 9325, the option labelled "Split: approve policy now, drift later", which he selected).** This amendment lands the floor policy and the host constants only. The redesign of this clause's drift and freshness text is a follow-up amendment, task #4444, which gates plan task T16 of #4161 and nothing else. The deferral covers: the weekly drift workflow of the 2026-10-19 text, its conditions read per key, and its read of the references from `ci.yml` (the 2026-10-20 text); its release-based conditions, "when the latest 16.x minor on the PostgreSQL security page or the latest pgvector release on OSV exceeds the recorded floors", which R-0117-a (3) and R-0117-b (3) as amended replace with policies set from security advisories, leaving the detector of those policies to #4444; the end-of-life tiers and the new-upstream-major report of R-0117-a (7) and (8) as amended; how a red drift run is cleared, waivers included; the freshness heartbeat step inside "CI gates" (the 2026-10-19 text) and the `actions: read` override of the 2026-10-20 correction (2); the drift workflow's issue delivery and its permissions; and finding SG-8 of the #4161 plan review, both its halves: the 2026-10-19 premise "Dependabot is disabled on this repository", which is stale (`.github/dependabot.yml` exists, MEASURED at `0076b25`), and the drift workflow's `issues: write`. **Until #4444 lands, none of these is added to `.github/workflows/`**, because a freshness step that reads a workflow nobody has built would turn "CI gates" red. **What goes unchecked meanwhile:** a floor that a newly published advisory overtakes stays invisible until #4444 lands, because the review of R-0117-a (3) and R-0117-b (3) fires only on a change that sets a floor; and the PostgreSQL image digest pin has no automatic bump, because its weekly digest-moved check is deferred to #4444 with the drift workflow and Dependabot's version updates cover only the toolchain pin, with no entry for the image (INHERITED from the repository's `CLAUDE.md`). **Still binding on #4444:** the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-29 in #4291 activity 9171 (item 1, end of life: the drift job warns about 180 days and fails about 90 days before the upstream end-of-life date, read with a fail-closed source check, and a failing key is cleared by removing it or by a short expiring waiver) and activity 9257 (no waiver renewal extends past the upstream end-of-life date; review of workflow edits is made structural on #4425; and the drift workflow's residual, that a pull request can edit the workflow that judges it, enters the overview's accepted-risk register while #4425 is open, with its identifier measured when #4444 adds the entry). **Tripwire:** #4444 is a prerequisite of T16, recorded on #4444, and the implementation lane SHALL add #4444 to T16's Held line in the plan; until that edit lands, the hold is named but not wired. [P-Defer DF1 — the deferral names what holds the work it defers; maintainer ruling 2026-10-29 (#4291 activity 9325)]Numbered on from the 2026-10-20 corrections (1) to (4):* **(5) One reference per supported major.** `ci.yml` SHALL hold one image reference of the form in (1) per key of R-0117-a's table, each held once, no two serving one major. The required "CI gates" check SHALL pass only when the whole verify chain has run once per reference, each run against a container started from that reference, and an R-0120-g fresh-machine job has run once per reference. The runs iterate over the references `ci.yml` holds, never over a list of majors written in a workflow, and the reconciliation of (7) compares what ran with the table. The whole chain runs per reference, not a list of the gates that reach Postgres, because such a list is a second enumeration a new Postgres-reaching gate could miss silently (P-TrustworthySignal — run everything, so an omission cannot narrow coverage). **The chain legs** are sequential legs of the `ci` job, sharing the runner's build. Each leg is one run under R-0120-a(5), with its own run id, run owner, end-of-run teardown and `GATE leaked-objects` line, against its own container started under (1) from its reference. The job's one `ci` lane declaration serves every leg; each leg's setup step writes the lane claim of R-0120-i(a) on its own container; and each leg stops its container after its teardown and its report, before the next leg's container starts, so the lane has one live server at a time. **The fresh-machine runs** are one job per reference, written out in `ci.yml` rather than generated by a matrix, each on its own runner (R-0120-g as amended 2026-10-29). **Cost, named.** Each key added costs the required check one more sequential leg and one more fresh-machine job, and compounds its exposure to a flaky leg: N legs that each pass with probability p pass together with probability p^N. Paying it is the quality-weighted choice (V-Quality), and the ceiling of two (R-0117-a (5)) bounds it. The change that adds a second key SHALL record, beside R-0117-a's key-count pin, the existing leg's results over its last 20 runs on `main` and the required check's wall-clock time over them, both read from the GitHub API, and a failure among those runs is triaged under P-TrustworthySignal TS1 before the key lands. The pin fires mechanically; whether the record is present is a review check. **Coverage on the highest major only (ruling 2026-10-29, item 4, "Highest major only").** `coverage-pg` runs once, from the reference whose server reports the highest key, and fails `coverage_major_not_highest { found, highest }` otherwise, reading the keys only from the step of (7). Every key still gets its chain leg and its fresh-machine job. Premise, INFERRED, not measured: the host's line coverage does not vary with the server's major, because the host runs the same statements on every supported major; a host path only another key's server reaches is tested in that key's leg but is not measured by coverage. The coverage number has no consuming gate (coverage-shard spec R-0095). R-0117-a (5)'s pins name this premise, so it is re-read whenever the keys change. *Rejected: running only `verify-smoke` and the R-0120-g job on majors beyond the first — a major whose `PG_TEST_FLAGS` cells never ran is a major nobody has tested, in the sense the 2026-10-25 ruling refuses; a matrix of legs — legs that write one output name overwrite one another (INFERRED, not measured), so per-leg reports could reach the reconciliation only through a new artifact Action (coverage-shard spec R-0097); a run matrix keyed on the tag text — the tag is a label, not the server's report (P-CheckCoversTheClaim, key parity); `coverage-pg` once per key — its build and run paid per key for a number nothing reads (the option not selected under ruling item 4); no ceiling on keys — growth bounded only by each addition's record (the option not selected under ruling item 3).* **(6) What a change edits.** Adding a major: R-0117-a's table and `ci.yml`. Raising a floor: the table, plus that major's image reference when its image is now below the floor, which (5) forces because that leg's host check then fails R-0117-a. Raising the pgvector floor: the constant, plus each reference whose image carries an older pgvector, which (5) forces because that leg's host check then fails R-0117-b. Removing a major: the table and `ci.yml`. Bumping an image: `ci.yml` alone. None is an edit to this spec. **(7) How the keys and the reports reach the reconciliation.** **The logic** of the keys read and of the reconciliation SHALL be Rust functions in the `mnemra-host` library, in the module that holds R-0117-a's constant, each a function of its parsed inputs. A workflow step only invokes them, through one entry point the implementation names, and passes their exit status through; no condition is decided in workflow shell (finding F5). [P-TestableByDesign — each is verified without booting the world] **The keys:** a step SHALL write R-0117-a's keys, each with its floor, to the job's output through that entry point, and fail `table_keys_unavailable` when that output is absent, does not parse or lists no key. The reconciliation and `coverage-pg` read the keys only from this step. **The reports:** each chain leg and each fresh-machine job SHALL read `SHOW server_version_num` from its own container after its last step against that container, and record `{ run, reference, server_version_num }`, where `run` is `chain` or `fresh-machine`. A chain leg's report reaches the reconciliation as a file on the `ci` job's own runner, and a fresh-machine job's as that job's `outputs:` value, read through `needs.<job id>.outputs`. No matrix job carries a report ((5)). Both channels are native workflow features, so no Action is added (coverage-shard spec R-0097). **The reconciliation:** a step of the `ci` job, after every leg, SHALL fail, naming the major and the run kind, when a key has no `chain` report or no `fresh-machine` report whose server major equals it (`run_report_missing { major, run }`), when a report's server major is not a key (`run_major_not_supported`), when two reports of one kind name the same major, and when there is no report at all. It reads the reports' `server_version_num`, never a tag. A job the `ci` job `needs:` is admitted only by R-0120-g's first-step test, `needs.<job id>.result` is `success`, so a job that was skipped, cancelled or never generated fails it. [P-TrustworthySignal TS2 — the per-key runs ship their reconciliation; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — keyed on each server's own report; P-SecurityLayered — the success test admits one value; V-Simplicity — native channels, no new Action] **(8) The mechanism tests are pinned by name (finding F4).** The unit tests this amendment adds (R-0117-a (5)'s ceiling, count-pin and set-pin tests, the decision tests of R-0117-a and R-0117-b, R-0117-b's interface test, the keys and reconciliation fixture tests of (7), and the version-statement test of the deployment pre-condition as amended) run under `cargo test -p mnemra-host --lib` in `verify-test` (`justfile:237`; `verify-test` is listed by both `_ci-verify-chain` and `_ci-verify-chain-nocoverage`, `justfile:1026` and `:1039`; MEASURED at `0076b25`). `verify-test` SHALL hold their names in one list and fail, naming each, when a listed name is absent from the run's output as passed, so a test deleted, renamed or filtered away turns the gate red. The key is a name, not a count, because the claim is that these tests ran (P-CheckCoversTheClaim, key parity). Each named failure of (7), and of R-0117-a (5), has at least one listed fixture test that produces it. *(Binary-observable, added: `ci.yml` holds one reference per key, each held once; on a seeded two-key table with two references, the `ci` job runs two chain legs, each with its own `GATE leaked-objects` line, its lane claim written on its own container before its first test step, and its container stopped before the next leg's starts, two fresh-machine jobs run on their own runners, and the reconciliation reads two reports of each kind and passes; with either fresh-machine job removed it fails `run_report_missing` naming that major and `fresh-machine`; a seeded chain report whose major is not a key fails `run_major_not_supported`; two seeded chain reports naming one major fail; a seeded run with no report at all fails; a seeded keys output listing no key fails `table_keys_unavailable`; `ci.yml` declares no `strategy:` matrix on the `ci` job or on any fresh-machine job; `coverage-pg` started from a reference whose server major is not the highest key fails `coverage_major_not_highest`. Removing one listed test from the source turns `verify-test` red naming it. The change that raises R-0117-a's key-count pin above one carries, beside the pin, the existing leg's results over its last 20 runs on `main` and the required check's wall-clock time over them. Until #4444 lands, `.github/workflows/` holds no floor-drift workflow, and `ci.yml` holds no freshness step and no `actions: read` permission. At V0, `ci.yml` holds exactly one reference, and the `ci` job, `coverage-pg` and the R-0120-g job resolve to it, as the 2026-10-20 observable states.)* [maintainer rulings 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-29 (#4291); P-TrustworthySignal TS2; P-CheckCoversTheClaim; P-Defer DF1; V-Quality])*

- R-0120-d (local): The deployment document SHALL state two supported ways to provide the server for a local run — PostgreSQL 16 plus pgvector from the operating system's package manager, or the same container image CI uses — with the exact commands that create the harness role, template and extension, and the two `export` lines.
