No `just` recipe installs or starts Postgres (installation is #4019's scope). *(Binary-observable: following either documented path verbatim, `cargo test -p mnemra-host --test schema_init` passes on a fresh machine.)* [V-Consistency — local and CI share one mechanism; #4019 boundary] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119: **the local server is a dedicated test instance**, stated plainly and enforced: the store guard of R-0120-a(8) refuses any server holding a database other than `template0`, `template1`, `postgres` and `mnemra_test_*` names, so a developer's shared instance is refused, and the document SHALL say "dedicated to tests" at the top of both paths, and SHALL warn that a database created for convenience (a `createdb "$USER"` after a package install, for example) makes the guard refuse the server. *Superseded (revised 2026-10-22): "the production-store scan of R-0120-a refuses any server holding a database it cannot connect to or that carries the migrations table".* The server SHALL be PostgreSQL 16 or later (R-0120-a(9)). **Local TLS equivalent (ruling 7849 item 2):** both documented paths end with the same two steps CI runs — generate a throwaway CA and server certificate with `openssl` (its subjectAltName containing the host of the documented harness URL, the server key owned by the server's uid at `0600`, a container's port published on `127.0.0.1` only) (the commands held in the deployment document and executed verbatim by the CI job, the R-0120-g pattern; a check SHALL assert the document's command block equals the one CI runs), and start the server with `ssl = on`, `scram-sha-256` under `hostssl` only, non-TLS TCP rejected — so a local run connects exactly as production does under R-0119-d, and the document states the synchronised-clock assumption of R-0120-a(3). "The two `export` lines" become **one**, of `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, whose database is `postgres` (R-0120-a(7)). The documented block also writes the superuser URL and its `0600` password file to the superuser location R-0120-a(1) names, never exported, so a local `just ci` runs the gated cell with no further step (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 8067 item 3: required locally, with setup cheap enough that skipping is not the easy path); and it creates the template with `IS_TEMPLATE true` (R-0120-a(9)). *Superseded (revised 2026-10-22): "are written where the gated cell's recipe reads them", which named no place.* *Rejected: a local-only peer/socket path — it would make the local fixture a second mechanism and could not serve per-run role names.* *(Binary-observable, added: following either path verbatim on a fresh machine, `cargo test -p mnemra-host --test schema_init` passes and `psql "<harness URL with sslmode=disable>"` is refused by the local server; the document contains the phrase "dedicated to tests" in both path sections; following either path verbatim, then running `just ci`, the gated cell's recipe prints `GATE instance_check_privileged PASS`.)* [V-Consistency; R-0119-d; Bolt M3, R3-9(b); ruling 8067 item 3])* *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363, for maintainer ruling 9016 — one local server per lane. Drafted by #4363 and put to the maintainer in R-0120-i(h).)* The deployment document SHALL state, at the top of both paths and beside "dedicated to tests", that **each lane needs its own server**. Each lane follows one path for its own server:
  - a package-manager instance with its own data directory and its own port, or its own container with its own port published on `127.0.0.1`;
  - its own throwaway CA and server certificate;
  - its own harness role, and a template carrying the lane claim of R-0120-i(a), written from `just test-lane show` with `psql` variables, which refuses to overwrite another lane's claim unless the operator sets the `reclaim` variable by hand;
 - a declaration in each of its worktrees, task worktrees included, written with `just test-lane declare <lane id>`;
 - a port other than PostgreSQL's default `5432` and the host's `/health` default `8877`, named in the document for each lane;
  - its own superuser files, under that lane's superuser location (R-0120-a(1) as amended by #4363-C10);
  - its own harness URL, exported in that lane's environment only.

  The document SHALL state the cost: one more local PostgreSQL instance, port and certificate for every extra lane. The verbatim command block that CI executes (above) gains the claim line. *(Binary-observable, added: the document contains the phrase "its own server" in both path sections; following a path for one lane, a Postgres test binary run in another lane's worktree against that lane's harness URL fails with `server_lane_mismatch` and creates nothing.)* (Anchors: maintainer ruling 9016; V-Consistency — local and CI run the same setup block.) *(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: "PostgreSQL 16 plus pgvector from the operating system's package manager" reads: a PostgreSQL server on a major in R-0117-a's table, at or above that major's floor, with pgvector at or above R-0117-b's floor, as the deployment document's version statement gives them (deployment pre-condition as amended 2026-10-29). "The same container image CI uses" reads: an image CI uses, of which there is one per supported major (R-0120-c (5) as amended 2026-10-29).)*

- R-0120-e (the smoke gate): `verify-smoke` SHALL spawn the production binary with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` pointing at a fixture-provisioned per-run database on the same server, connecting as the **run-scoped serving role** (R-0120-a), after `mnemra init` ran as the run-scoped owner role, so the restart acceptance (R-0121-c) and the readiness scenarios run against the real binary with no engine construction in test code. The `scripts/ci-reap.sh` postmaster reap has no object once no test spawns a postmaster; its successor is the **orphan sweeper** of R-0120-a (the run's own prefix plus same-`<hosthash>` runs whose pid is dead), and retiring or narrowing the script is the implementation lane's call, listed in P-0010 §Amendment 2026-10-19. *(Binary-observable: `grep -n 'EmbeddedEngine\|postgresql_embedded' libs/mnemra-host/tests/smoke_e2e.rs` returns nothing; the smoke child's `instance_check` record shows every hard clause `met` including `R-0117-c`.)* [tier-2 R-0033 partition carried; P-CheckCoversTheClaim] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119 — three corrections, and a fourth added 2026-10-25. **(1)** The smoke child is spawned with R-0119's inputs exactly as R-0120-a(2) states (no password in the URL, `verify-full`, the run's CA, the serving role's `0600` password file), from `env_clear()` with an explicit variable list (R-0120-a(1)), so its environment contains no `MNEMRA_TEST_*` variable — `smoke_e2e` itself necessarily holds `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, because it provisions the child's database through the fixture, and that is the case this rule admits. **(2)** "retiring or narrowing the script is the implementation lane's call" is superseded: **`scripts/ci-reap.sh` SHALL be retired** — with no test spawning a postmaster, the only process it can ever kill is the operator's or the other lane's server (the cross-lane reap trap `CLAUDE.md` records, with no own-spawned set left to exclude). The `ci` recipe stops sourcing it; its `EXIT` trap runs the leftover check, the end-of-run teardown and `GATE leaked-objects` of R-0120-a(5) instead (superseded, revised 2026-10-22: "its `INT`/`TERM` trap runs the end-of-run teardown", which missed the failing-gate path); `tests/ci_reap_baseline.rs` goes with it (`NONPG_TEST_FLAGS` and its count pin, and `EXPECTED_TEST_FILE_COUNT`, adjusted in the same commit). The successor is `GATE leaked-objects` (R-0120-a(5)), which fires on every gate run. **(3)** `scripts/flake-runner.sh`'s reap is the tier-1 spec's R-0024, amended the same way there. **(4) The required check proves it ran the Linux assertion (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382; Charon F1, accepted risk `AR-0017`; revised again 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8452: Warden's scoped re-check L-b and Charon's re-check; landing-prep pass 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8494: Warden's final re-check L4).** On Linux, each time its `/proc` assertion of (1) passes, each of `smoke_e2e` and the gated binary SHALL print one marker line, `proc_environ_asserted { binary: <name> }`, where `<name>` is its compile-time test-target name (`env!("CARGO_CRATE_NAME")`, as R-0120-a(1) keys the leak check), to the process's real stderr, as `fixture_teardown_failed` is printed, because libtest captures a passing test's output. The required "CI gates" job SHALL fail, naming each binary whose marker is missing, unless the output of its chain step holds at least one marker for `smoke_e2e` and at least one for `instance_check_privileged`. Capturing that output SHALL NOT change the chain step's exit status: the step fails whenever the chain fails, so no pipe or other capture may mask the chain's status. The requirement is a step of that job and reads no environment variable, so it holds whatever `CI` is set to and on whatever runner the job names: a build in which the `/proc` assertion was not compiled cannot pass the required check. On every platform other than Linux, the two binaries SHALL also fail, naming the platform and R-0120-e(1), when the environment variable `CI` is `true`. This is an earlier, cheaper layer that rests on GitHub Actions setting `CI=true` in every job; that premise is INFERRED from GitHub's documented default variables, nothing in this repository measures it, and the job-level requirement does not depend on it. The check sits inside a test that exists on every platform, so it changes no test count between platforms (R-0120-a(1) pins the gated binary's count). The residual, stated: the requirement holds while its step exists in `ci.yml`, which nothing lints. A change that deletes the step deletes the check, and `AR-0017`'s parked cadence (a re-read at each change to `ci.yml`) is what covers it. *Rejected: pinning the gates job's `runs-on` value in `ci.yml` — it keys the check on a workflow string rather than on whether the assertion was compiled into the binary that ran; the `CI=true` failure as the only detector (round 4) — it held only while `CI` was exactly `true`, so `env: CI: false` beside a `runs-on` change, or a CI system that sets `CI=1`, passed it, and its premise was called measured by a seeded run that set `CI` by hand (Warden, scoped re-check L-b; Charon).* (Anchors: P-Defer DF1 — the platform half of `AR-0017` gets a detector, and the residual is parked with its cadence named; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — key the check on the attribute the claim is about, which here is that the assertion ran; P-GuaranteeByMechanism; V-Honesty — a premise nothing measures is labelled INFERRED.) *(Binary-observable, added: on Linux, where the required CI check runs, `smoke_e2e` and the gated binary each read `/proc/<child pid>/environ` after spawning the host and assert it holds no key beginning `MNEMRA_TEST_`, and a seeded spawn without `env_clear()` fails that assertion — on other platforms the assertion is not compiled and the platform limit is stated in the test, so the local run proves less than CI; on a non-Linux platform each of the two binaries run with `CI=true` fails naming the platform, and run with `CI` unset passes (the local admit case), while on Linux with `CI=true` each runs the `/proc` assertion and passes; on Linux each of the two binaries prints `proc_environ_asserted { binary: <name> }` on its real stderr at least once, and on any other platform it prints none; the required "CI gates" job's log on the pull request that lands R-0120 shows both marker lines and the marker step passing (the observable taken from the required check); a seeded change that removes the marker print from `smoke_e2e`, and one that removes it from the gated binary, each make the "CI gates" job fail naming that binary; a seeded `env: CI: false` on the "CI gates" job leaves it passing on its Linux runner with both markers present (the marker requirement does not read `CI`); a seeded failing gate in that chain step, with both markers present in its output, makes the step and the "CI gates" job fail; the child's `instance_check` shows `R-0117-f met`; `grep -rn 'ci-reap' justfile scripts .github` returns nothing and `scripts/ci-reap.sh` does not exist; `just ci` interrupted with `SIGINT` mid-chain leaves no object parsing to its run on the server.)* [P-LeastAuthority — kill authority with no object is removed, not narrowed; P-GuaranteeByMechanism — the retirement is a requirement with a grep, not a hand-off; Bolt M4; Warden M6])* *(Amended 2026-10-28, #4363 — the per-worktree temp root retires with `scripts/ci-reap.sh`. Drafted by #4363 and put to the maintainer in R-0120-i(h).)* #4361, interim until #4161, makes the `ci` recipe and `scripts/flake-runner.sh` call `ci_reap_isolate_tmpdir`. That function is defined in `scripts/ci-reap.sh` and exports `TMPDIR` as a per-worktree directory before the baseline is captured (MEASURED at `74f49dd`, the pre-landing commit on the implementation lane's branch, cited as provenance only: a rebase-merge gives the landed commit another id). Its one object is the reap's data-directory condition: it keeps the other worktree's postmaster out of the reap. After #4161 there is no reap, so it has no object. It SHALL retire in the same change that retires `scripts/ci-reap.sh`, and no recipe or script SHALL set a per-worktree `TMPDIR` in its place. **Effect on the per-run directory locator of R-0120-a(5).** Within a run there is none: the run owner, its binaries and the executor all inherit one `TMPDIR` from the recipe. Across runs, a per-worktree `TMPDIR` would give two worktrees' runs different locator results. Under the maintainer's O1 ruling (#4363 activity 9046), a swept run's directory is located by a record on the server, not by the sweeper's environment (R-0120-i(i)(1)), so a per-worktree `TMPDIR` no longer affects that removal. *Superseded (round 6 draft):* "That is one reason the cross-run directory removal of R-0120-i(h) O1(a) is hard to build (INFERRED)". #4164, which owns the location, names `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` in its task text (INHERITED), and states no fallback for a platform without it. The existing observable `grep -rn 'ci-reap' justfile scripts .github` does not match this function, whose name uses underscores, so it would pass with the call left in place. *(Binary-observable, added: after the change that retires `scripts/ci-reap.sh`, `grep -rniE 'ci_reap_|mnemra-ci-' justfile scripts .github` returns nothing. The positive control is a fixture the check builds: the same command over a temporary directory holding a file with the line `ci_reap_isolate_tmpdir` returns that line.)* (Anchors: V-Simplicity — a mechanism whose object is gone is removed, as tier-1 R-0024's 2026-10-20 amendment rejected a temp-root reap kept for an engine that no longer exists; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the retirement check is keyed on the names the mechanism actually uses.)

- R-0120-f (the guard class survives, the symbols change): The silent-failure class the tier-2 R-0032 lint guards — a test reaching a database by a path the fixture does not control — SHALL keep a mechanism: a CI-gated source check that fails when a test file outside the fixture module opens a connection directly (the driver's pool/connection constructors) or reads `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` / `MNEMRA_TEST_*` itself (the superuser URL's one gated binary excepted by name), shown failing on a seeded violation before it lands. The precise symbol list is that spec's amendment to state; this clause fixes that the class keeps a loud-failing guard. *(Binary-observable: a seeded `PgPoolOptions::new().connect(…)` in a non-fixture test file turns the gate red, naming the file.)* [P-GuaranteeByMechanism — a silent-failure class keeps its detector across the refactor] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119 — the exception set and the scan scope, stated here so the tier-2 spec's R-0032 does not widen this clause silently (Charon C-3). **Exceptions, exhaustive:** (i) `startup_run_full`, for the production pool-source function **only** (class (b) of the tier-2 list), with its suppressed-site count pinned in the entry; (ii) a function-scoped **sentinel** list for pools that must never reach a server — each entry names one file, one function and one URL literal, and suppresses exactly one **site**: one expression, the builder chain that ends in the `connect_lazy` call (for example `PgPoolOptions::new().max_lifetime(None)….connect_lazy("…")`), every class-(a) token inside that expression included, so a second constructor anywhere else in the function is a second site and fails the check. The check admits the site **only** when all of the following hold, each computed from the source tokens: the site's constructor is `connect_lazy` (on `PgPoolOptions` or on `PgPool` / `Pool`), the one constructor whose call opens no connection; its argument is a string literal, equal byte for byte to the entry's literal; and that literal is admitted by the R-0120-a(7) parser in its `sentinel` mode, which is the one evaluator (revised 2026-10-22 under ruling 8037: every test URL goes through R-0119-a's parser; the round-3 regular expression is kept here only as the readable form of the same shape, `^postgres(ql)?://([^@/?#,]+@)?127\.0\.0\.1:1/[A-Za-z0-9_]+$`; the chain-spine half of the site rule is #4165) — scheme `postgres` or `postgresql`, optional userinfo (a password in it is admitted, since this pool never authenticates anywhere), host exactly the literal `127.0.0.1`, port exactly `1`, a database name, and nothing after it: no query, no fragment, no second host. The check is static: it does not observe whether the pool later attempts a connection, and one entry's does (`libs/mnemra-host/tests/coordination_failclosed.rs:292-325` issues `begin()` and is refused). What the predicate guarantees is that every attempt such a pool can make targets loopback port 1; that nothing listens there on the CI runner (a privileged port) is INFERRED, not measured. The three entries at `65e58e0` all satisfy it (two carry `user:password@` userinfo); `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:15432/x`, `…:1/x?port=1`, `…:1/x?host=/tmp`, `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:1,db.example:5432/x`, `postgres://u@localhost:5432/realdb`, a `const` argument and a `connect` call on a matching literal are each rejected; (iii) the superuser accessor module of R-0120-a(1) — for reads of `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` and `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD_FILE` (one site each) and for the one constructor that builds the superuser pool, and for nothing else; the module is `#[path]`-included by `tests/instance_check_privileged.rs` and by no other file. *Rejected (round-2 shape): a sentinel admitted by a byte-exact literal with no query component, and nothing more — the requirement that the target is loopback port 1 fell out, so an entry recording `127.0.0.1:15432` or `localhost:5432/realdb` was admitted as long as the site matched it.* Nothing else: the former `schema_init` whole-binary and `coordination_schema` function entries, which existed to boot a fresh *engine* for `init()` idempotency, retire with the engine — `schema::init::init()` must take a connection under R-0119 anyway (the #2069 `init_on()` refactor is therefore a consequence of the engine move, not a hidden prerequisite, and #2069 is absorbed here), and both binaries obtain a fresh **database** from the fixture and run `init` twice on it. **The retirement holds three conditions (maintainer ruling 2026-10-22, #4119 activity 7867 item 2):** it lands in the same change as, or after, the change in which `schema::init::init()` takes a connection rather than an engine (`libs/mnemra-host/schema/init.rs:854` takes `&EmbeddedEngine` at `65e58e0`); the fixture offers a named function that returns a fresh database cloned from the template **with no schema applied**, and an owner-role connection to it, so the first-run path of `init` stays tested; and `init` is shown to issue no cluster-level DDL — it runs as the run-scoped owner role, which holds none of superuser, `CREATEDB`, `CREATEROLE` or `REPLICATION`, so a `CREATE ROLE`, `CREATE DATABASE`, tablespace or `ALTER SYSTEM` statement in it fails the test, and the test also compares the `pg_roles`, `pg_database` and `pg_tablespace` row sets before the first `init` and after the second. **Scan scope:** every `.rs` under `libs/mnemra-host/tests/` outside the fixture module, **and** every `#[cfg(test)]` item in every other `.rs` under `libs/mnemra-host/` — the lib test at `libs/mnemra-host/coordination/write_path.rs:1181` (`65e58e0`) boots the engine from `cargo test --lib`, which the rest coverage shard and `verify-test --lib` run with no server; that test SHALL move to `libs/mnemra-host/tests/` as a `PG_TEST_FLAGS` member so `--lib` has no database consumer. Production (non-`cfg(test)`) code is out of scope: it constructs pools legitimately. **Two standing detectors, so the list cannot rot:** the check holds the `sqlx` version it was evaluated against as its own constant and a standing test compares it with `Cargo.lock` (independent of the R-0120-h list, which empties); and a standing self-test resolves every production pool-source name on the list to a function definition under `libs/mnemra-host/` and fails when one is absent — so #4120's rename cannot leave the list naming nothing. The embedded-engine crates SHALL be kept out by mechanism, not by the absence of a symbol: `deny.toml` `[bans].deny` gains `postgresql_embedded`, `postgresql_archive`, `postgresql_commands` and `postgresql_extensions` (the four in `Cargo.lock` at `65e58e0`), so `verify-advisories` goes red if any returns. *(Binary-observable, added: a seeded `#[cfg(test)]` function in a `libs/mnemra-host/**` source module calling `PgPoolOptions::new().connect(…)` turns the gate red naming the file; the check admits the three sentinel entries present at `65e58e0` unchanged, and rejects, naming the file, a seeded entry whose site and recorded literal agree on each of `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:15432/x`, `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:1/x?port=1`, `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:1/x?host=/tmp`, `postgres://u@127.0.0.1:1,db.example:5432/x` and `postgres://u@localhost:5432/realdb`, as well as an entry whose site passes a `const`, and an entry whose site calls `connect` on a matching literal; a seeded read of `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` in a test file other than the accessor module turns the gate red; in the change that removes the `schema_init` and `coordination_schema` entries, `grep -n 'EmbeddedEngine' libs/mnemra-host/schema/init.rs` returns nothing, a `schema_init` test acquires the schema-less database and observes no `mnemra` schema before its first `init` and the migrations table after it, and the `pg_roles` / `pg_database` / `pg_tablespace` comparison across the two runs is equal; bumping `sqlx` in `Cargo.lock` without re-recording the check's constant turns it red; renaming the pool-source function without updating the list turns the self-test red; `cargo deny check bans` fails on a seeded `postgresql_embedded` dependency.)* [P-GuaranteeByMechanism; P-TrustworthySignal; control-integrity CI5; P-SecurityLayered — the deny ban is the supply-chain layer, and the sentinel rule enumerates the permitted target rather than matching a forbidden one; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — a correction is checked by the predicate it corrects, so the sentinel's seeds cover the shapes the round-2 rule admitted; maintainer ruling 7867 item 2; Bolt H1; Warden H3, H4, H6, M2, R2 sentinel finding; Charon C-1, C-3])*

- R-0120-g (the fresh-machine job replaces `verify-cold-start`'s "a fresh machine reaches serving"): A CI job SHALL run, from a clean runner with only the service container: `mnemra check-db --setup-sql` applied by the service's superuser, `\password` for both roles (scripted through `psql`), `CREATE EXTENSION vector`, `mnemra init` as the owner role (exit `0`), `mnemra check-db` as the serving role (exit `0`), then the host spawned as the serving role until `mnemra ready` exits `0` — using the deployment document's compose snippet and setup commands verbatim, so the documented operator path is the tested path. The job SHALL run **inside the required "CI gates" check** (`CLAUDE.md`: the `protect-main` ruleset requires that one status check), as a step of the gates job or as a job the gates job depends on — never as a separate non-required job, which a PR could merge around. *(Binary-observable: the job's steps appear in the "CI gates" check's log and a seeded failure in them turns that check red; the log shows the five exit codes in order; a seeded typo in the documented setup commands makes it fail.)* [P-CheckCoversTheClaim — "a fresh machine reaches serving" is claimed only by a job that does it; P-SecurityLayered — the structural layer is the required check, not an advisory job; V-Consistency — docs and CI run the same commands; Bolt M7] *(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119: this job is also the condition that **licenses retiring `verify-cold-start`** (R-0120-c(3) as amended): the two land in one change and the fresh-machine job's five exit codes appear in that change's required-check log. Its setup SHALL include the TLS steps of R-0120-c(1), so "the documented operator path" it runs is the TLS one. "With only the service container" and "applied by the service's superuser" read: with only the Postgres container a job step starts under R-0120-c(1), and applied by that container's superuser **within the setup step**, the superuser credential confined there as R-0120-c(1) confines it. **Revised 2026-10-22 (#4119; threat model F-4):** the fresh-machine job SHALL be **its own CI job**, on its own runner, with its own Postgres container started under R-0120-c(1) from the same image reference. It never runs as a step of the gates job and never shares the gates job's container, so the database `mnemra init` creates there never meets the store guard of R-0120-a(8), and that guard SHALL NOT exempt it by name. The gates job (`ci`, "CI gates") SHALL list it under `needs:` and SHALL run under `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, and the gates job's first step SHALL fail, naming the job, unless `needs.<job id>.result` is `success`. A job skipped because a job it needs failed may report as passing to a required check, so `needs:` alone could let a pull request merge around a red fresh-machine job; that GitHub reports such a skip as passing is INFERRED, and the seeded failure below is the measurement. *Superseded: "as a step of the gates job or as a job the gates job depends on" and "inside the required "CI gates" check", which now reads: the job gates the required check through `needs:` and the success test above; and the observables "the job's steps appear in the "CI gates" check's log", "the log shows the five exit codes in order" and, from the 2026-10-20 note, "the fresh-machine job's five exit codes appear in that change's required-check log", which now read: the five exit codes appear in order in the fresh-machine job's own log, and "CI gates" is red when that job fails. Run as a step, `mnemra init`'s database would sit on the container the test binaries use, so the store guard would refuse every binary after it, which presses for a by-name exemption (control-integrity CI5).* *(Binary-observable, added: `ci.yml` declares the fresh-machine job with its own container start, and the `ci` job lists it under `needs:`; no step of the `ci` job runs `mnemra init`; a seeded failure in the fresh-machine job turns "CI gates" red, not skipped, and so does the fresh-machine job seeded `if: false`, which GitHub reports as skipped (revised 2026-10-25, #4119 activity 8382; Warden L2); the store guard's allowlist names no database beyond the four of R-0120-a(8).)* [P-CheckCoversTheClaim; P-LeastAuthority; P-SecurityLayered — fail closed; control-integrity CI5; Bolt M5; threat model F-4])* *(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: **one fresh-machine job per supported major.** The fresh-machine job is one job per image reference `ci.yml` holds (R-0120-c (5) as amended 2026-10-29), written out rather than generated by a matrix, each on its own clean runner with its own container started under R-0120-c(1) from that reference, and each reports its server version under R-0120-c (7) as amended. The 2026-10-22 rule applies to each: the gates job lists each under `needs:`, and its first step fails unless each one's `result` is `success`. A second run on a runner a first run used would not be the clean runner this clause names (P-CheckCoversTheClaim). Every job runs the deployment document's compose snippet verbatim, so at two keys one snippet text has to start each job's image from outside itself. **Deferred, with a mechanical tripwire: where the compose snippet takes its image.** At V0 the table holds one key and `ci.yml` one reference, so a rule for two images in one snippet text would be built for a second key that does not exist (P-Defer — a mechanism waits until evidence forces the choice). Decision content when it fires: (a) the snippet names its image through one variable that the deployment document defines and each fresh-machine job sets from its own reference, so the text stays verbatim; (b) the deployment document carries one snippet per supported major, and each job runs the one for its major; (c) each job writes its reference into the snippet text before running it, giving up "verbatim". **The tripwire** is R-0117-a (5)'s key-count pin: the change that adds a second key fails `supported_majors_count_changed` until the pin is edited, and the failure message names this image source (R-0117-a (5) as amended). Its key-set pin fires the same way on a change that replaces the only key, failing `supported_majors_set_changed` with the same message, because a replaced major needs a snippet that starts the new image. Where the snippet starts the server the fresh-machine job reports on, a snippet that still fixes one image also fails that same change at the reconciliation of R-0120-c (7): both jobs' servers report one major, so it fails `run_report_missing` naming the other major and `fresh-machine`, keyed on the servers' own reports. **Merge point with #4412:** the snippet's scope is maintainer ruling SG-3 (#4412: the snippet covers PostgreSQL and pgvector only, and the host runs as a stdio child of the job step), and #4412 is drafting the rule that the fresh-machine job starts Postgres from that snippet. Whichever of #4291 and #4412 lands second states the snippet's image source in the snippet's own wording under that scope, and reads this deferral's options against it. [P-Defer DF1 — the pin fires on the change that would break the one-image assumption] *(Binary-observable, added: on a seeded two-reference `ci.yml`, two fresh-machine jobs run, each on its own runner, and each log shows the five exit codes in order; a seeded removal of one of them turns "CI gates" red with `run_report_missing` naming that major and `fresh-machine`.)* [maintainer ruling 2026-10-25 (#4291); P-TrustworthySignal TS2 — the per-key runs are reconciled])*

- R-0120-h (expected failures with a named exit condition, a detector on the exit, and a signature — visible in the gate output): A test that pins a requirement whose mechanism is implementation-time — today exactly two: the cleartext/MD5 authentication-request refusal and the same-connection peer-uid check (R-0117-f) — SHALL be a **named expected failure**, not an `#[ignore]` and not a red gate. **Mechanism:** the test body runs its assertion through a harness-level `expect_failure(name, signature)` wrapper from the fixture module: the wrapper looks the name up in the single-sourced `XFAIL` list, and (i) when the assertion fails **with the recorded failure signature** (the specific assertion message, never "any failure") it records the expected failure and returns `Ok` so `cargo test` and `nextest` stay green; (ii) when the assertion **passes** it panics with `XFAIL entry <name> must be removed: the mechanism has landed`; (iii) when it fails with a different signature it panics with that failure — so a regression hiding behind an XFAIL is impossible. The gate prints one `GATE test XFAIL <name> (exit: <condition>; sqlx <version>)` line per entry from the wrapper's record. **Admission rule and count pin** (control-integrity CI5 — bypass lists accrete): an entry is admitted only with the test name, the failure signature, the exit condition (the driver exposing a pre-startup hook or the host owning the connection's startup packet; the driver exposing an auth-request filter or the host owning the authentication exchange), the requirement it pins, and the **locked `sqlx` version it was evaluated against**; the list length is pinned (`2` at landing) and the pin changes only in the same change as the entry. **Detector on the exit** (P-Defer DF1): a gate check reads `sqlx`'s version from `Cargo.lock` and fails when it differs from the version recorded beside any entry — every driver bump forces a re-evaluation of each entry (re-record the version, or remove the entry because the mechanism is now available). An entry whose test is absent fails the gate. *(Binary-observable: the gate output carries exactly two `XFAIL` lines at landing, each naming the sqlx version; deleting an entry while its test still fails turns the gate red; making a listed test pass without removing its entry turns the gate red; a seeded entry naming a non-existent test, or a seeded third entry without the pin bump, turns the gate red; a seeded failure with a different message under a listed name fails the test; bumping `sqlx` in `Cargo.lock` without touching the recorded versions turns the gate red; `cargo test` and `nextest` exit `0` with both entries present.)* [P-TrustworthySignal — a known failure is reported as what it is, matched by signature, never hidden, and its flip is detected; P-Defer DF1 — the exit condition is named and the sqlx-version check is the detector; control-integrity CI5 — the bypass list is pinned and admission-gated]

- R-0120-i (one server per lane, and concurrent runs within one lane: another run's failure, interruption or kill, and its teardown and sweeps, leave a live run's objects in place) *(Added 2026-10-28, #4363; revised the same day for maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9016; a draft lock until the maintainer's exit approval, (h))*: **(a) One server per lane.** A **lane** is a line of work that runs this repository's gates, such as the implementation lane and the design lane, which is identified by its **lane id**, a declared lane name (below). Each lane SHALL have its own PostgreSQL server, on its own port, with its own harness role and harness URL, its own template database, and its own superuser credential. No two lanes SHALL use one server. This is the maintainer's ruling. He selected only "Separate server per lane (Recommended)", whose option text read "each lane gets its own Postgres server and port, so lanes cannot interfere", and did not select "one harness role per lane (D2), keep sharing and patch in round 3, cut scope and park" (#4363 activity 9016, Puck's record of his selection; the option wording was Puck's, and this text is the draft's, not his).

  **The lane claim, by mechanism.** The claim is a mechanism, not a convention:
  - **Write.** The setup step runs as the server's superuser (R-0120-c(1) and R-0120-d, both as amended). It takes the lane id from `just test-lane show`, capturing it in a variable with an explicit status check, and refuses, writing nothing, when the command fails or prints nothing. It then writes `COMMENT ON DATABASE mnemra_test_template IS 'mnemra_test_lane=<lane id>'`, running `psql` with `ON_ERROR_STOP` set. The id is passed to `psql` as a variable and quoted by `psql`, and is never interpolated into SQL text. *Illustrative, not normative:* `lane="$(just test-lane show)" || exit 1; [ -n "$lane" ] || exit 1`, then `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -v lane="$lane"` running `\set claim mnemra_test_lane= :lane` and then `COMMENT ON DATABASE mnemra_test_template IS :'claim';`. *Superseded (round 4 draft):* the illustrative `psql -v lane="$(just test-lane show)"`. A failed command substitution there does not stop the shell, so it wrote the empty claim `mnemra_test_lane=` over a valid one (Warden R4-3 = Bolt L-1, round 4).
  - **No silent re-claim.** In the same superuser session, before its `COMMENT`, the setup step reads the existing comment with `shobj_description`. It SHALL refuse to overwrite a claim that is present and names a different lane, naming both ids, unless the operator sets the `psql` variable `reclaim` (`-v reclaim=1`) by hand. The refusal names the remedy as "run the setup against your own lane's server", and does not mention `reclaim`. The read and the write are not atomic: two setups running at once against one server are not a case this rule covers. *Superseded (round 4 draft):* "its named flag `--reclaim`", which named no program to carry it, since the setup is a block of `psql` lines (Bolt L-5, round 4).
  - **Owner only.** The template is owned by the superuser, and only its owner can change its comment, so no lane's harness can rewrite the claim (INFERRED from PostgreSQL's ownership rule for `COMMENT`; a row below checks it on a server).
  - **Read, at every acquisition.** An acquisition is a fixture start, once per binary, not each test-database clone. The read runs on the harness connection to `postgres`, after the four checks of R-0120-a(6) and before the store guard of (8) or any provisioning. The fixture reads the comment with `shobj_description`. The comment must equal exactly `mnemra_test_lane=<id>`, with `<id>` in the lane-id grammar below. Anything else, an absent comment included, fails the first acquiring test with `server_lane_unclaimed { expected }`. A well-formed claim that names another lane fails it with `server_lane_mismatch { expected, found }`. Each message names the remedy: run the lane's setup block (R-0120-d) against that lane's own server. `server_lane_mismatch` also says "if this worktree belongs to the expected lane, correct the harness URL; do not re-declare the worktree to match the server", because re-declaring would make the check pass on the wrong server. In either case nothing is created.
  - **The other readers.** The teardown executor makes the same check before its first query of step (i), and exits non-zero on a refusal. The gated cell (R-0120-a(1)) makes it on its superuser connection, before its first test.
  - **Residual, named: the check binds the checked session.** The fixture's later harness sessions, and the executor's drops, reconnect to the same host and port. A different server swapped onto that port between the check and a drop would need a second, misconfigured server on that port. This is negligible and is not fixed here (Warden R3-7, round 3).

  So a worktree pointed by mistake at another lane's harness URL is refused at its first acquisition, before it can create, sweep or drop anything there.

  **The lane id: a declared lane name (maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9037).** Peter chose "Declared lane name (Recommended)". Its option text read: "each worktree has a small gitignored file naming its lane (e.g. implementation, design); a task worktree names its parent lane and shares that lane's server" (Puck's record of his choice; the option wording was Puck's). The conditions below come from the round-3 reviews, after the ruling. They are the draft's, and go to the maintainer at (h).
  - **Grammar.** A lane id matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$`. One function, the **lane-id function**, validates it, and it is the only producer of every lane id: the setup step's, the fixture's, the executor's, the gated cell's, and the superuser location's (#4363-C10). A value outside the grammar is refused with `lane_id_invalid`, before any connection. It is never escaped or repaired.
  - **The declaration.** A worktree's declaration is a gitignored file at the fixed path `.mnemra-test-lane` in its workspace root. It holds exactly two lines: `lane=<lane id>`, and `root=<the absolute workspace root it was written for>`. The lane-id function finds it under the compile-time workspace root of the binary that calls it; a binary is not relocatable, so that root is its own worktree's. It refuses:
    - an absent file, with `lane_undeclared`, whose message prints the exact declaration command;
    - a file whose `root=` line differs from that compile-time root, with `lane_declaration_foreign`, so a declaration copied from another worktree is refused;
    - a symlink at that path, or any other content, with `lane_declaration_invalid`.

    Each of the three refusals prints the exact declaration command, with the lane name from the file when it parses (the usual cause of `lane_declaration_foreign` is a worktree moved by hand).

    **Kept out of git, by mechanism.** The file holds an absolute workspace path, which on a developer machine carries the home directory, and this repository is public. The repository's own root `.gitignore` SHALL list `.mnemra-test-lane`; adding that entry is the implementation's. A standing check SHALL run `git -c core.excludesFile=/dev/null check-ignore -v .mnemra-test-lane` and require a match whose source is the repository's root `.gitignore` and whose reported pattern does not begin with `!`. `check-ignore -v` also reports a negated match, so a `!.mnemra-test-lane` line would otherwise pass while the file is not ignored (INFERRED from git's documentation of `--verbose`; Warden, confirmation of `f1cc7d7`). A developer's global ignore file, or `.git/info/exclude`, cannot satisfy it. The same check SHALL require that `git ls-files .mnemra-test-lane` lists nothing. `just test-lane declare` SHALL refuse to write, naming the path, when that check fails. The standing check is a member of `NONPG_TEST_FLAGS`, landed in the change that adds the lane tool, with the list's count pin and `EXPECTED_TEST_FILE_COUNT` bumped in the same commit, so it runs in both chains on every `just ci`. *Superseded (round 4 draft):* "The file is gitignored, so the path in it is never published", a claim nothing checked (Warden R4-2 = Bolt N-3, round 4).
  - **The declaration command.** One trusted command writes the declaration: `just test-lane declare <lane id>`. It validates the id and writes the file fresh and atomically, recording the worktree's own compile-time root. `just test-lane show` prints the validated lane id, or fails with one of the refusals above. Both run the lane-id function through one small non-default workspace binary, which is neither the production `mnemra` binary nor the teardown executor. No recipe, workflow step or document computes a lane id any other way.
  - **Its weight is bounded.** The lane-id function and its binary SHALL depend on no crate of the host library (`libs/mnemra-host`), and so on none of its database, WebAssembly or cryptography dependencies. The function lives in a small crate, or a `#[path]` module that the fixture and the tool both include. A fresh worktree's first `declare` or `show` then compiles only that tool. The implementation records that tool's cold build time on the CI runner in its landing pull request, as evidence. *Rejected: a wall-clock gate on the cold build* — on a shared runner its time varies with load, so it would go red with no code change (P-TrustworthySignal). The dependency rule is the computable proxy (Bolt N-1, round 4).
  - **Checked before the chain.** `just ci`'s existing preflight, and the helper of R-0120-c(4), run `just test-lane show` before the executor's build and before any gate, and refuse with the same message. So an undeclared worktree fails in its first seconds, not at its first Postgres gate several minutes in (Bolt N-2, round 4).
  - **Who runs it.** The operator runs it once per lane worktree, at setup (R-0120-d as amended). For a task worktree, whoever creates it runs `just test-lane declare <parent lane>` before its first Postgres gate. Inside this repository the mechanism is the loud refusal: the first acquisition in an undeclared task worktree fails with `lane_undeclared`, printing that command. Running the command automatically when the harness creates a task worktree needs a hook in the harness, or in the tooling that dispatches work into task worktrees. This repository owns neither, so that is a dependency on those tools, not a requirement of this clause. Until it exists, an undeclared task worktree fails loudly once and is fixed by one command.
  - **CI.** CI declares the lane `ci` in a step before the setup step (R-0120-c as amended).

  **The tension between root binding and automatic declaration, resolved.** Warden asked that a declaration be bound to the root it was written for, so a copied file is refused (R3-2, round 3). Bolt asked that task worktrees be declared without a manual step on every dispatch (M-3, round 3; 13 task worktrees existed on 2026-10-28, a count INHERITED from that review). A declaration copied from the parent worktree satisfies Bolt and is exactly what Warden's rule refuses. The declaration command satisfies both: it writes a fresh, root-bound file naming the parent lane, so nothing is copied and a copy still fails. What it leaves open is who invokes it at creation, which is the outside dependency named above.

  (Anchors: ruling 9016 for one server per lane, and ruling 9037 for the declared name; P-GuaranteeByMechanism — two lanes on one server is the cross-lane reap class, which produces no signal of its own, so the rule is a check that fails loudly; P-SecurityLayered — an unclaimed or unreadable claim refuses, and a value that reaches superuser SQL and a credential path is validated against a closed grammar, never escaped; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the check is keyed on the lane's identity, not on the port the URL happens to name, and a declaration is bound to the root it was written for; V-Consistency — one producer, as (b) requires for the run id; V-Simplicity — one catalog read on a connection the fixture already holds, and no new server object.) *Rejected:*
  - *one server per lane by convention, a port per lane written in the deployment document* — a copied harness URL then shares a server with no signal, the class the ruling exists to end;
  - *detecting another lane by its live objects on the server* — it sees only a lane that has objects at that moment, misses an idle one, and needs the lane id in every generated name, which the 63-byte budget of R-0120-a(3) has no room for;
  - *a claim database the harness creates* — a harness can create and drop it, so a lane could re-claim another lane's server;
  - *the worktree as the lane* (O5 (a), not chosen by the maintainer) — it needs a server, port, certificate and setup for every gate-running worktree, task worktrees included, and a worktree recreated at another path gets a new id;
  - *the server's address as the lane* (O5 (c), moot after the ruling and unsound) — the setup and the acquirer would both derive the id from the URL, so a copied URL always matches and the check could never fail (P-CheckCoversTheClaim);
  - *copying the parent's declaration into a task worktree* — indistinguishable from the accident the claim exists to catch, so it is refused as foreign, and the declaration command writes a fresh one instead;
  - *a declaration holding the name alone* — a copied file passes it (Warden R3-2, round 3).

  **Concurrent runs within one lane.** Within one lane, concurrent runs still share the lane's server: two `just ci` runs in one worktree, a task worktree's run beside its lane's main worktree's, and E1's holding helpers, which are runs of their own. For two such concurrent runs A and B on the lane's server, the guarantee holds under four preconditions:
  - **(P1)** their run ids carry the same `<hash8>`, the case of one machine;
  - **(P2)** their run ids differ, and each is bound to its own run owner under (b);
  - **(P3)** B's `<epoch>` is younger than 22 h, past which B's own fixture refuses to acquire ((j)), and the server and both fixtures keep synchronised clocks as R-0120-a(3) states. *Superseded (round 6 draft):* "younger than the age constant of R-0120-a's age sweep"; under (j) the age sweep no longer reaches a same-host run. This is the range that accepted risk `AR-0016`, as amended 2026-10-28 to runs within one lane, leaves protected (`docs/src/architecture/overview.md`);
  - **(P4)** the lane's server's connection limit admits both runs at once.

  Under P1–P4, no action of A SHALL remove or recreate any object of B's run, and B's run SHALL pass. B's objects are its test databases, its run-scoped owner and serving roles, and its per-run directory with the files in it. That SHALL hold in each of these cases:
  - A passes;
  - A's chain fails;
  - A's process group receives `SIGINT` or `SIGTERM`;
  - one of A's test binaries is killed with `SIGKILL` after its first acquisition, so A's `EXIT` trap still runs;
  - A's whole process group is killed with `SIGKILL` after its first acquisition, so no teardown of A runs;
  - A runs the gated cell of R-0120-a(1) while B acquires.

  The actions of A that can remove an object are the four drop paths of R-0120-a: a binary's exit hook (tier-2 R-0028, R-0120-b as amended); a standalone run owner's in-process steps (ii) and (iii) (R-0120-a(5)); sweep (b) and the age sweep at each of A's fixture starts; and A's teardown executor (R-0120-a(5)). That list was derived by reading R-0120-a, not computed. The acceptance below observes outcomes and does not depend on the list being complete.

  The protection on those paths is R-0120-a's, with sweep (b)'s liveness rule made precise in (b) below:
  - for a database, the parse-and-compare identity and the `datdba` check of (3);
  - for a role, the name parse of (3) alone, bounded by PostgreSQL 16's rule that a `CREATEROLE` role administers only roles it created (9);
  - for the directory, the identity check of (5).

  **Outside the range, and not claimed:**
  - the failure of P3, which `AR-0016` as amended accepts within a lane;
  - the failure of P4 (a `53300` or a pool timeout). No clause of R-0120 sizes a lane's server for concurrent runs, and the clone lock's bound is task #4166's;
  - any action outside R-0120's specified behaviour, such as a hand-written statement or a modified fixture, taken by a process that holds the lane's harness credential.

  (Anchors: maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #3575 activity 8909 — concurrent runs are tested, which within one lane is what remains of that ruling after 9016; `AR-0016` as amended — the residual within a lane is accepted, so this clause claims no more than its preconditions deliver (P-CheckCoversTheClaim — draw no conclusion wider than the range).)
  **(b) Liveness, and the run id's binding.**
  - **One source.** A process's start epoch SHALL be read by one function, from the kernel's own record of the process's start time. That function SHALL be the only producer of every run id's `<epoch>`, the run owner's included, and the only reader sweep (b) uses for a live pid. No second implementation, in a recipe's shell or elsewhere, SHALL compute it. *Permitted shape, not required:* the run owner obtains its run id from a small workspace binary that calls the fixture's run-id source. That binary is not the teardown executor, which R-0120-a(5) forbids to generate a run id or to call that source.
  - **One source for the whole run id.** The same holds for `<hash8>`: one function computes it, and it is the only producer of every run id's hash.
  - **Tolerance.** Two start epochs for one pid are equal when they differ by at most 5 s, a code constant. The reason is its own: the epoch the run owner recorded and a later reading of the same process's start time can disagree. Both are rounded to whole seconds, and on Linux the start time is derived from a boot-time base that moves when the wall clock is stepped (INFERRED). 5 s covers a one-second rounding difference and a small clock step with margin. A pid reused within 5 s of the original process's start is not credible on a machine whose pid space cycles more slowly than that (INFERRED). A pid reused within that window reads as live, which keeps the object instead of removing it.
  - **Dead, reused and unverifiable.** A pid reads **dead** when `kill(pid, 0)` returns `ESRCH`, or when the process is a zombie (Linux `/proc` state `Z`, macOS `SZOMB`). It reads **reused** when its start epoch is read and differs from `<epoch>` by more than the tolerance. Every other outcome reads **unverifiable**: `EPERM`, a start time that cannot be read or parsed, or any other error. Sweep (b) keeps an unverifiable run's objects and prints `sweep_kept_unverifiable { run, reason }` on stderr at every sweep that meets it, so a permanent keep stays visible.
  - **Binding at acquisition.** The fixture SHALL refuse to acquire, failing the first acquiring test with `run_id_unbound { reason }`, when its run id's `<pid>` is neither its own process nor an ancestor of it. It SHALL refuse the same way when the start epoch it reads for that pid differs from `<epoch>` by more than the tolerance, and when the id's `<hash8>` differs from the hash it computes itself. So a run id that a process did not inherit from its own run owner is refused. A mismatch between the run owner's epoch and the fixture's reading fails the run that produced it, never another run's sweep.
  - **The executor's binding.** The teardown executor SHALL refuse, exiting non-zero before any drop, a run id whose `<pid>` reads live with a matching epoch and is not an ancestor of the executor, because that is another live run's id. A non-ancestor owner that reads unverifiable is refused the same way, failing closed. A dead or reused owner is an owner that exited before its trap finished, and the executor tears that run down.

  (Anchors: V-Consistency — one implementation of the value both sides compare, the rule R-0120-a(5) applies to the name parser ("the same functions … never copied"); the 5 s value has its own reason, stated under Tolerance, and R-0116-g's "within 5 s of the pidfile's recorded start epoch" is retired prior art only, not an anchor, since R-0116-g was retired with no successor; P-SecurityLayered — an unverifiable read denies the drop; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — identity on a hit, keyed on an attribute a copied id cannot carry.) *Rejected:*
  - *computing the epoch in the recipe's shell* — locale-formatted `ps` output and second-boundary arithmetic disagree with the kernel's value, and every disagreement reads as a reused pid and removes a live run;
  - *exact equality* — a one-second rounding difference has the same effect;
  - *reading an unverifiable result as "differs"* — it removes objects on an error;
  - *making the teardown executor the run-id source* — R-0120-a(5) rejected that (Warden's scoped re-check M1, #4119 activity 8452).

  **(c) Drop outcomes on the sweep path and in executor step (ii).** A drop issued by sweep (b) or the age sweep has exactly these outcomes:
  - success: removed;
  - SQLSTATE `3D000` from `DROP DATABASE` or `42704` from `DROP ROLE`, or the notice the `IF EXISTS` form issues in their place: **already gone**, counted removed, with no `fixture_teardown_failed` and no failed test;
  - SQLSTATE `55006`, `40P01` or `XX000`, the **transient** set, a code constant: retried up to 3 times at 500 ms, the retry shape of R-0120-a(4), and then classified again by its last outcome;
  - any other SQLSTATE or error: a sweep error under R-0120-a(5).

  An identity failure under (3) is not a drop outcome: the object is kept and reported `identity_mismatch` (R-0120-a #4363-C3). A sweep that counts an object already gone has met another process removing it first, either another sweep or the target run's own teardown. That is the case this rule exists for: after A's process group is killed, a re-run of `just ci` in A's worktree and B's next test binary can both start a fixture and both find A's objects under sweep (b). In the teardown executor's step (ii), `3D000` and `42704` count as removed only when the run's owner reads dead or reused under (b). While the owner reads live, the object is named under `vanished` (R-0120-a #4363-C2), because only a foreign actor removes a live run's objects. A binary's exit hook is unchanged. The members of the transient set are INFERRED from PostgreSQL's documented behaviour under concurrent DDL, not measured here. The drop function takes its statement runner as an injected input (P-TestableByDesign), so each outcome has a deterministic test below.

  (Anchors: P-TrustworthySignal — a red on B must mean that B failed, and a drop whose postcondition already holds is not B's failure; P-SecurityLayered — the outcomes that pass are enumerated by SQLSTATE, and every other outcome fails. **Ruled:** maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9046, "Not an error (Recommended)" (Puck's record, and the option wording was Puck's). "Already gone" is not an error, under the SQLSTATE pinning above, #4363-C2's owner-liveness condition and #4363-C3's identity outcome. That answers Charon's canon question, whether such a drop is an "error" under P-SecurityLayered's fail-closed rule ("a control that errors denies rather than passes through"). It is not, because the drop's postcondition, the object being absent, holds.) *Rejected by the ruling: counting "already gone" as an error* — a red on B would then report a failure B did not have, and a lane's concurrent sweeps would fail each other on a correct tree. *Also rejected:*
  - *serialising sweeps across processes under a second advisory-lock key* — it closes the sweep-against-sweep race but not a sweep racing a teardown executor, and every bound task #4166 puts on the clone lock would apply to a second lock too;
  - *re-querying the catalog after any error and counting absence as removed* — it would count a privilege error (`42501`) as success whenever another actor removed the object afterwards, so a defect in this run's own sweep would pass;
  - *retrying every error* — a non-transient error repeats.

  **(d) No test mutates the lane's server-wide objects.** Concurrent runs of one lane share the lane's server, so a test that changes a server-wide object fails the lane's other runs. No test SHALL change any of these:
  - the attributes, password or settings of the harness role or the bootstrap superuser;
  - their memberships in roles whose names do not fully parse to the run grammar of R-0120-a(3);
  - any database other than one whose name parses to the test's own run or to a run the test itself seeded (`postgres` and `mnemra_test_template` included);
  - a role or database setting that applies to `ALL`;
  - the lane claim of (a).

  A test MAY revoke the harness's membership in, or privilege on, a role or database of its own run or of a run it seeded, because that harms no other run. *Superseded (round 2):* "No test SHALL issue `ALTER ROLE`, `GRANT … TO`, `REVOKE … FROM` or `ALTER ROLE … SET` naming the harness role of `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, the bootstrap superuser, or any role the test did not itself create". It was keyed on the statement instead of the object changed, so it forbade positive controls R-0120-a still requires, such as a drop made to fail by revoking the harness's privilege on the test's own database (Warden N4, round 2).

  The cases of R-0120-a(6), (8) and (9) that would need such a change are shown over their decision functions' facts records (#4363-C6 for (6) and (9), #4363-C9 for (8)). Three on-server controls keep the queries that build those records covered without a change:
  - the facts the fixture reads for a harness URL naming the bootstrap superuser carry `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` and `rolreplication`, each true;
  - the facts it reads for the harness, after the harness self-granted this run's roles, carry `MEMBER` true for both run roles;
  - the store guard reads a fresh lane server's real `datname` list and admits it.

  **Detector.** The gated cell of R-0120-a(1) records, before its first test and after its last, only what concurrent runs of the lane do not move:
  - the harness role's `pg_authid` row, its password verifier included, which the cell can read as superuser;
  - the harness's memberships in roles whose names do not fully parse to the run grammar;
  - the `pg_db_role_setting` rows for the harness, for `ALL` (role `0`), and every row whose `setdatabase` is `postgres` or `mnemra_test_template`;
  - the bootstrap superuser's whole `pg_authid` row, its password verifier and attributes included;
  - every `pg_db_role_setting` row whose `setrole` is the bootstrap superuser. These are its role-level settings, which `pg_authid` does not hold: `pg_roles.rolconfig` is derived from the rows with `setdatabase = 0` (INFERRED from PostgreSQL's catalog documentation, and not checked against it in this session). *Superseded (round 4 draft):* the widened list recorded the superuser's `pg_authid` row in place of its `rolconfig`, which dropped its role-level settings, so `ALTER ROLE <superuser> SET role = <harness>`, the round-1 case, passed the detector (Warden R4-1, round 4);
  - the `pg_database` rows of `postgres` and `mnemra_test_template`, every column, `datacl`, `datistemplate`, `datallowconn`, `datconnlimit` and `datdba` included, and their `pg_shdescription` rows, the lane claim among them;
  - the set of database names that are neither `template0`, `template1`, `postgres`, `mnemra_test_template`, nor a name that parses to the run grammar.

  When the two records differ, the cell fails and names the difference **by column, never by value**: a changed password verifier is reported as `rolpassword changed`, so no verifier reaches test output, which CI publishes. When they are equal, it prints `shared_roles_unchanged` on its real stderr. Memberships in run-grammar roles are left out, because the lane's concurrent runs create and drop them, and R-0120-a(6) step 4 already polices each one. *Superseded (round 2):* the detector compared "every `pg_auth_members` row naming the harness as member", which a concurrent run's acquisition or sweep changes, so the cell failed on a correct tree (Bolt N-1, Warden N2, round 2). It also missed a password change and settings held in `pg_db_role_setting`. A change made and reverted inside the cell is still not seen; that residual is the lane's own and is accepted under `AR-0016` as amended. *Superseded (round 3 draft):* the detector recorded the superuser's `rolconfig` alone, and of `postgres` and the template only their names, so a change to the superuser's password or attributes, or to the template's `datallowconn`, `datistemplate`, connection limit, settings or privileges, passed it (Warden R3-3, round 3). *Rejected: also recording per run, from the run owner's trap through the executor.* The executor takes no argument (R-0120-a(5)), so a start-of-run record would need a second entry point. The harness cannot read `pg_authid`, so a per-run record would miss a password change, the main change it targets. A non-gated binary that changes the harness's own password or settings acts outside R-0120's specified behaviour, which (a) puts out of range.

  (Anchors: P-LeastAuthority — superuser authority stays with the one gated binary of R-0120-a(1); P-GuaranteeByMechanism — a change left in place fails loudly in the cell that made it; P-TrustworthySignal — the detector is keyed on what legitimate concurrency does not move, so its red means a change.) *Rejected:*
  - *a run-scoped probe role on the server.* Created by the harness, it is a harness membership under (6) step 4, because the harness holds `ADMIN` on every role it creates and that reports `MEMBER` true, so every run's acquisition fails while it exists. Created by the superuser, it has no harness membership, and the gated cell could drop it itself. But a probe the tests connect as needs `LOGIN` and a credential, so a killed cell leaves a privileged login role behind that no detector watches;
  - *a source lint for those statements* — role names are interpolated at run time, so a token walk cannot tell the harness from a run role.
  **(e) E1, the standing evidence.** E1 is a Postgres test binary that runs in **its own gate**. That gate is wired as a plain prerequisite of every chain in tier-2 R-0033(4)'s derived chain set, as `verify-smoke` is; at `0e82b3e` those chains are `_ci-verify-chain` and `_ci-verify-chain-nocoverage`. Its exact passed count is pinned, as R-0120-a(1) pins the gated cell's. It is a named standalone cell of tier-2 R-0033 and not a `PG_TEST_FLAGS` member, so it runs once per chain and never under coverage. It runs only under its run owner: it fails, naming the variable, when `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` is absent.
  - **The executor's path.** The run owner builds the executor before the chain (R-0120-a(5), "Order") and exports its path in `MNEMRA_TEST_TEARDOWN_EXECUTOR`, which only the fixture module reads. E1 fails, naming the variable, when it is unset or names no executable file. E1 spawns the executor from `env_clear()` with exactly `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and the target run's `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID`. Before the spawn, it asserts that the run id differs from the gate's own and from B's.
  - **The holding helper.** A holding helper is a process started without `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID`, so it owns a run of its own (R-0120-a(5)) whose run id names a real live pid and that process's real start epoch. It acquires through the shared fixture, prints its run id, and holds its acquisition until it is released, with its database, its two roles and its per-run directory present. On release it runs one query on its database as the serving role and exits. It also exits at the end of its standard input, and at a hard deadline, a code constant of 120 s. Its vehicle SHALL need no `#[ignore]`, and SHALL leave E1's pinned passed count the same whether or not a holder runs. *Permitted shape:* a re-execution of E1's own binary, filtered to one entry test that returns without acquiring when a holder variable, read in the fixture module, is absent.
  - **Bounds.** Every wait in E1 has a timeout from a code constant and fails naming what it waited for. Any process E1 kills is reaped (`waitpid`) before the next acquisition, so no killed run is left as a zombie.
  - **The identity snapshot.** "B's objects survive" is observed by identity, never by name. A snapshot of B holds: the `pg_database.oid` of each database that parses (3) to B's run; the `pg_roles.oid` of B's two roles; and, for B's per-run directory as the R-0120-a(5) locator resolves it, its device and inode numbers and the name and SHA-256 digest of each file in it. A role that is dropped and then recreated by B's next acquisition keeps its name but gets a new `oid`.
  - **No guard exception.** The snapshot, the helper and the run-id lookup are functions of the fixture module, so E1 needs no exception to the R-0120-f guard, whose exception list is exhaustive. E1's process spawns are `Command::new` sites that the count-pinned allowlist of #4165 counts, and the pin is bumped in the same change.
  - **Attribution.** Every fixture connection sets `application_name` to `<run>_<pid>`. Each acquisition prints one `fixture_sweep { removed, already_gone, kept_unverifiable, identity_mismatch }` event on its real stderr, so a vanished object can be traced to the sweep that removed it.

  **(f) E2, superseded 2026-10-28 by ruling 9016.** The round-1 and round-2 drafts specified E2 as landing evidence on #4161's pull request: two `just ci` runs in two worktrees on one shared server, in modes F, I, T, K1, K2 and G. Under one server per lane there is no shared server to run it on, and the lane claim of (a) refuses the setup it would need. What E2 observed within one lane is covered elsewhere:
  - at the drop-path level, by E1, which runs concurrent runs on the lane's server in every `just ci`;
  - the trap's signal paths, by R-0120-a's single-run rows (`kill -INT` of `just ci` leaves no object parsing to its run; a seeded failing gate; a binary killed with `SIGKILL` inside `just ci`) and by the standing trap test of (g), which the maintainer ruled in (#4363 activity 9046, O4);
  - mode G's concern, by (d).

  *Rejected: keeping E2 as two concurrent `just ci` runs within one lane* — beside E1 its remaining content is a full chain run alongside a full chain, at an INFERRED one and a half to two and a half hours, observed once.
  **(g) The trap's signal handlers.** The run owner's recipe runs under `bash` and SHALL install explicit `INT` and `TERM` handlers that call `exit`, so its `EXIT` trap runs on both signals. A default signal disposition is not relied on. (Anchors: P-CheckCoversTheClaim — a row that interrupts a run observes the trap only if the signal reaches the shell that holds it; *superseded:* "E2's I and T rows observe the trap only if the signal reaches the shell that holds it", since E2 is superseded ((f)).)

  **The standing trap test (maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9046, O4 option (a), "Add a standing test (Recommended)"; Puck's option text).** The trap body SHALL live in a script that the `ci` recipe and the helper of R-0120-c(4) source, as they source `scripts/ci-reap.sh` today. The trap body is: the `EXIT` trap, the `INT` and `TERM` handlers, the executor's call, and the choice of the `GATE leaked-objects` line from the closed outcome set. A standing test drives that same function, with no copy of it. It uses a stub chain and a stub executor. In the signal cases only, the stub chain runs a bounded `sleep`, a code constant of 30 s. In the normal and failing cases it exits at once. The stub executor records each run and prints one `teardown_report` naming nothing. The test waits for the trap's output with its own short bound. It runs the script through the same interpreter resolution the recipe uses, `/usr/bin/env bash` on the test's `PATH`, because bash versions defer a trapped signal differently. It starts the script in its own process group with `CommandExt::process_group(0)`, which is safe Rust, and needs no `pre_exec`. Before the signal cases it checks the `SIGINT` disposition it inherited: on Linux from `SigIgn` in `/proc/self/status`, and elsewhere through a probe child that reports whether `kill -INT` reaches a trivial trapped shell. When `SIGINT` was ignored at entry, the test fails by name with `sigint_ignored_at_entry: run the gate in the foreground`, and does not report the handler row. A shell started in the background inherits `SIGINT` ignored, and bash cannot trap a signal ignored at its start (INFERRED from bash's documentation), so without this check the row would blame a correct handler (Bolt M-2, round 5).
  - On normal completion, the stub executor ran once, the line is `GATE leaked-objects PASS`, and the exit status is `0`.
  - With a failing stub chain, the stub executor ran once, and the run exits with the chain's status.
  - With `SIGINT` sent to the run's process group, the stub executor ran once, a `GATE leaked-objects` line is printed, and the run exits non-zero.
  - With `SIGTERM`, the same.
  - With `SIGKILL` sent to the group, the stub executor did not run and no `GATE` line is printed. That is the expected outcome, and it is the case sweep (b) exists for.
  - Started with `SIGINT` ignored at entry, the test fails naming `sigint_ignored_at_entry`, and reports no handler row.
  - The normal and failing cases each finish without waiting for the 30 s `sleep`.

  Every wait is bounded and fails by name. The test starts no PostgreSQL and builds nothing. It is a new test binary under `libs/mnemra-host/tests/` and a member of `NONPG_TEST_FLAGS`, as `libs/mnemra-host/tests/ci_reap_baseline.rs` is today. It lands in the change that moves the trap body into the sourced script, with two count pins set in the same commit to the tree's computed counts at that commit: the `NONPG_TEST_FLAGS` count pin in the `justfile`, and `EXPECTED_TEST_FILE_COUNT` in `libs/mnemra-host/tests/lint_engine_construction.rs`. Those counts are net of `libs/mnemra-host/tests/ci_reap_baseline.rs`, which R-0120-e retires in the same #4161 work (Bolt L-4, round 5). (Anchors: P-GuaranteeByMechanism — a handler that is wrong or missing fails a standing test, not a manual interrupt; P-TestableByDesign — the stub chain and stub executor are the injected seams.) *Rejected by the ruling: (b) no standing test* — a wrong handler would show only when someone interrupted a run (Bolt). *Superseded:* the draft's round-1 objection, that a nested run owner inside a gate would rebuild the executor. The stub executor answers it.

  **(h) For the exit gate.** The direction, one server per lane, is the maintainer's (ruling 9016). So is the lane id's source, a declared lane name (ruling 9037). He has also ruled on E-1, O1, O2 and O4 (#4363 activity 9046, Puck's record of his exit-gate answers; the option wording was Puck's), and those rulings are locked in (c), (i), (j) and (g). The one exit-gate item that remains is the maintainer's approval of the corrections bundle as a whole. Every other lock in this clause is a draft lock of #4363 until then, and so is every lock in the #4363 notes on R-0120-a, R-0120-c, R-0120-d, R-0120-e, tier-1 R-0024, tier-2 R-0027 and R-0033, in the amendments of `AR-0016`, and in ADR P-0031's Amendment 2026-10-28. That the amendment does not land before that approval is a **convention, not a gate**: the `protect-main` ruleset requires 0 approving reviews and the repository allows auto-merge (MEASURED 2026-10-22, recorded in this repository's instructions). Each lock is labelled a draft, so a premature landing would still read as unratified; that label covers every #4363 note, the overview's and tier-2's included. *Superseded (round 4 draft):* the claim held for some notes only (Charon R4-2, round 4).
  - **Corrections to locked text: open, for the maintainer's approval as a whole.** The bundle holds:
    - the R-0120-a corrections #4363-C1 to #4363-C12;
    - the R-0120-c and R-0120-d notes (one server per lane);
    - R-0120-e's temp-root retirement;
    - tier-1 R-0024's interim and tightened criteria;
    - tier-2 R-0027's and R-0028's notes and R-0033's third cell;
    - the amendments of `AR-0016`;
    - P-0031's Amendment 2026-10-28;
    - the overview's note on `EE-other-lane`;
    - the round-3 conditions on the declared lane name in (a): the grammar, the root binding, the named command and the CI declaration.

    Draft recommendation: approve.
  - **E-1, "already gone counts as removed": ruled, "Not an error"** (#4363 activity 9046), and locked in (c). The record of positions:
    - Bolt accepted the intent and asked for the transient retry with a deterministic test (taken, (c)).
    - Warden accepted on three conditions: pinning the SQLSTATEs, the `identity_mismatch` outcome, and keying executor step (ii) on the owner's liveness (all taken: (c), #4363-C2, #4363-C3).
    - Charon asked whether such a drop is an "error" under P-SecurityLayered's fail-closed rule. The ruling answers it: no.
  - **E-2, the seed reading** (#4363-C4), and **the 23-hour supersession** (#4363-C5): part of the bundle above.
  - **(O1) The per-run directory of a run that never reached teardown: ruled, "Sweeps remove it", option (a)** (#4363 activity 9046), and locked in (i). This is against the reviewers' converged (b), so (i) carries the burden. It states the locator, the identity checks, the error rule and the reach, and keeps (b) and the positions as the record. That activity records that the maintainer was told (a) needs a cross-run locator #4164 does not provide, plus stronger directory identity checks ("more mechanism").
  - **(O2) A live run older than the age constant: ruled, both (c) and (b)** (#4363 activity 9046: "Age sweep skips same-host runs" and "Old run refuses to continue", at 22 h), and locked in (j). `AR-0016` is amended again for it. The record of positions: Warden held (c) with (b); Bolt held (b) with the 22 h margin; Charon held that the guarantee be bounded to what `AR-0016` accepts.
  - **(O3) Whether E2 gains a firing condition: collapsed under ruling 9016.** E2 is superseded ((f)). The positions are kept as the record: Bolt wanted a source digest whose movement without a new E2 record fails a gate step; Warden wanted it parked with a named cadence.
  - **(O4) A standing test of the run owner's trap: ruled, "Add a standing test", option (a)** (#4363 activity 9046), and locked in (g). Bolt held (a).
  - **(O5) What the lane id is keyed on: ruled 2026-10-28** (#4363 activity 9037, "Declared lane name"; Puck's record, and the option wording was Puck's), and locked in (a). Its rejected options, (a) the worktree and (c) the server's address, are kept there with their reasons. The round-3 conditions on the declared name are part of the bundle above.

  **(i) A swept run's directory is removed with it (maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9046, O1 option (a), "Sweeps remove it"; Puck's option text).** When sweep (b) or the age sweep removes a run's databases and roles, it SHALL also remove that run's per-run directory, under four conditions. #4363-C11 amends R-0120-a's sweep for this.
  - **(1) The locator is a record on the server.** The first acquirer of a run creates the per-run directory exclusively, refusing one that already exists, at the location #4164 sets. Then, in one transaction, it creates the run's owner role and writes `COMMENT ON ROLE <owner role> IS 'mnemra_test_run_dir=<absolute path>;dev=<device>;ino=<inode>'`. The device and inode are read with `fstat` on the descriptor that created the directory. Before writing, the fixture checks the path against an allowlist: it must match `^/[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,255}$`, contain no `..` segment, and pass the read rules below. When any rule fails, the fixture fails the acquiring test with `run_dir_path_invalid` and writes no record. The values are bound, never spliced into text: the fixture runs `SELECT format('COMMENT ON ROLE %I IS %L', $1, $2)` as a parameterised query, with the role name and the record text as its two bound values, and then executes the statement text that query returns. The path comes from the environment (#4164's location), and `COMMENT` itself takes no bound parameters, which is why the quoting is done by `format` on bound inputs. *Superseded (round 7 draft):* a denylist (`;`, `'`, `=` and control characters) and "`format('COMMENT ON ROLE %I IS %L', …)` executed inside a `DO` block, and never by string concatenation". A `DO` body takes no bound parameters, so the path was spliced into the body as text, and a `$$` in it could end the body early (Warden R7-1, round 7). The role passwords are written into the directory only after that transaction commits.

    Every remover reads that record with `shobj_description` before it drops the owner role: a sweep, the teardown executor, and a standalone run owner. A sweep finds it in the catalog it already reads to find dead runs, so the locator depends on neither the sweeper's environment nor its worktree. The record's text is validated, and a remover that finds it invalid does not remove anything at that path. Validation requires:
    - an absolute path, with no `.` or `..` component;
    - a basename that fully parses as `mnemra_test_<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>_<suffix>`, through the right-to-left parser of R-0120-a(3), with its run fields equal to the run's own and `<suffix>` exactly six characters of `[A-Za-z0-9]`. That is exact equality after parsing: a name that contains the fields but is not exactly that shape is refused;
    - decimal device and inode fields.
