    **The record is a locator, not a control.** Every holder of the lane's harness credential can rewrite or clear it, because the harness holds `CREATEROLE` and `ADMIN` on every run role (R-0120-a(9); that `COMMENT ON ROLE` needs exactly those is INFERRED from PostgreSQL's documentation). The control is (2): a forged record can point a remover only at a directory that the remover's uid owns, at mode `0700`, with the dead run's own name and the recorded device and inode, and the remover deletes only regular files there. What forging can do is suppress removal, and (3) reports that at every sweep (Warden L1, round 6).

    This record is the "record whose own location is so computable" that R-0120-a(5) admits. It settles the choice (5) left to #4164, so the executor uses it too (#4363-C11).

    **Residual, named:** a kill between the directory's creation and the transaction's commit leaves a directory with no record, which no sweep locates. It holds no password file yet, because the passwords are written after the commit.

    (Anchors: P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the locator is keyed on the run being removed, not on whoever removes it; V-Consistency — one locator for every remover, which is (5)'s own second branch; V-Simplicity — a comment on an object the run already creates, read by a query the sweep already runs.)
  - **(2) Identity is checked on an opened descriptor, before anything is removed.** The remover opens the recorded path with `O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`, and opens its parent the same way. On the directory's descriptor, `fstat` must show all of the following:
    - its owner is the remover's effective uid;
    - its mode is exactly `0700`;
    - its device and inode equal the record's.

    The remover removes the entries with `unlinkat` relative to the directory's descriptor, regular files only and with no recursion. It removes the directory itself by name, relative to the parent's descriptor, after an `fstatat` of that name with `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` again shows the same device and inode.

    A directory that fails any check is not removed, and is reported `sweep_dir_identity_mismatch { run, check }`, naming the check. Where the platform lacks one of these calls, the remover refuses to remove the directory and reports that it refused: it fails closed. This answers Warden's objection that (5)'s check "does not cover the directory's own owner, mode, link status or (dev, ino) on an opened descriptor". The teardown executor's "Identity before a removal" in R-0120-a(5) gains the same checks.

    **Permitted, not required:** resolving the whole path with no symlinks, through `openat2` with `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` on Linux or `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` on macOS, where available.

    **Residual windows, named (INFERRED from POSIX semantics):**
    - `O_NOFOLLOW` guards only the final path component, so a symlinked intermediate component is followed unless the permitted resolution above is used;
    - an entry swapped between its type check and `unlinkat` makes the remover unlink a symlink rather than its target, or fail on a directory;
    - a name swapped between the parent's `fstatat` and the final removal lets it remove at most an empty directory of that exact name.

    The device and inode, the owner, the mode, the name, regular-files-only, and removal of an empty directory together bound what a swap can reach (Warden L2, round 6).

    **The calls sit behind a safe wrapper.** The descriptor-relative calls (`openat` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat`, `fstatat`, `unlinkat`) SHALL be reached through a filesystem trait. Its real implementation is the only place they live, and it adds no `unsafe` code: `unsafe_code` stays denied workspace-wide. *Permitted shape, not required:* a crate already in the workspace's `Cargo.lock` that offers safe wrappers for these calls. Which dependency, if any, is the implementation's choice, and a new direct dependency is checked by `verify-advisories` like any other (Bolt M-4, round 5). (Anchors: P-SecurityLayered — a control that cannot confirm identity does not act; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — identity on a hit.)
  - **(3) A directory error is reported, never fatal to the acquirer, and the owner role is its retry handle.** A sweep removes a run in this order:
    1. read the record;
    2. drop the run's databases, then its serving role;
    3. remove the directory;
    4. drop the owner role only when the directory was removed, or `ENOENT` showed it already gone.

    **Discovery.** Sweep (b) and the age sweep find runs from both `pg_database` and `pg_roles`, from every name that parses under R-0120-a(3), because a retained run has an owner role and no database (Bolt, round 7).

    `ENOENT` at any step counts as removed: already gone, as (c) rules for drops. On every other outcome the sweep keeps the owner role, after `ALTER ROLE <owner role> NOLOGIN PASSWORD NULL` so that it holds no credential. That outcome is a removal error, an identity failure, a platform refusal, or an invalid record. The retained role carries the record to the next sweep, which retries and reports again. When the disarming `ALTER ROLE` itself fails, the sweep reports `sweep_role_not_disarmed { run, error }`, keeps the role, and retries the disarm and the removal at every later sweep (Warden R7-2, round 7).

    R-0120-a(6) step 4 still admits such a retained role: its name fully parses to a run-scoped `…_owner` role, and its own `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` and `rolreplication` are false, so the harness's membership in it stays admitted. A run whose owner role exists with an absent or `NULL` record is reported as `sweep_dir_unrecorded { run }`, and its role is kept the same way. Each such outcome is printed on the real stderr as `sweep_dir_not_removed { run, error }`, `sweep_dir_identity_mismatch { run, check }`, `sweep_dir_unrecorded { run }` or `sweep_role_not_disarmed { run, error }`, with the retained role named. Every process whose sweep meets the run prints it, but at most once per process for a given run, so a binary that acquires many times does not repeat it (Bolt, round 7). Each report names the operator's remedy: correct the directory's owner or mode so that (2) admits it, or remove the directory and then `DROP ROLE` the named owner role by hand (Bolt, round 7). It SHALL NOT fail the acquiring test. There is no automatic bound: a directory that can never be removed, such as one owned by another uid, is reported in every sweeping process until a human acts. *Superseded (round 7 draft):* "printed … at every sweep that meets it", which repeated within one process.

    The teardown executor takes the sweep's order for its step (ii): read the record, then drop the databases, then the serving role, then remove the directory, then drop the owner role only when the directory went. When the directory does not go, it keeps the owner role disarmed, the same way. Its step (iii) then names the owner role and the directory, so `GATE leaked-objects` fails, and the retained role is the retry handle for the lane's next sweeps. So an executor killed between removing the directory and dropping the role leaves a role that a sweep finds, never a directory with no record. *Superseded (round 7 draft):* "The teardown executor keeps R-0120-a(5)'s order (databases, roles, directory)", under which an executor killed after the role drop leaked the directory silently (Bolt, round 7). R-0120-a(5)'s "drop every database that parses to the run (`WITH (FORCE)`), then the run's two roles, then the per-run directory" is read in this order (#4363-C11). Its `run_dir` reads `removed` when it read the record and the path then gave `ENOENT`, and reads `missing` only when the run's roles existed and no record was found (Bolt L-1, round 5).

    *Superseded (round 6 draft):* removing the directory after the run's roles, the order the round-6 text implied. The record died with the owner role, so a directory that would not go was reported once and then never again (Warden M1 = Bolt M-1, round 6). *Superseded:* "Every other removal error, an identity failure included, is printed … at every sweep that meets it. It SHALL NOT fail the acquiring test." That was Bolt's round-1 condition for (a). A leftover directory blocks nothing the acquirer needs; a leftover database or role stays a sweep error under R-0120-a(5). (Anchors: P-TrustworthySignal — a red on B means B failed; V-Observability — a directory that will not go is reported at every sweep, never silent.)
  - **(4) Reach.** Warden's first objection to (a) was that one lane's sweep would reach into another lane's filesystem. One server per lane (ruling 9016) removes it. A sweep removes only runs recorded on its own lane's server, which are its own lane's runs on its own machine, and (2) refuses any directory the remover's uid does not own.
  - **Requirements on #4164**, which owns the location of the per-run directory and the controls on opening it:
    - write the record of (1) on the owner role, in the same transaction that creates the role;
    - create the directory exclusively, with a name of the form `mnemra_test_<hash8>_<pid>_<epoch>_XXXXXX` (an `mkdtemp` template), so (1)'s exact basename check can apply;
    - apply (2)'s checks at every removal, by every remover;
    - state the location's fallback on a platform without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`. Its task text names `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and states none (INHERITED from that task).

  *Rejected:*
  - *(b) the directory stays, recorded as an accepted risk.* This was the reviewers' converged choice after Bolt conceded in round 2, and the maintainer did not choose it. Its reasons are kept as the record: Warden's three, which are cross-lane reach, a locator computed from the sweeper's environment, and the weak identity check; and the small residual, since a dropped role's password authenticates nothing. The three are answered by (4), (1) and (2) above. (b)'s two trigger detectors guarded its acceptance and go with it. Its exclusive creation is kept, in (1);
  - *a locator computed from the sweeper's environment* — a per-worktree `TMPDIR`, or another `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, points elsewhere (Warden, round 1);
  - *a fixed per-lane path under the per-boot runtime directory* — #4164 names its directories with `mkdtemp`, so a fixed path would need a second naming scheme, and macOS has no `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`;
  - *a record file on disk* — its own location would need a locator.

  **(j) Age, decided by host and by the run itself (maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9046, O2: "Age sweep skips same-host runs" and "Old run refuses to continue"; Puck's option text).**
  - **The age sweep skips same-host runs.** The age sweep SHALL remove only names whose `<hash8>` differs from the sweeper's. A same-hash name of any age is decided by sweep (b)'s liveness alone:
    - dead or reused: removed;
    - live: kept;
    - unverifiable: kept and reported at every sweep (Q6), and never aged out.

    #4363-C12 amends R-0120-a's age sweep for this.
  - **An old run refuses to continue.** A run's own fixture SHALL refuse to acquire, failing the first acquiring test with `run_too_old { age }`, when its run id's `<epoch>` is more than 22 h before the fixture's clock. 22 h is a code constant set below the age constant of 24 h, so a binary that acquires near the age constant is not left running past it (Bolt's margin, round 2).

  (Anchors: ruling 9046; V-Reliability — a suspended run is neither removed by its own lane nor continued past a bound; P-TrustworthySignal — a run past the bound fails as itself, by name.) *Rejected: (a) keep `AR-0016` as amended, parked on its cadence* — not chosen; it left Q5's kill of a live same-host run in place.

  *Rejected (the evidence design):*
  - *seeded objects as the only evidence* (R-0120-a's existing form) — a seeded name has no live process behind it, so Q2 is never evaluated against a real pid and start epoch, and no executor runs beside a live run;
  - *survival checked by name* — it passes a role that was dropped and recreated;
  - *A and B on different `<hash8>` values in the test* — B is then kept by Q4, and Q2 goes untested;
  - *running E1 inside `PG_TEST_FLAGS`* — it would run three times over, under coverage too, where its holders are slower and write their own profile data.

  *Superseded (round 2):*
  - *E2 as the only evidence*, rejected because E2 runs once — E2 itself is now superseded ((f));
  - the cross-lane cases of (a): "two worktrees of this repository on one machine, pointed at one server by one harness URL", "A runs the gated superuser cell of R-0120-a(1) while B acquires", and the out-of-range bullet "anything done deliberately by a process that holds the shared harness credential, which `AR-0016` places among mutually trusted lanes". Ruling 9016 ends cross-lane sharing, the gated cell now runs on its own lane's server under (d), and the deliberate-action bullet read literally disclaimed every specified test action (Warden N1, round 2), so it now names actions outside R-0120's specified behaviour.

  **What the sweep does to each kind of run.** This restates sweep (b) and the age sweep case by case, read with (b) above. `<now>` is the fixture's clock.
  - **(Q1)** Same `<hash8>`, `<pid>` dead, `<epoch>` under 24 h before `<now>`: removed by sweep (b). This is a lane's own run whose process group was killed.
  - **(Q2)** Same `<hash8>`, `<pid>` live, with its start epoch within the tolerance of `<epoch>`: kept, whatever its age ((j)). This is a live concurrent run of the lane. *Superseded (round 6 draft):* "under 24 h old".
  - **(Q3)** Same `<hash8>`, `<pid>` reused: removed by sweep (b).
  - **(Q4)** A different `<hash8>`, `<epoch>` under 24 h old, whatever the pid: kept, because the sweep cannot see that hash's pids. On a lane's own server, this is a run from before a reboot, since the boot id is in the hash, or a host configured onto the wrong server; the lane claim of (a) refuses the second case at its acquisition.
  - **(Q5)** A different `<hash8>`, `<epoch>` more than 24 h before `<now>`: removed by the age sweep. A same-hash name of any age is decided by Q1 to Q3 and Q6 ((j)). *Superseded (round 6 draft):* "Any `<hash8>`, `<epoch>` more than 24 h before `<now>`: removed by the age sweep, including a run whose process is live … open at (h) O2". Ruling 9046 made the age sweep skip same-host runs.
  - **(Q6)** Same `<hash8>`, `<pid>` unverifiable: kept and reported.

  *(Binary-observable — E1.* Everything runs on the lane's own server, with B held throughout. B's first snapshot, B0, is taken after B acquires and before any A starts, and every later snapshot of B is compared with B0. The `<hash8>` field of every A's run id equals B's; a differing hash voids the row. The rows:
  - **A's sweep with B live:** an A that acquires while B holds leaves B's snapshot equal to B0.
  - **A fails:** an A that acquires and then exits non-zero leaves B's snapshot equal to B0. Afterwards no database or role parses to A's run and A's per-run directory does not exist. The positive control: a snapshot of A taken after its acquisition named at least one database, both roles and the directory.
  - **A's teardown executor:** A is a holding helper started with a run id that names E1's own process. That process is live and is the executor's ancestor, so no sweep of the lane removes A's objects (Q2), and the executor's binding admits the id. A is killed with `SIGKILL` once `pg_stat_activity` shows a session on its database, then reaped, and then the teardown executor runs for A's run. B's snapshot stays equal to B0. The executor exits `0` with one `teardown_report` that names A's database under `leftover`, names nothing under `vanished`, and shows `run_dir: removed`. Afterwards no database or role parses to A's run. The positive control: the report named A's database under `leftover`, so the executor evaluated A's run, and no other remover could reach A's objects first. *Superseded (round 5 draft):* "`run_dir: removed` shows the executor evaluated A's run, because no sweep removes a per-run directory (O1)". Under ruling 9046 a sweep does remove it. *Superseded (round 3 draft):* "A's database was evaluated: it is named under `leftover`, or a concurrent remover's `fixture_sweep` event names it under `removed` or `already_gone`". A concurrent remover's event is on another process's stderr, which E1 cannot read (Bolt H-1, round 3). *Superseded (round 2):* the row required A's database under `leftover`, which a concurrent run of the lane sweeping it first defeats on a correct tree (Warden N3, round 2).
  - **The next sweep after a kill:** an A is killed with `SIGKILL` once its session shows, then reaped, with no teardown run for it, and then a fresh holding helper C acquires. Afterwards no database or role parses to A's run (Q1), A's per-run directory does not exist ((i)), and B's snapshot equals B0 (Q2). The positive control: a snapshot of A taken before the kill named its database, both roles and its directory. *Superseded (round 5 draft):* "E1 then removes A's per-run directory itself, through the locator it uses for snapshots, since no sweep removes it (O1)". Under ruling 9046 the sweep removes it. *Superseded (round 3 draft):* "C's `fixture_sweep` event names A's objects under `removed`". A concurrent run of the lane can sweep them first, and its event is not C's.
  - **The gate's own run:** the run the recipe exported, whose roles exist because E1 acquired, keeps the same two role `oid`s across C's acquisition. So C's sweep has evaluated Q2 over a run id the recipe produced, not only over one the fixture generated.
  - **The sweep cases, seeded:** the seeds are placed before C acquires. Every seed's `<pid>` and `<epoch>` fields are derived from the instance's own run id, so two instances never seed the same name (Bolt L-4, round 4). Each is a database named in the full grammar of R-0120-a(3), created `OWNER` a seeded `…_owner` role of the same seeded run on which the harness holds `SET`:
    - the seeded run `<B's hash8>_<B's pid>_<B's epoch − 3600>` is gone after C acquires (Q3), while B's snapshot equals B0;
    - a seeded run whose `<hash8>` differs from B's, with an epoch 23 h before `<now>`, is kept (Q4), and E1 drops that seed itself before it exits;
    - a seeded run with the same differing `<hash8>` and an epoch 25 h before `<now>` is gone (Q5, the age branch isolated from the liveness branch).
  - **Identity mismatch:** a database in the full grammar of a dead same-hash run, owned by the harness, survives C's sweep, and C's output names that seed's own name under `identity_mismatch` exactly once. E1 drops that seed itself before it exits. *Superseded (round 2):* "appears exactly once as `identity_mismatch` in C's output", which another run's seed on the lane's server makes false, and an undropped seed would be reported at every later sweep (Warden N3, round 2).
  - **The directory record ((i)(1)):** a held run's owner role carries a comment `mnemra_test_run_dir=<path>;dev=<device>;ino=<inode>` whose path, device and inode match its per-run directory.
  - **Directory identity ((i)(2)), on seeded dead same-hash runs, each with a record:** each of the following is not removed. C's output names it with `sweep_dir_identity_mismatch` and the check. That run's databases and serving role are removed, and its owner role is kept, `NOLOGIN` and with no password:
    - a recorded directory at mode `0755`;
    - a recorded path that is a symlink to a directory, whose target is left untouched;
    - a replaced directory, seeded deterministically: the original is renamed aside, the replacement is created at the recorded path while the original still exists, and the seed asserts their inode numbers differ, retrying a bounded number of times and failing as an apparatus fault if they never do. The original is deleted after the sweep (Bolt M-3, round 5);
    - a record whose path is relative, or holds a `..` component;
    - a forged record naming a live run's directory, which is refused on the basename, because its run fields are not the dead run's;
    - a basename that contains the run's fields but is not exactly `mnemra_test_<run>_<six characters>`, such as one with a prefix or a trailing component.

    E1 removes those seeds itself afterwards. **Residual, named:** an E1 that crashes before that cleanup leaves its seeds' owner roles retained, and every later sweeping process reports them, with the remedy, until someone removes them by hand. That is loud, not silent, and it needs a crash (Bolt, round 7).
  - **Directory errors ((i)(3)), over an injected filesystem:** the removal function given `ENOENT` counts the directory removed, and the owner role is dropped. Given `EACCES` from `unlinkat`, it prints `sweep_dir_not_removed` naming the run, keeps the owner role, and the acquiring test passes.
  - **The second sweep ((i)(3)):** after one sweep has kept a seeded run's owner role for an identity mismatch, a second fixture start reports the same run again with `sweep_dir_identity_mismatch`. Once the seed's directory is put right (mode `0700`), the next fixture start leaves neither the directory nor the owner role. The assertion is on that outcome: a concurrent E1 instance's sweep may remove them first, and its report is accepted, as in the executor row (Warden R7-3, round 7). A seeded run whose owner role exists with its comment set to `NULL` is reported `sweep_dir_unrecorded` at both of two sweeps, and its role is kept.
  - **Write-side validation ((i)(1)):** given a per-run directory path containing `$`, `$$`, a space, `'`, `;`, `=`, a non-ASCII character, or a `..` segment, the fixture fails acquisition with `run_dir_path_invalid` and writes no record. A valid path's record round-trips byte for byte through `shobj_description`.
  - **Disarm failure ((i)(3)), over an injected statement runner:** a failing `ALTER ROLE … NOLOGIN PASSWORD NULL` is reported `sweep_role_not_disarmed`, the role is kept, and the next sweep retries the disarm.
  - **Once per process ((i)(3)):** a binary that acquires three times prints a retained run's report once; a second binary prints it once too.
  - **The executor's order ((i)(3)):** with an injected filesystem failing the directory's removal, the executor keeps the owner role disarmed, its report names the role and the directory under `remaining`, and the next sweep reports the run.
  - **The executor's `run_dir` (Bolt L-1):** for a run whose record was read and whose directory a sweep had already removed, the executor's report shows `run_dir: removed`. For a run whose roles exist with no record, it shows `run_dir: missing`.
  - **Age ((j)), over injected facts and an injected clock:**
    - the acquisition decision refuses with `run_too_old` when the run's `<epoch>` is 22 h and 1 s before the clock, and admits at 21 h 59 min;
    - the sweep decision keeps a same-hash run that is live and 25 h old;
    - it removes a same-hash run that is dead and 25 h old, by sweep (b);
    - it removes a different-hash name 25 h old, by the age sweep;
    - it keeps a same-hash run that is unverifiable and 25 h old, and reports it.
  - **Two instances:** E1's own binary runs its rows twice at once. The gate starts one instance, and that instance starts a second copy of itself, with its own holders and a run id the run-id function generates for that copy, as the holder vehicle does. Both pass. Every row's positive control is read from the server or the filesystem, never from another process's output, so the rows hold whether or not E1 is the lane's only sweeper. *Superseded (round 3 draft):* "two `just ci` runs in one worktree, each running E1 concurrently on the lane's server, both pass". That is two full chains, the shape (f) rejects (Bolt H-1, round 3).
  - **A bound run id:** a holding helper started with B's run id exported is refused with `run_id_unbound`, and B's snapshot equals B0. The teardown executor run for B's run while B is live exits non-zero before any drop, and B's snapshot equals B0.
  - **The liveness decision, over injected process facts:**
    - `ESRCH` reads dead;
    - a zombie reads dead;
    - a start epoch 4 s from `<epoch>` reads live;
    - one 6 s from it reads reused;
    - `EPERM` reads unverifiable;
    - an unreadable start time reads unverifiable;
    - a sweep given an unverifiable run keeps its objects and prints `sweep_kept_unverifiable` naming it.
    - the binding check, given a run id whose `<pid>` is the acquirer's own and whose `<epoch>` is 6 s from the value it reads, refuses with `run_id_unbound`, and at 4 s it admits.
    - the binding check, given a run id whose `<hash8>` differs by one character from the hash the acquirer computes, refuses with `run_id_unbound`;
    - the executor's binding, given a non-ancestor owner that reads unverifiable, refuses before any drop.
  - **The drop outcomes, over an injected statement runner:**
    - `3D000` on a database counts removed;
    - `42704` on a role counts removed;
    - `55006` once, then success, counts removed after one retry;
    - `55006` four times is a sweep error after 3 retries;
    - `40P01` once is retried;
    - `42501` is a sweep error with no retry;
    - `2BP01` is a sweep error with no retry.
    - On the server, the positive control: a drop made to fail by revoking the harness's privilege prints `fixture_teardown_failed` naming the object, and fails the acquiring test.
  - **Executor step (ii), over injected liveness:** with the owner live, `3D000` names the object under `vanished`. With the owner dead, it counts removed.
  - **The holder's bounds:** a holder whose standard input is closed exits without being released, and a holder never released exits at its deadline. In both cases its exit hook removes its run's objects.
  - **B passes:** B, released last, runs its query and exits `0`. Afterwards no database or role parses to B's run, and B's per-run directory does not exist.
  - **No new exception:** the change that adds E1 adds no entry to the R-0120-f exception list, and the R-0120-f guard passes on it.
  - **The gate itself:** E1's gate is reachable from every chain of tier-2 R-0033(4)'s derived chain set. Run with `MNEMRA_TEST_RUN_ID` unset, or with `MNEMRA_TEST_TEARDOWN_EXECUTOR` unset, E1 fails naming the variable and does not pass.)*


  *(Binary-observable — (d).*
  - Given each facts record that #4363-C6 and #4363-C9 name, the decision functions of R-0120-a(6) and (8) return the reason the row names.
  - The facts read on the server for a harness URL naming the bootstrap superuser carry `rolsuper`, `rolbypassrls` and `rolreplication`, each true.
  - The facts read for the harness after self-granting carry `MEMBER` true for both run roles.
  - The store guard admits a fresh lane server holding only `template0`, `template1`, `postgres` and `mnemra_test_template`.
  - An unmodified gated cell prints `shared_roles_unchanged`, also when a holding helper of the same lane acquires and is swept while the cell runs.
  - The detector's comparison, shown over two records, fails naming the difference when they differ only in the harness's connection limit, and again when they differ only in its password verifier. It fails naming `datallowconn` when they differ only in the template's `datallowconn`. It fails naming the superuser's `role` setting when they differ only in the superuser's role-level `role` setting. When they differ only in the password verifier, its output names `rolpassword` and contains no `SCRAM-SHA-256$` substring. It passes when they differ only by a membership in a run-grammar role. *Superseded (round 2):* "A seeded test in the gated cell that runs `ALTER ROLE <harness> CONNECTION LIMIT 5` and does not revert it makes the cell fail". On the lane's server that seed would cap every concurrent run's harness connections until someone reverted it (Warden N4, round 2).)*

  *(Binary-observable — (a).*
  - The claim decision admits a comment naming the acquirer's lane id. It refuses one naming another lane id with `server_lane_mismatch`, and refuses no comment, or `mnemra_test_lane=` with nothing after it, with `server_lane_unclaimed`.
  - In CI, on the job's own container: a step sets the claim to a seeded other lane id as superuser, runs one Postgres test binary and the teardown executor, then restores the claim in a step marked `if: always()`. Both steps reach the container with `docker exec … psql`, so no superuser credential enters the job environment (R-0120-c(1)). The binary fails naming `server_lane_mismatch`, with the `pg_database` and `pg_roles` row counts equal before and after. The executor exits non-zero before its first query of step (i). The chain that follows passes, which is the positive control.
  - On a lane's server, `COMMENT ON DATABASE mnemra_test_template IS 'x'` issued as the harness fails, and the comment is unchanged.
  - A comment `mnemra_test_lane=<acquirer's id> ` (with a trailing space) reads `server_lane_unclaimed`. Each refusal message names the expected id and the setup block.
  - The setup step run against a server claimed for another lane refuses, naming both ids, and leaves the claim unchanged. With `-v reclaim=1` set by hand, it overwrites the claim.
  - The lane-id function refuses declarations naming `../x`, `a'b` and `Impl`, each with `lane_id_invalid` and before any connection.
  - It refuses an absent declaration with `lane_undeclared`, whose message contains `just test-lane declare`.
  - It refuses a declaration copied byte for byte from another worktree with `lane_declaration_foreign`.
  - It refuses a symlink at `.mnemra-test-lane` with `lane_declaration_invalid`.
  - `just test-lane declare design` followed by `just test-lane show` prints `design`.
  - The gated recipe refuses, naming the path, a symlink at either level of the superuser location, a symlink at its `url` file, and either directory level that is not `0700` and owned by the test uid.
  - In the repository, `git -c core.excludesFile=/dev/null check-ignore -v .mnemra-test-lane` reports a match whose source is the root `.gitignore` and whose pattern does not begin with `!`, and `git ls-files .mnemra-test-lane` lists nothing. The positive controls: the same check fails in a temporary repository whose `.gitignore` lacks the entry while a global ignore file lists it, and fails in one whose root `.gitignore` holds `.mnemra-test-lane` followed by `!.mnemra-test-lane`. In that same temporary repository, `just test-lane declare design` refuses and writes no file.
  - `cargo tree` for the lane tool's package lists no `mnemra-host`, `sqlx` or `wasmtime`.
  - In an undeclared worktree, `just ci` exits non-zero with `lane_undeclared` before any `GATE` line and before the executor is built.
  - The setup block run in an undeclared worktree exits non-zero and writes no claim; the template's comment is unchanged.
  - No workflow or `justfile` line sets the `reclaim` variable.
  - `lane_declaration_foreign` prints `just test-lane declare <the declared name>`.)*

  [maintainer ruling 2026-10-28, #4363 activity 9016 ("Separate server per lane") for the direction; #3575 activity 8909 ("Test two lanes in #4161") for testing concurrent runs, which within one lane is what remains of it; `AR-0016` as amended bounds the range; P-GuaranteeByMechanism — the lane claim enforces one server per lane, and E1 makes the within-lane guarantee a standing test, because a cross-run drop shows only as another run's red attributed to the wrong cause; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — identity on a hit (the snapshot, the claim keyed on the lane's id), a positive control on every zero, and each check keyed on the branch it claims (Q4 and Q5 seeded under another hash); V-Reliability — a live run's correctness when another process fails or is killed is designed and measured, never assumed; P-TestableByDesign — the named seams are the drop function's injected statement runner, the injected process facts, the clock (the `now` input of the age and refusal decisions), and the filesystem trait the removal function takes (i)(2). Each is a parameter of a pure decision or removal function, not a `test-hooks` seam, so none needs a `no_test_seams` fixture; P-TrustworthySignal — already gone counts as removed, and the detector is keyed on what legitimate concurrency does not move]

### R-0121 — Durability across host restart against the operator's instance: the invariant, what the host verifies, and the restart acceptance (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018; anchors the brief's Success criterion "Durable across restart", [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md) §Amendment 2026-10-19, the [overview](../src/architecture/overview.md) Reliability / durability axis; supersedes R-0116-a and R-0116-h)

*The maintainer's 2026-10-18 correction (activity 7472, verbatim: "data surviving restarts was our problem, if an embedded postgres didn't use a data directory that would be have been a non-starter for such a thing") fixes the framing: the persistence defect (#3565) was mnemra's own `.temporary(true)` (MEASURED `libs/mnemra-host/storage/postgres/engine.rs:617` at `57cfeca`), not a property of embedding. Its fix under this amendment is a **correction** — the host no longer owns any data directory, temporary or otherwise — and is never a benefit of the move. The durability requirement itself stays in the core: the invariant below, and R-0117-d / R-0117-j for the half that is now the instance's. Keystone anchors: **V-Reliability**; **P-CheckCoversTheClaim** — the host claims exactly what its acceptance restarts and its check verifies, and names what it relies on Postgres for.*

- R-0121-a (the invariant): Every write acknowledged to a caller — an `artifact.create` / `artifact.update` / `artifact.delete` host-fn, or the MCP verb wrapping it, that returned success — before the host stops SHALL be reflected identically after the host next starts against the same instance and database, for a clean stop (SIGTERM, graceful drain; stdin EOF counts as a clean stop, resolving the R-0116 plan's SG-15) and for a hard kill of the host process (SIGKILL). "Reflected identically" is R-0116-a's definition, carried: `frontmatter` semantically equal as parsed JSON, `body` byte-equal, each acknowledged delete absent, per-table row count under the workspace unchanged; an unacknowledged in-flight write MAY be present or absent. A write that cannot be durably committed SHALL NOT be acknowledged: the dispatch surfaces the failure as R-0004-a `outcome = "error"`. *(Binary-observable: the two restart scenarios below.)* [brief Success criteria "Durable across restart"; V-Reliability]
- R-0121-b (what the host relies on Postgres for, named): Survival of an acknowledged write across a crash or restart of the **instance** itself is PostgreSQL's WAL guarantee under the settings R-0117-d verifies and the storage placement R-0117-j names; the host neither tests nor re-implements it. The host's contribution to that guarantee is the check: a drift in any R-0117-d setting takes readiness false within one interval, so the host never acknowledges a write under weakened durability for longer than that interval. The embedded design's "hard kill of the storage engine" scenario is therefore **dropped from the host's acceptance with reason**, not parked: the host cannot kill a process it does not own, and a claim about Postgres's crash recovery is Postgres's to make. *(Binary-observable: the R-0117-d drift scenario; the deployment document states this reliance in the operator obligations section.)* [P-CheckCoversTheClaim; V-Honesty — the reliance is named, not implied]
- R-0121-c (restart acceptance placement): The restart acceptance SHALL be out-of-process in the `verify-smoke` gate (`libs/mnemra-host/tests/smoke_e2e.rs`): spawn the production binary with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` pointing at a fixture-provisioned database as the run-scoped serving role (R-0120-e), write through MCP stdio, stop the child by the scenario's method, spawn it again against the same URL, read back over MCP. It constructs no engine and no server in test code. The harness reads SQL observables (`SHOW` values, row counts) over `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` against the same database. *(Binary-observable: the scenarios pass under `just verify-smoke`; the harness's SQL session and the child's connection resolve to the same `current_database()`.)* [P-TestableByDesign; tier-2 R-0033 partition unchanged]
- R-0121-d (mid-write loss of the instance): With a verb's write in flight, loss of the instance connection SHALL leave the write **unacknowledged** (`storage_unavailable`, R-0118-m), and after the instance returns the host SHALL serve again without a restart; the write is either wholly present or wholly absent (Postgres transaction atomicity), never partial. The harness cannot kill the operator's instance in CI, so this scenario SHALL use the **fake-server or connection-cut seam** (a `test-hooks` injection that closes the pool's connections mid-query), with the real-instance half covered by R-0117-d's settings check and R-0121-b's named reliance. *(Binary-observable: the mid-write kill scenario below.)* [V-Reliability; P-CheckCoversTheClaim — the claim is scoped to what the seam can exercise; Bolt M3]

## Out of Scope

The following are explicitly outside the `0.1.0` substrate increment scope. An implementing developer SHALL NOT build these in the `0.1.0` increment.

- **RLS policy enforcement (Postgres `CREATE POLICY` statements)** — deferred to V0.1+ as additive `CREATE POLICY` additions per accepted risk `R-0001`; the column-shape ships, the policies do not.
- **HSM-backed or runtime-fetch signing key custody** — deferred to `{{P-SigningKeyCustodyHardening}}` (Tier-C ADR, not yet authored); activated by the multi-deployment trip-wire in [P-0005](../src/adrs/P-0005-v0-signing-chain.md).
- **Increment-to-plugin mapping ratification** — the assignment of `0.2.0`–`0.14.0` capability families to the four `core: true` plugins is proposed in [P-0002](../src/adrs/P-0002-core-plugin-partition.md) and flagged for maintainer ratification before the `0.2.0` dispatch; it is not a deliverable of this `0.1.0` spec.
- **Capability family implementations** (`0.2.0` task management through `0.14.0` content-corpus migration) — the substrate provides the ABI, storage, auth, and runtime; actual plugin code for capability families is out of scope for `0.1.0`.
- **Domain / non-CRUD verb export ABI and the dynamic-resolution mechanism** (per [P-0013](../src/adrs/P-0013-plugin-invocation-model.md)) — V0 ships the fixed typed `content` CRUD export interface only (R-0019). Domain / non-CRUD verbs (e.g., `echo.audit`) and the load-time dynamic verb→export resolution mechanism they would need (a runtime registry mapping a manifest verb-name to a typed export handle — a *different* mechanism from V0's static fixed-interface `wit_bindgen`) are deferred past V0; the deferred ABI will be typed too when designed (no string hatch). *(Named tripwire, `P-Defer`: the first domain / non-CRUD verb that must dispatch at the V0 surface — a required verb the fixed `content` CRUD interface cannot express — fires the design of the deferred export ABI and its dynamic-resolution mechanism. A preference for generality is not a firing condition.)*
- **Migration mechanics** (Tier-B ADRs: `{{P-MigrationID}}`, `{{P-FKPreservation}}`, `{{P-BackupRestore}}`, `{{P-CutoverDualWrite}}`) — deferred to migration-increment briefs; the migration handler builtin's execution logic is not in `0.1.0` scope.
- **Tier-C operational hardening** (`{{P-PostgresExtDeploy}}`, `{{P-MCPWriteSemantics}}`, `{{P-SigningKeyCustodyHardening}}`, `{{P-AdminCLIDiscipline}}`) — deferred per [P-0005](../src/adrs/P-0005-v0-signing-chain.md) `P-Defer` principle.
- **Cross-tier `{{P-ProjectionRebuild}}` detailed semantics** — cross-substrate rebuild ordering and refresh-queue dependency tracking is a follow-up Frame extension when the first cross-substrate projection lands.
- **Status-churn write-amplification numeric budget** — the C1 write-amplification weakness under high status-flip rates is known; a numeric model and potential C2-influenced partial evolution are deferred to V0.1+ when dogfood data accumulates.
- **R-0001-g forward-compat Acceptance Criteria expansion** — the AC for the three V0.1 promotion paths (full-text, graph-edge, multiple embedding columns) is flagged in [P-0001](../src/adrs/P-0001-storage-layout.md) as a follow-up; the `0.1.0` AC covers the single pgvector smoke test only.
- **Streamable-HTTP MCP transport** — V0.1+ activation conditional on the microVM-appliance trip-wire.
- **External authorization server integration (OIDC AS federation)** — V0.1+ scope per accepted risk `R-0002`; the static admin token is the V0 auth path.
- **Third-party plugin install** — V0.1+ scope per product brief idea tier D11; the runtime rejects non-`core` plugins at V0.
- **Hot-reload / admin-triggered plugin reload** — V0.1+ scope. Plugin instances are only created at host startup from the fixed `core: true` set. No post-startup plugin loading path exists at V0. Scenario 6 (signing verification failure) is complete for V0 scope: it covers the startup load path only.
- **Per-plugin scope extension** (beyond the binary admin/read-observer roles) — V0.1+ scope per [P-0009](../src/adrs/P-0009-rls-admin-token.md); fine-grained per-plugin scopes are not in V0.
- **Adaptive plugin pool sizing** — V0.1+ scope; V0 uses fixed pool size 3–5.
- **Saga coordination / cross-plugin atomicity** — not V0; the day-1 contract is "no cross-plugin atomicity, ever; design for partial failure."
- **UI behavior** — no user interface at V0 substrate level; UI is post-V0.
- **`get_context_for(artifact_id)` retrieval verb** — V0.1 scope (`1.1.0`), not substrate.
- **Ongoing ingest pipeline** (watchers, scheduled polls, webhooks) — V0.1 scope (`1.2.0`); `0.1.0` covers only the substrate plumbing, not continuous ingest.
- **Artifact-list pagination — out-of-scope mechanisms** (per R-0020 / the [paging Frame](../intent/artifact-list-paging-frame.md)) — the keyset (cursor) pagination added in R-0020 SHALL NOT introduce: **(1) offset / page-number pagination** (offset degrades on large append-only tables and is non-resumable across concurrent inserts; keyset is the locked mechanism per R-0020-a — this is the recorded Alternatives-Considered rejection, so no standalone P-ADR is warranted); **(2) a `COUNT` / total-count surface** (`has-more` is derived by fetch-one-extra per R-0020-a; no total is computed or returned); **(3) any schema or index change** (the keyset reuses the existing ULID-text PK index; R-0001-d's four expression indexes are unchanged and no new index is added); **(4) any change to `filters` semantics** (the keyset `id > $cursor` predicate ANDs with the existing `filters` predicate per R-0020-a; filter parsing/semantics are unchanged); and **(5) RLS policy activation** (the keyset SELECT continues to satisfy the existing R-0006-d application-layer `workspace_id` WHERE-clause — RLS policy objects remain deferred per the **RLS policy enforcement** item above and the accepted-risk `R-0001` trip-wire; this amendment does not fire it). *(Tripwires where these are deferrals, `P-Defer`: a future requirement for a total-count surface, an offset/random-access path, or a non-`id` page order would each re-open the pagination design; a preference for generality is not a firing condition.)*
- **Artifact-list ids-only return — richer-return revisit** (per R-0020-b; provisionally accepted by the maintainer 2026-06-29) — the `artifact.list` / `content.list` page record narrows to **ids-only** (no inline frontmatter); a caller resolves frontmatter via `artifact.get` per id (the accepted N+1 fan-out characterized on R-0020-b). Carrying frontmatter in the page record, adding a `fields` projection, or adding a frontmatter-only `get` are **out of scope for `0.1.0`** — the ids-only shape ships as-is and is validated by dogfood data. *(Named tripwire, `P-Defer`: the ids-only return is revisited — carry frontmatter in the record / add a `fields` projection / add a frontmatter-only get — **if the V0 dogfood instrumentation on R-0020-b shows browse-by-metadata-via-`list` (list-then-N+1-`get`-for-metadata) is a common access pattern**; a preference for a richer return, absent that access-pattern evidence, is not a firing condition. Tracking task #1919.)*
- **External Postgres server as the substrate engine** (per R-0116; maintainer ruling 2026-09-15) — an operator-provisioned Postgres reached over a connection string is a future engine behind the P-0010 D5 `Storage` trait, not V0: it moves the durability problem to that server rather than solving it, and when supported, durability of stored context is that server's responsibility. *(Deferral, `P-Defer`: self-announcing — the need cannot arise unasked; an operator or the product register (the brief's `idea`-tier entry for an external Postgres server, added by maintainer ruling 2026-09-17) raises it. Decision content when it fires: connection configuration surviving restart in place of a host-owned data directory; which of R-0116-b/-c/-f/-g apply to a cluster the host does not own; and that R-0116-e's socket-only posture cannot hold for a remote server, so the credential-at-rest question this amendment dissolved returns there.)* **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: this deferral FIRED and the item is no longer out of scope — the operator-run instance IS the V0 engine (P-0010 §Amendment 2026-10-19; maintainer ruling 2026-10-18). The 2026-09-15 ruling is superseded on new evidence and kept in P-0010 as the rejected alternative with the reason it lost. The three decision-content questions are answered by R-0119 (connection configuration), the R-0116 supersession banner (none of -b/-c/-f/-g applies to a cluster the host does not own) and R-0119 (the credential at rest).)**
- **Postgres major-version upgrade of an existing store** (per R-0116-f) — the host performs no `pg_upgrade` and no dump/restore across engine majors at V0; the operator's way out is stated in the refusal itself (run the previous binary) and in the deployment documentation. *(Deferral, `P-Defer`: self-announcing and fired at the last cheap point — R-0116-f refuses to start and names the mismatch before any postmaster start, the moment an engine pin bump meets an existing store. Decision content when it fires: in-place `pg_upgrade` bundled with the binary, versus an operator-run dump/restore documented as the upgrade path, versus a one-major-back compatibility window in the engine pin.)* **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: re-based on the operator instance. A Postgres major upgrade is the operator's (`pg_upgrade` or dump/restore on their instance); the host's part is R-0117-a (refuse an unsupported major, not ready, with the supported set in the reason) and R-0117-h (refuse a schema newer than the binary). The remaining deferral is widening R-0117-a's major set beyond `16`, which is self-announcing: the operator who upgrades meets the `pg_version_unsupported` reason. Decision content when it fires: which majors the host supports concurrently, and whether pgvector's floor moves with them. The way back after a `mnemra init` on the new major is restore-from-backup, not "run the previous binary" (R-0117-h, r2).)** **(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: the remaining deferral above, "widening R-0117-a's major set beyond `16`" with its decision content "which majors the host supports concurrently, and whether pgvector's floor moves with them", is retired, not resolved by a later amendment. Which majors the host supports concurrently is now the content of R-0117-a's table, at most two, changed by a code change under R-0117-a (6) as amended; pgvector has one floor for every key (R-0117-b as amended). Support for a new upstream major stays a self-announcing deferral, as the note above states: an operator who moves to the new major meets the `pg_version_unsupported` refusal, which now lists the supported majors with their floors (R-0117-a (8) as amended). A mechanical report of a new upstream major is part of the drift workflow deferred to #4444 (R-0120-c as amended 2026-10-29). What stays out of scope is unchanged: the host performs no `pg_upgrade` and no dump/restore.)**
- **pgvector extension-version reconciliation within an engine major** — when the bundled `PGVECTOR_VERSION` pin advances, an existing store's installed `vector` extension stays at its old version (`CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` is a no-op). *(**Parked**, not deferred: no mechanism fires it. It resurfaces on the human cadence already written into `libs/mnemra-host/storage/postgres/engine.rs`'s pin-maintenance doc comment, which the hash-pin gate forces every `PGVECTOR_VERSION` bump to pass through. Decision content: `ALTER EXTENSION vector UPDATE` at startup, versus refuse-to-start on mismatch, versus ignore while pgvector keeps its within-major compatibility.)* **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: the parked item dissolves. There is no bundled pin and no host-run `ALTER EXTENSION`: the extension's version is the operator's, the host verifies a floor (R-0117-b), and a version below the floor is a not-ready reason the operator meets — self-announcing.)**
- **Multi-host or shared data directories, managed storage tiers** (per R-0116-g) — one host owns one data directory at a time. A second host on the same machine is refused by the lock; a host on another machine (the network-mount case) is refused by the holder record's foreign identity without any liveness test, so the refusal does not depend on lock semantics the mount may not provide. Failover between machines is a documented manual step (confirm the old host is stopped, delete `host.lock`). Multi-host topologies and managed storage are the brief's `idea`-tier managed/appliance entries, not substrate scope. *(Self-announcing: the refusal is the signal, and it fires on the foreign record, not on a lock.)* **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: superseded — there is no host-owned directory to own. What replaces it is OPEN, left open on the page for the maintainer (P-0010 §Amendment AE9): whether two hosts may serve one database concurrently. Nothing in R-0117 to R-0121 prevents it, and under stdio MCP each client spawns its own host, so two agent sessions for one operator are two hosts on one database — the case the R-0116 plan's SG-16 flagged and the embedded design refused with `data_dir_in_use`. Option A, allowed: the substrate is a server and concurrency is its job (transactions, RLS); cost — an audit that the host holds no in-memory state assumed exclusive (the projection refresh queue, R-0001-f, is the candidate; INFERRED, not audited). Option B, single writer: a session-scoped `pg_try_advisory_lock` on a dedicated connection, the second host not ready with `instance_in_use { holder }`; cost — a second agent session cannot work at all until the first host exits. Recommendation: A, with the audit as the implementation lane's first task; B is the embedded design's constraint carried forward by habit rather than by need. **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-19 (activities 7787–7790; P-0010 §Amendment AE9):** the maintainer ruled the **shared-service model** — one long-running mnemra service that every agent session reaches over streamable-HTTP MCP, HTTPS/TLS only, localhost included ("change the model so its acts like remote even when local. I have multiple sessions running always and wasn't aware of this restriction") — so the two-hosts question dissolves: one host serves every session. That model is the **next** amendment, tracker **#4127**, not this one; R-0010-a/-e stay as written here, and #4124's host-state audit is lower priority under it. **Interim, until #4127 lands: two hosts on one database is UNSUPPORTED at V0** (r2, Bolt M7), and the tripwire that detects it is mechanical: every instance check counts the other sessions of the serving role whose `application_name` is `mnemra-host` (`pg_stat_activity`, visible to the role for its own sessions) and, when the count is non-zero, logs `concurrent_host_detected { count }` once per transition and increments the `instance_check_total{status="concurrent"}` label — so the first real occurrence is recorded with evidence rather than noticed by someone.)**
- **Bundling or pinning changes to Postgres/pgvector; backup and restore** — unchanged by R-0116: the engine and extension pins stay as they are (P-0010), and backup/restore stays with the Tier-B `{{P-BackupRestore}}` slot above. Under R-0116-e no credential lives in the data directory, so a copy of it — including an operator's OS-level backup of the default location — carries content but no secret; what such a copy does carry (every row of every substrate table) is the exposure the `{{P-BackupRestore}}` slot already owns. **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: bundling has no object (there is nothing to bundle — the single binary is the host alone; the brief's "single-binary constrains the server, not the packaging" clause is what still holds), and the engine and extension pins exit with the embedded engine (P-0010 §Amendment, "What exits"). Backup and restore stay with `{{P-BackupRestore}}`, now against an instance the host does not own; the operator's backup obligation is R-0117-j item 2. The exposure note changes shape: an operator's Postgres backup carries every row, and the host's password file — if one exists (R-0119-b) — is a separate file under the host uid, never inside the instance's data directory.)**
- **Container image contract** (the image-side half of R-0116-i) — **parked**, reopened by a named trigger: **the first `Dockerfile` for the host landing in the tree**, which is the moment there is something real to test. Nothing is testable before that, and a locked clause with nothing to run it against is the unfireable-trip-wire shape; that is the reason for parking, not that the host-side mount check covers the container risk (it does not — it only refuses a directory that is not a mount point). Decision content recorded for that work: the image sets `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=/var/lib/mnemra` and `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT=1`; it runs as a fixed numeric non-root `USER`; the data directory's ownership shape under Kubernetes `fsGroup` (group-writable, group-owned) is refused by R-0116-b's `0700`/owner check as written and needs a ruling — relax the check under the mount requirement, or require `fsGroupChangePolicy`/an init step that restores `0700`; a Dockerfile lint under `just ci` with a seeded-failure proof is the check that would make the contract fire; and the container form of the replace-the-executable check (R-0116-j: recreate from a different image, same volume) lands with the image. **Dropped, not parked: a `VOLUME /var/lib/mnemra` declaration** — `docker run` without `-v` then gets an anonymous volume that `--rm` deletes, so the mount check would pass in exactly the case it exists to refuse; the operator's explicit mount is the only shape that is durable. **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: still parked on the same trigger — the first `Dockerfile` for the host landing in the tree — but the decision content changes: the image carries no data directory and no mount requirement (those were the embedded cluster's); it sets no `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` (the operator's), runs as a fixed numeric non-root `USER`, and the operator mounts the R-0119-b password file (or uses peer authentication over a mounted socket directory); the probes are exec probes (`mnemra health`, `mnemra ready`) per R-0118-g while the listener is loopback-only; the `fsGroup` ownership question moves from the data directory to the password file's `0600`/owner check. The persistent-storage obligation moves to the operator's Postgres deployment (R-0117-j item 1), where a `--rm`'d writable layer is the same failure in a different container. **Parked with a cadence and a wired resurfacing (r2, Charon C2):** the tracker task Puck files for the image contract is wired as a blocking dependency of the #4019 packaging decision, so the question cannot be closed without this item being dispositioned; the human cadence is each revision of this amendment and the #4019 gate.)**
- **Non-loopback bind of the `/health` + `/ready` listener (HTTP probes from an orchestrator)** *(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018)* — at V0 the listener binds `127.0.0.1` only (R-0004-g), so an orchestrator reaches liveness and readiness by exec probes (R-0118-g). **RULED 2026-10-19 (activity 7790, AE5): deferred to the HTTPS shared-service transport amendment, #4127, which decides the listener.** *(Deferral, `P-Defer`: the trigger is #4127 itself, a filed task that is the next amendment; independently, a non-loopback bind is only possible through a bind-address configuration surface this spec does not define, and the change that adds one meets R-0004-g's tripwire clause in the same text. Decision content when it fires: the detail body is served only to callers presenting a valid admin token and unauthenticated callers receive a status-code-only `200`/`503` (R-0004-g's own clause, which is exactly what an HTTP probe needs), versus a separate probe-only port with no body; and whether `/health` and `/ready` bind the same address.)*
- **A soft class and per-capability gating** *(Amended 2026-10-19 r2, #4018; maintainer ruling 2026-10-19, activity 7771)* — not built at V0: no `capability_unavailable` error, no `degraded` field, gate or test. The design for the first entrant is recorded once in R-0118-i. *(Deferral, `P-Defer` DF1, with a mechanical trigger: a migrations-suite test fails when a migration introduces the first `vector` column while R-0117-b is still hard with no capability set, which forces the classification before the column lands — the last cheap point. Decision content when it fires: R-0117-b moving to soft, gating the embedding-write and dense-retrieval capabilities, once [P-0014](../src/adrs/P-0014-retrieval-architecture.md)'s degraded-mode matrix is live, versus keeping it hard.)*
- **Password-based cluster access and credential rotation** — a **rejected alternative**, recorded rather than deferred: R-0116-e locks peer-authenticated socket-only access with no role password, so there is no cluster credential at rest and nothing to rotate. The rejected shape (loopback TCP with passwords persisted under `<data_dir>/secrets/`, rotation as a follow-up) and its reasons are carried inline on R-0116-e. If R-0116-e is ever re-opened — a remote server (the external-Postgres item above) is the case that would do it — rotation returns with it and needs a firing event of its own at that time. **(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: re-opened by exactly that case and answered by R-0119 — a password lives only in a `0600` file under the host uid, never in the URL or environment; peer authentication over a socket stays the recommended local shape with no secret at all; rotation is the operator's procedure with `SIGHUP` as the host's reload path (R-0119-b). The `<data_dir>/secrets/` shape stays rejected for the reasons on R-0116-e.)**

## Scenarios

### Scenario: Single-node V0 substrate initialization — happy path

**Given** a fresh Postgres instance with no mnemra-core schema, and the mnemra-core binary built with signed `core: true` plugin artifacts and embedded verification material  
**When** the operator runs `mnemra init`  
**Then** the system creates all substrate tables and indexes, installs the bundled `pgvector` extension against the embedded engine (no `timescaledb` — re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability); no in-app observability hypertable, retention policy, or continuous aggregate is created), creates the `default` workspace, initializes all seven builtin components, initializes the plugin pool (3–5 instances per `core: true` plugin type), verifies plugin signatures synchronously before creating any instance, emits a structured health event to the OTel/stdout surface, and responds to a `GET /health` request on the loopback-only listener with the structured detail body `{ "postgres": true, "pgvector": true, "workspace_default": true, "overall": "ok" }` (loopback IS the gate at V0 — no admin token is required on the detail body; the listener binds `127.0.0.1` only, so every caller it serves is on loopback).

### Scenario: Plugin signing chain operates on load — happy path

**Given** a `tasks` plugin manifest at `tasks/manifest.toml` declaring `schema_version = 1`, `core = true`, the valid `[signature]` section signed by the mnemra root key on the build host  
**When** the host loads the `tasks` plugin at startup  
**Then** `verify()` is called synchronously and returns `Ok`; a per-instance host-fn allowlist is compiled from the manifest's `[host_fns]` section; a pool of 3–5 `tasks` plugin instances is created; each instance is ready to service `task.*` MCP verbs.

### Scenario: MCP verb dispatches under `WorkspaceCtx` — happy path

**Given** an MCP client connected over stdio presenting a valid admin token associated with `workspace_id = W1`  
**When** the client sends a JSON-RPC request for verb `task.create` with valid arguments  
**Then** `P-builtin-auth` validates the token; a `WorkspaceCtx { workspace_id: W1, role: Admin, token_id: T1 }` is constructed at a single construction site; the `WorkspaceCtx` is passed as the first parameter to the `artifact.create` host-fn; the host derives `workspace_id` from the context and performs `INSERT INTO tasks (...) WHERE workspace_id = W1`; the response carries the new artifact's ULID; a per-verb metric record is emitted to the OTel/stdout surface for verb `"task.create"`, outcome `"ok"`, and measured `duration_ms` (not written to an in-app hypertable — re-derived per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability)).

### Scenario: MCP verb dispatches to a typed content export — happy path

**Given** a `tasks` plugin built as a component that exports the typed `content` interface (`create`/`get`/`list`/`update`/`delete`) and exports no `run`-shaped string-dispatch function, with `task.create` declared in its manifest `verbs` list, and an MCP client connected over stdio presenting a valid admin token for `workspace_id = W1`  
**When** the client sends a JSON-RPC request for verb `task.create` with valid arguments  
**Then** after `DF-auth-check` and the per-verb capability check against the manifest `verbs` list (R-0010-d), the host invokes the **typed `content.create` export** on a pooled `tasks` instance — resolved statically against the fixed `content` interface, with no runtime export-registry lookup (R-0019-a, R-0019-c) — passing the typed arguments across the WIT boundary and receiving a typed return; no string-based verb-dispatch path is traversed (R-0019-b); the response carries the new artifact's ULID. *(Companion: a manifest-declared verb with no matching typed export returns the R-0019-d structured non-dispatchable error and leaves the pre-dispatch permission outcome unchanged — covered by R-0019-e, not a separate scenario at V0 since no such verb ships.)*

### Scenario: Observability emits during a dogfood session — happy path

*(Re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability): observability is emitted, not stored in-app. No metrics/events hypertable or continuous aggregate exists; the percentile distribution is derivable from the emitted `duration_ms` values at the operator-chosen sink.)*

**Given** the substrate is initialized (no in-app observability store; the server emits to stdout/OTel)  
**When** a typical dogfood session generates 50 MCP verb calls across `task.create`, `task.update`, `task.list`, `task.get` over 1 hour, with no persistent observability backend configured  
**Then** 50 per-verb metric records are observable on the OTel/stdout emission surface, each carrying `workspace_id`, `verb`, `outcome`, `duration_ms`; `p50`/`p95`/`p99` per `(verb, workspace_id, outcome)` are derivable from the emitted `duration_ms` values for the window; `\d+` introspection shows **no** metrics/events hypertable and `\dx` does **not** list `timescaledb`; emission succeeds with no observability database present (storage-independent emission, R-0004-c/f).

### Scenario: Admin token authenticates a destructive operation — happy path

**Given** the admin token file at mode 600 with `token_value = T_ADMIN`, and a `admin_tokens` row `{ token_hash: BLAKE3(T_ADMIN), workspace_id: W1, scopes: ["admin"] }`  
**When** the operator runs `mnemra workspace list --token T_ADMIN`  
**Then** the CLI performs a hash lookup `BLAKE3(T_ADMIN)` against `admin_tokens.token_hash`; the lookup returns the row; `WorkspaceCtx { workspace_id: W1, role: Admin, token_id: <row id> }` is constructed; `workspace list` executes and returns the list of workspaces scoped to `W1`.

### Scenario: Signing verification failure — plugin SHALL NOT load

**Given** a plugin artifact whose `[signature]` section contains bytes that do not verify against the embedded mnemra root key  
**When** the host attempts to load this plugin at startup  
**Then** `verify()` returns `Err`; the plugin load is rejected with a structured error naming the plugin's `name` and `version`; no plugin instance is created; the host continues startup (it does not crash); the MCP server does not expose any verbs for the rejected plugin; the error is emitted as a structured event (destination per R-0004).

### Scenario: Resource limit breach — kill-and-replace

**Given** a `tasks` plugin instance running verb `task.list` that enters an infinite loop (pathological implementation)  
**When** the epoch-interruption deadline (5 seconds) fires  
**Then** the Wasmtime `Store` traps with an epoch deadline error; the host catches the trap; a structured event is emitted (destination per R-0004): `{ event_type: "plugin_limit_violation", payload: { workspace_id: W1, plugin_id: "tasks", plugin_version: "0.2.0", limit_type: "epoch_deadline", limit_value: 500 } }`; the pool slot is poisoned; a new `tasks` instance is created for the pool; the caller receives a structured error `{ code: "plugin_execution_timeout", plugin: "tasks", verb: "task.list" }`; the host process does NOT panic.

### Scenario: Admin token mismatch — 401

**Given** an MCP client presenting a token string `T_BOGUS` for which no matching row exists in `admin_tokens`  
**When** the client sends a JSON-RPC request for any verb  
**Then** `P-builtin-auth` performs `SELECT ... WHERE token_hash = BLAKE3(T_BOGUS)`; the query returns zero rows; the MCP handler returns JSON-RPC error code distinguishable as authentication failure (NOT verb-not-found, NOT parameter-invalid); no `WorkspaceCtx` is constructed; no host-fn is invoked; no workspace data is accessed.

### Scenario: Cross-workspace SQL leak — compile-time ABI prevention (build-time test)

**Given** a `core: true` plugin author who attempts to declare a host-fn that accepts `workspace_id: Uuid` as a write-path parameter  
**When** the author compiles the plugin against the WIT-defined host-fn interface  
**Then** the WIT-generated bindings do not include any write-path function that accepts `workspace_id` as a parameter; the compiler rejects the attempt at the type boundary; no runtime check is necessary because the ABI makes the wrong thing impossible to express.

### Scenario: Read-observer token denied write access

**Given** an admin token row with `scopes = ["read_observer"]` associated with `workspace_id W1`  
**When** an MCP client bearing this token sends a `task.create` request  
**Then** the `WorkspaceCtx { workspace_id: W1, role: ReadObserver, token_id: T2 }` is constructed; the host-fn boundary check for `artifact.create` evaluates `Role::ReadObserver` against the permission matrix; the request is denied with a structured permission error; no artifact is written; no `workspace_id` exposure outside W1 occurs.

### Scenario: Build-host key-on-disk leak — trip-wire activation criterion

**Given** the mnemra-core binary is deployed to a second deployment node beyond the maintainer's single dogfood instance  
**When** this deployment event is detected (by the operator, by the CI system, or by any monitoring mechanism)  
**Then** the multi-deployment trip-wire fires; `{{P-SigningKeyCustodyHardening}}` (Tier-C ADR) MUST be authored and locked before the second deployment proceeds; the accepted risk `R-0004` is NOT considered retired until `{{P-SigningKeyCustodyHardening}}` locks.

### Scenario: Admin CLI — schema-driven subcommand generation

**Given** a `tasks` plugin manifest that declares a verb `task.audit` in its `[verbs]` section, and the host has loaded this manifest at startup  
**When** the operator invokes `mnemra admin`  
**Then** a `task.audit` subcommand is available in the admin CLI reflecting the manifest's declared verbs; the subcommand was not hard-coded in the CLI binary; removing the verb from the manifest and restarting removes the subcommand.

### Scenario: `/health` degraded and down states

> ⛔ **SUPERSEDED (2026-10-19, #4018)** by R-0118-e/-f/-l and the "Operator-run instance" scenarios: `/health` no longer reports dependencies, the drain is R-0118-l, and `overall` has no successor field. Retained as the embedded-era record.

**Given** the mnemra-core host is running with the loopback TCP listener active  
**When** the Postgres database is unreachable (connection refused) and `GET /health` is invoked on the loopback listener  
**Then** the response body includes `{ "postgres": false, "overall": "degraded" }` if any dependency is degraded but the host is running, or `{ "overall": "down" }` if Postgres is fully unreachable and no structured detail is available; the HTTP status code is `503 Service Unavailable`.  
**And given** the host is mid-shutdown (SIGTERM received, graceful drain in progress)  
**When** `GET /health` is invoked  
**Then** the response reflects `{ "overall": "down" }` with HTTP `503`.

### Scenario: Token rotation event ordering

**Given** a valid admin token `T_OLD` with `workspace_id W1`, and a request to rotate it  
**When** `mnemra token rotate --token T_OLD` is executed  
**Then** the rotation event (type `token_rotated`, carrying `token_id` of `T_OLD`) is emitted (destination per R-0004) BEFORE the old token row is deleted from `admin_tokens`; the new token row is created; subsequent lookups against `T_OLD`'s hash return zero rows; subsequent lookups against the new token succeed.

### Scenario: Plugin fuel exhaustion mid-verb — kill-and-replace (independent of epoch breach)

**Given** a `tasks` plugin instance running verb `task.list` that consumes 10B+ fuel ticks before the epoch deadline (a pathological module constructed to consume CPU instructions without sleeping, so the epoch deadline does not fire first)  
**When** the fuel ceiling is hit  
**Then** the Wasmtime `Store` traps with a fuel-exhaustion error; the same kill-and-replace path fires as in Scenario 7 (pool slot poisoned, new instance created, caller receives structured error); the structured event uses `limit_type: "fuel"` and `limit_value: 10000000000`; the pool recovers; the host does NOT panic. Note: this scenario is tested independently of Scenario 7 — the pathological module must consume fuel without triggering the epoch deadline, confirming the fuel-ceiling path is exercised separately.

### Scenario: Artifact-list keyset pagination walks a type end-to-end — happy path

**Given** a workspace `W1` holding `N` artifacts of type `task` (with `N` greater than one page), and an MCP client connected over stdio presenting a valid admin token for `W1`  
**When** the client walks the type starting from `cursor = none`, following `next-cursor` on each call until `has-more = false` — through the full path client → guest `content.list` export → host-fn `artifact.list` → Postgres  
**Then** a finite sequence of `artifact-page` records is returned in `id`-ascending (creation) order such that: on **every** page `has-more = true` **iff** `next-cursor = some` (R-0020-b); on **every non-final page** `next-cursor = some(ids.last())` — the resume cursor equals the `id` of the last row on the page (R-0020-b); on the **final** page `has-more = false` **and** `next-cursor = none`; the concatenation of all pages' `ids` contains each of the `N` artifacts **exactly once** (no duplicates, no gaps); global order is `id`-ascending across page boundaries; the walk **terminates** (no infinite cursor loop); and the walk is exercised **through the guest `content.list` export**, not only the host-fn (the end-to-end path is the asserted surface). [Frame QA-1]

### Scenario: Artifact-list pagination DoS bounds — size cap and scan-cost timeout

**Given** the keyset read path with the host-side clamp (R-0020-c) and host-side `statement_timeout` (R-0020-d) active  
**When (A — size)** a caller requests `limit = 100_000` against a type holding more than 500 artifacts  
**Then (A)** a single bounded `artifact-page` is returned with `ids.len() <= 500` (clamp) even though the requested `limit` exceeded the cap; rows **returned** are `<= cap + 1` (fetch-one-extra; no `COUNT`); and no list path issues an unbounded (`LIMIT`-less) query — closing the result-set-*size* half of the prior unbounded-`SELECT` finding.  
**And when (B — scan cost, adversarial)** a caller issues a `filters` predicate on a **non-indexed** frontmatter field (outside the four R-0001-d expression-indexed fields) matching sparsely or zero rows, against a type holding more than `cap` artifacts — forcing `ORDER BY id LIMIT cap+1` to walk the entire `id`-btree tail  
**Then (B)** the query is **canceled by the `statement_timeout`** within the bounded budget and surfaces as a structured error — observable as the **R-0004-a metric `outcome = "timeout"`** (not `"ok"`) with a bounded wall-clock `duration_ms` — rather than completing an unbounded full-tail scan, closing the *scan-cost* half the cap leaves open. [Frame QA-2]

### Scenario: Artifact-list tenant isolation preserved across the keyset rewrite

**Given** a paginated `list` issued under workspace `A` while workspace `B` holds artifacts of the same type, with the `workspace_id = ctx.workspace_id` WHERE-clause condition retained on the keyset SELECT (R-0006-d) and the existing R-0018-d read-path lint in CI  
**When** workspace `A` paginates the type across all pages, including under a cursor value that `B`'s rows would sort into  
**Then** only workspace-`A` artifacts are ever returned, on any page, for any cursor; the existing **R-0006-d / R-0018-d read-path CI lint stays green** across the rewrite (the `workspace_id` WHERE-clause condition is present on the new keyset read path — no new mechanism is introduced); and a **cross-workspace probe** returns **zero** `B`-owned `id`s on any page. [Frame QA-3]

### Scenario: Artifact-list breaking return-type change — recompile-all-core-plugins before merge

**Given** the `artifact.list` / `content.list` return type changes from `list<string>` / `[{ id, frontmatter }]` to the `artifact-page` record — a breaking change within the `0.y.z` band  
**When** the R-0020 ABI-change PR is presented at the pre-merge gate with all `core: true` content plugins in the tree  
**Then** the breaking change lands only with all consumers migrated and green: every `core: true` plugin recompiles against the new ABI **and** passes its tests **before** the change merges (R-0017-a, asserted at the merge gate, not after); the changed host-fns carry the correct stability annotation reflecting the breaking change (R-0012-e); and the trap/replace plugin path pinned to the current export shape per P-0013 is re-pinned to the new return shape with its kill-and-replace tests staying green. [Frame QA-4]

### Scenario: Artifact-list cursor and `limit` boundary semantics

**Given** the `artifact.list` host-fn boundary validation (R-0020-e) ahead of query construction and the host-side clamp (R-0020-c/-f)  
**When** a caller supplies (a) a malformed `cursor` (not a 26-char ULID), (b) a well-formed but out-of-range `cursor` (valid ULID past the end), and (c) `limit = 0`  
**Then** each boundary input yields a bounded, defined outcome — never an unbounded query and never a leaked internal error: **(a)** the malformed `cursor` returns the **R-0010-f structured "parameter invalid" error** (not an empty page, not a raw Postgres error), and the error body contains **no DB/schema/Postgres internals** (R-0020-e no-leak posture); **(b)** the out-of-range valid-ULID `cursor` returns an **empty page** (`ids = []`, `has-more = false`, `next-cursor = none`), derivable from keyset `id > $cursor`; **(c)** `limit = 0` returns a **bounded** default-clamped page (`ids.len() <= 100`) and the emitted SQL is `LIMIT 101` — **never** a `LIMIT`-less or `LIMIT NULLIF`-zeroed query (R-0020-f), asserted against the actual emitted SQL. [Frame QA-5]

### Scenario: Artifact-list paging params excluded from telemetry

**Given** any paginated `artifact.list` / `content.list` dispatch carrying a `cursor` in and a `next-cursor` out, with the per-verb metric record (R-0004-a) and structured-log emission on the dispatch  
**When** the dispatch completes and emits its telemetry  
**Then** the emitted per-verb metric record contains `workspace_id` / `verb` / `outcome` / `duration_ms` and **does not** contain the `cursor` or `next-cursor` value (R-0004-a: no artifact IDs in the metric record), and the structured-log line for the dispatch likewise **omits** the `cursor` / `next-cursor` values — assertable by inspecting the emitted record/log (R-0020-g). [Frame QA-6]

### Scenario: Artifact-list empty type — zero artifacts

**Given** a workspace `W1` holding **zero** artifacts of type `task`  
**When** a client calls `artifact.list` for type `task` with `cursor = none`  
**Then** a single `artifact-page` is returned with `ids = []`, `has-more = false`, and `next-cursor = none` — the empty-set case satisfies the R-0020-b biconditional (`has-more = false` ∧ `next-cursor = none`); no error is raised and no `cap+1`-th probe row is implied. [R-0020-b]

### Scenario: Artifact-list exact-last-page boundary — final page exactly full

**Given** a workspace `W1` holding **exactly** `page_size × K` artifacts of type `task` (e.g., 200 at the default page size of 100), and a client walking from `cursor = none`  
**When** the client walks to the last page  
**Then** page 1 returns `ids.len() = 100` with `has-more = true` and `next-cursor = some(ids.last())`, and the 2nd (**final**) page returns `ids.len() = 100` with `has-more = false` and `next-cursor = none` — the final page is **exactly full** yet correctly reports end-of-set. This is the highest-value paging edge: it catches an `ids.len() == limit` end-of-set heuristic (which would wrongly report `has-more = true` on an exactly-full final page) in place of the R-0020-a **fetch-one-extra** mechanism. [R-0020-a, R-0020-b]

### Scenario: Artifact-list page-size cap boundary — limit 499 / 500 / 501

**Given** a workspace holding more than 501 artifacts of a type, and the host-side clamp `effective_limit = (limit == 0 ? 100 : min(limit, 500))` (R-0020-c)  
**When** a client requests `limit = 499`, then `limit = 500`, then `limit = 501`  
**Then** the effective page size is **499, 500, and 500** respectively (`min(limit, 500)`): `limit = 499` returns up to 499 ids; `limit = 500` returns up to 500 ids; `limit = 501` is **clamped** to 500 ids — every page returns `ids.len() <= 500`, and the emitted SQL `LIMIT` is `effective_limit + 1` (500 / 501 / 501) per fetch-one-extra. [R-0020-c]

### Scenario: Artifact-list foreign-but-valid cursor — zero foreign rows

**Given** workspaces `A` and `B` each holding artifacts of the same type, and a **well-formed 26-char ULID** cursor that is the `id` of one of `B`'s artifacts
