**When** workspace `A` calls `artifact.list` passing `B`'s artifact `id` as `cursor`  
**Then** the keyset `id > $cursor` predicate is ANDed with `workspace_id = ctx.workspace_id` (R-0006-d), so the returned page contains only `A`-owned ids whose `id` sorts after the cursor value — **never** any `B`-owned row; the foreign-but-valid cursor is safe (it can at most return an empty or shifted page **within `A`'s own workspace**), and the cross-workspace probe returns **zero** `B`-owned ids on any page. [R-0020-e]

### Scenario: Artifact-list concurrent insert during walk — forward-non-losing keyset

**Given** a client mid-walk of type `task` under `cursor = C`, having already emitted all ids `<= C`  
**When** new artifacts of type `task` are inserted concurrently (each created after the cursor's row, so each sorts at `id > C` under the lexicographic = chronological `id` ordering)  
**Then** the newly-inserted `id > C` rows appear on a **later** page of the same walk (the walk is **forward-non-losing**: it neither loses already-emitted rows nor duplicates them); the walk is **not a point-in-time snapshot** — it MAY include rows created after the walk began, which is the **defined** keyset behavior, not a defect. [R-0020-a]

### Scenario: Artifact-list scan-cost statement_timeout — caller-visible query_scan_timeout

**Given** the keyset read path with the host-side `statement_timeout` (R-0020-d) armed **inside an explicit transaction**, exercised with a **test-only reduced `statement_timeout`** and a seeded row count large enough to exceed it under an adversarial sparse non-indexed `filters` predicate  
**When** the read-path query exceeds the `statement_timeout` and Postgres cancels it  
**Then** the dispatch surfaces **both** the R-0004-a metric `outcome = "timeout"` (not `"ok"`) with a bounded `duration_ms` **and** a caller-facing JSON-RPC **`query_scan_timeout`** error; the error body carries **no** Postgres/schema/DB internals (R-0020-e no-leak posture); and `query_scan_timeout` is **distinct** from `plugin_execution_timeout` (guest epoch) and `-32602` (parameter invalid). This scenario guards **both** the explicit-transaction requirement (a `SET LOCAL` outside a transaction would silently disarm the backstop and this cancellation would never fire) **and** the distinct-error-code requirement. [R-0020-d]

### Scenario: Artifact-list degraded DB connectivity mid-walk — structured error, resumable

**Given** a client mid-walk of a type, having received `next-cursor = C` on the prior page  
**When** DB connectivity degrades (connection refused / dropped) on the next `artifact.list` call  
**Then** the dispatch returns a **structured error** (not a crash, panic, or hang) — the host process does not panic; once connectivity recovers, the client retries the **same call with `cursor = C`** and the walk **resumes from exactly where it left off** (keyset cursors are stateless and idempotent — there is no server-side walk state to lose). [R-0020-a]

### Scenario: Artifact-list concurrent cursor churn under pool pressure

**Given** multiple clients concurrently walking the same and different types, with the fixed 3–5-instance plugin pool (R-0016-a) under contention  
**When** the concurrent walks proceed through the guest export → host-fn → Postgres path under pool pressure  
**Then** **every** walk returns a correct, complete, duplicate-free page sequence (concurrency does not corrupt any individual walk — cursors are per-call and stateless per R-0020-a/-b), and the pool-slot wait latency under contention is **characterized** for the dogfood instrumentation (R-0016-a; the fan-out / pool-pressure validation referenced in R-0020-b). [R-0016-a, R-0020-a]

### Scenario: Artifact-list malformed cursor — invalid base32 alphabet

**Given** the `artifact.list` host-fn boundary validation (R-0020-e), which validates `cursor` as a well-formed 26-char ULID (Crockford base32 alphabet + range) **before** query construction  
**When** a client supplies a 26-character `cursor` that is the correct **length** but contains characters **outside** the Crockford base32 alphabet (e.g., `I`, `L`, `O`, `U`, or other invalid symbols)  
**Then** the host-fn rejects it with the **R-0010-f structured "parameter invalid"** error before any query is constructed — the validation is **alphabet-and-range, not a naive length check** (a 26-char wrong-alphabet string SHALL NOT pass); the error body carries **no** DB/schema/Postgres internals (R-0020-e no-leak posture). [R-0020-e]

> ⛔ **The ten R-0116 scenarios below are SUPERSEDED (2026-10-19, #4018)** with the R-0116 block; they are retained as the embedded-engine record and are not to be implemented. Their live successors are the "Operator-run instance" scenarios after them.

### Scenario: Stored context survives a clean stop and restart — happy path

**Given** the production `mnemra` binary spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` set to a fresh absolute per-run directory `D` under the recipe's `$TMPDIR`, an MCP client connected over the child's stdio with a valid admin token for `W1`, `N` artifacts of type `task` created through `task.create`, each acknowledged, and a pre-stop read set captured via `artifact.get` for every id; the harness has parsed `data_dir_resolved { initialized: "first_run", socket_dir: S }` from the child's stdout and connects over `S` as the superuser by peer authentication  
**When** the host receives SIGTERM, drains, stops its postmaster and exits, and the same binary is spawned again with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then** the second start emits `data_dir_resolved { path: D, source: "env", initialized: "existing", socket_dir: S }`, does **not** run `initdb` (`SELECT system_identifier FROM pg_control_system()` returns the first run's value), `D/initialized` is present, and it serves; `artifact.get` for each of the `N` ids returns `frontmatter` semantically equal and `body` byte-equal to the pre-stop read set; the `task` row count under `W1` equals `N`; on the harness's socket session `SHOW fsync`, `SHOW synchronous_commit` and `SHOW full_page_writes` return `on`, `SHOW data_sync_retry` returns `off`, `SHOW listen_addresses` returns the empty string, and `SELECT count(*) FROM pg_authid WHERE rolpassword IS NOT NULL` returns `0`; the postmaster's argv contains `-D D/pg` and no `-F`. [R-0116-a/-b/-c/-d/-e/-h]

### Scenario: Stored context survives a hard kill — host SIGKILL with an orphaned postmaster, and postmaster SIGKILL with a stale `postmaster.pid`

**Given** the same out-of-process setup as the clean-stop scenario, with `N` acknowledged `task.create` writes and a captured pre-kill read set  
**When (A — host killed)** the host process receives SIGKILL, leaving the `pg_ctl`-daemonized postmaster alive against `D/pg` (observable: the pid on line 1 of `D/pg/postmaster.pid` is a live process owned by the host uid with `-D D/pg` in its argv), and the binary is spawned again with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then (A)** the second host takes `D/host.lock`, classifies the previous holder as same-boot and dead, finds the orphaned postmaster live under the R-0116-g definition, stops it with a fast shutdown, starts its own, and serves; afterwards exactly one `postgres` process runs with `-D D/pg` in its argv; every acknowledged pre-kill write reads back equal; `system_identifier` is unchanged.  
**And when (B — postmaster killed)** with the host alive, the postmaster (pid from line 1 of `D/pg/postmaster.pid`) receives SIGKILL, leaving a stale `postmaster.pid` naming a dead pid; the host is then stopped and the binary is spawned again with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then (B)** the second host proceeds past the stale file (no `data_dir_in_use`, no `data_dir_postmaster_ambiguous`, no re-`initdb`), Postgres runs WAL crash recovery, and every write acknowledged before the kill reads back equal; a write in flight at the kill that was never acknowledged MAY be absent, and its absence is not a failure. [R-0116-a/-c/-d/-g/-h]

### Scenario: Engine major-version mismatch on an existing store — host refuses to start

**Given** a data directory `D` holding a completed store (`D/initialized` present) whose `D/pg/PG_VERSION` has been rewritten by the test, a byte-level snapshot of `D`, and the engine pin's major of `16`  
**When (A)** `PG_VERSION` reads `15` and the binary is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then (A)** before any postmaster start attempt the host exits non-zero with `data_dir_version_mismatch { data_dir: D, found: 15, engine_major: 16, guidance: <one sentence naming the previous binary> }` — the host's own error; no process ever ran with `-D D/pg` in its argv (`/health` may have answered `down` or `degraded` before the refusal — it is the first API and binds before the store is opened); a byte-level comparison of `D` against the snapshot shows no change; no new `PG_VERSION` file exists under `D` or the default data location.  
**And when (B)** `PG_VERSION` reads `garbage`  
**Then (B)** the same error with `found: null`, and `mnemra --version` run separately prints `engine_major: 16`. [R-0116-f]

### Scenario: Interrupted first run — `pg/` present without the completion marker

**Given** a data directory `D` in which `D/pg` exists (the test copies a cluster in, or kills a first run between `initdb` and the marker write) and `D/initialized` is absent, with a byte-level snapshot of `D`  
**When** the binary is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then** the host exits non-zero with `data_dir_incomplete_init { data_dir: D, guidance: <remove the directory and start again> }`; no `initdb` ran; `D` is byte-identical to the snapshot; and after the operator removes `D`, the next start is a clean first run that ends with `D/initialized` present. [R-0116-c]

### Scenario: Second host on a data directory a live host owns — refused, first host undisturbed

**Given** a host running against `D` holding `D/host.lock`, with `N` acknowledged writes, and a second instance of the binary spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**When** the second instance starts  
**Then** it exits non-zero with `data_dir_in_use { data_dir: D, holder: <the first host's record> }` without stopping, signalling or connecting to the first host's postmaster; the first host keeps serving and its `N` writes still read back; exactly one postmaster runs with `-D D/pg`; `D/host.lock` still exists. [R-0116-g]

### Scenario: Stale ownership state — a foreign holder record, and a reused pid on the same boot

**Given** a completed store `D` with no host running against it  
**When (A — foreign holder)** the test writes a `D/host.lock` record with `hostname: "other-host"` and a `boot_id` that differs from the machine's, naming a pid that happens to be live locally, and spawns the binary with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then (A)** the host exits with `data_dir_in_use` naming that holder, performs no liveness test and sends no signal to any process (the local process with that pid is still running afterwards), and `D/host.lock` is unchanged.  
**And when (B — reused pid)** the test writes a `D/host.lock` record with this machine's hostname and current `boot_id`, then writes `D/pg/postmaster.pid` whose line 1 is the pid of a `sleep 600` process it started (owned by the host uid, but with no `-D D/pg` in its argv), and spawns the binary  
**Then (B)** the host exits with `data_dir_postmaster_ambiguous { data_dir: D, pid, guidance }`, the `sleep` process is still running afterwards, and nothing under `D/pg` changed.  
**And when (C — earlier boot)** the test writes a `D/host.lock` record with this machine's hostname and a different `boot_id`, leaves a `D/pg/postmaster.pid` naming a live unrelated pid, and spawns the binary  
**Then (C)** the host reclaims ownership, signals no process, starts its own postmaster, and serves; the unrelated process is still running. [R-0116-g]

### Scenario: Data-directory resolution — default source, rejected inputs, unwritable location

**Given** the production binary  
**When (A — default)** it is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` unset, `XDG_DATA_HOME` unset and `HOME=/tmp/h` (a fresh directory)  
**Then (A)** `data_dir_resolved { path: "/tmp/h/.local/share/mnemra", source: "default", initialized: "first_run" }` is emitted and the host serves; the resolver's macOS branch is asserted by a unit test with an injected platform value, returning `/tmp/h/Library/Application Support/mnemra`.  
**And when (B — rejected)** it is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=rel/dir`, then with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` pointing at a symbolic link, then at a directory owned by another uid, then at a directory owned by the host uid with mode `0750`  
**Then (B)** each exits non-zero with `data_dir_rejected { reason: "relative" | "symlink" | "ownership" | "permissions", guidance }` before any postmaster start, and the `permissions` case's `guidance` names the path and `chmod 0700`.  
**And when (C — unwritable)** it is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` pointing at a read-only directory owned by the host uid  
**Then (C)** it exits non-zero with `data_dir_unwritable { data_dir }`. [R-0116-b]

### Scenario: Mount requirement — a missing mount is refused, and the check fails closed

**Given** the production binary on a Linux runner  
**When (A)** it is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT=1` and `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` pointing at a plain directory, then at the same path bind-mounted  
**Then (A)** the first exits non-zero with `data_dir_not_mounted { data_dir, reason: "not_mounted", guidance }` before any postmaster start, and the second serves.  
**And when (B)** it is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT=true` against the bind-mounted path, then with the variable set to `1` and `/proc/self/mountinfo` made unreadable (mount-namespace or permission trick of the harness's choosing)  
**Then (B)** the first exits with `reason: "bad_value"` and the second with `reason: "undeterminable"`; neither starts a postmaster. [R-0116-i]

### Scenario: Durability setting weakened — host refuses to serve

**Given** a test build carrying the `test-hooks` feature, spawned against a fresh `D` with an `InjectedFailure` that makes the postmaster start path pass `-F`  
**When** the host starts  
**Then** its own startup `SHOW fsync` returns `off`, the host exits non-zero with `data_dir_durability_off { data_dir: D, setting: "fsync", value: "off" }`, no MCP verb is served, the `/health` listener (bound first, R-0004-g) reports `overall: "down"` with `503`, and the same binary without the injection serves with `SHOW fsync` returning `on`. The injection seam is absent from a non-`test-hooks` build (`grep` of the release binary's symbols for `InjectedFailure` returns nothing). [R-0116-d]

### Scenario: Stored context survives replacing the executable — happy path

**Given** the clean-stop setup with `N` acknowledged writes and a captured pre-stop read set  
**When** after the clean stop, a copy of the binary at a different filesystem path is spawned with `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR=D`  
**Then** the new host starts against the same data location without re-`initdb` and every acknowledged pre-stop write reads back equal — distinguishing "at the operator's location" from "beside the executable", which a restart-only scenario cannot. The container form (a container recreated from a different image with the same volume) is parked with the image contract in Out of Scope. [R-0116-j]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Operator-run instance — all requirements met, host serves — happy path

**Given** a PostgreSQL 16 instance at or above the minor floor with `pgvector` installed in database `m`, the two roles `mnemra_owner` and `mnemra` created from `mnemra check-db --setup-sql` with passwords set through `\password` (or peer-mapped), `mnemra init` already run as `mnemra_owner`, and the production binary spawned with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL=postgres://mnemra@/m?host=/var/run/postgresql` under an OS user the instance's ident map maps to `mnemra`  
**When** the host starts  
**Then** the peer-uid and socket-directory check passes before the first byte, one `instance_check { trigger: "boot" }` record is emitted with every R-0117 hard clause `met` (R-0117-e `not_applicable (manifest_tables: 0)` while the #3591 manifest is empty; R-0117-c `met` including the ownership and membership scans), no `CREATE EXTENSION`, `SET fsync`, `SET synchronous_commit` or `ALTER ROLE … SET` statement is issued (SQL-observation seam), no password file is read, `GET /health` returns `200 { "alive": true }`, `GET /ready` returns `200 { "ready": true, "age_s": < 70 }` with no `degraded` key, `host_ready 1` is on the metrics surface, and an MCP `echo.create` over stdio succeeds. [R-0117, R-0118-a/-e/-f/-j, R-0119-c]

*(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: "a PostgreSQL 16 instance at or above the minor floor" reads: an instance on a major in R-0117-a's table, at or above that major's floor, with `pgvector` at or above R-0117-b's floor.)*

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Instance unavailable at boot — host stays up, serves nothing, becomes ready without a restart

**Given** the same configuration with the instance **stopped**, and an MCP client connected over the child's stdio  
**When** the host starts and 60 s pass  
**Then** the process is still running; `GET /health` returns `200` on every probe; `GET /ready` returns `503 { "ready": false, "reasons": [ { "clause": "R-0117-i", "reason": "unreachable", … } ], "next_retry_s": n, "last_run_at": …, "age_s": … }`; the client completes `initialize` and `tools/list`, and `echo.create` returns the JSON-RPC error `host_not_ready` whose `data` is exactly `{ "kind": "not_ready", "next_retry_s": n }` — no clause, reason or detail; the `instance_check` records are spaced 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30 s between completion and next start with `trigger: "schedule"`; exactly one `INFO` readiness log line was emitted at the transition and `host_ready{clause="R-0117-i"} 0` is on the metrics surface.  
**And when** the instance is started  
**Then** on the second consecutive `met` (hysteresis) and within two retry intervals `GET /ready` returns `200`, the same client (no reconnect) succeeds on `echo.create`, and the host's pid is unchanged. [R-0118-b/-c/-e/-f/-h/-j, R-0117-i]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Durability setting drifts while serving — readiness false within one interval, forced retry restores

**Given** a serving host connected as the run-scoped serving role, a harness session on `MNEMRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` (the harness role, which holds `CREATEROLE` and may `ALTER ROLE` the serving role), and one pooled serving connection deliberately held checked-out by an in-flight slow verb  
**When** the harness runs `ALTER ROLE <serving> SET synchronous_commit = off`  
**Then** within 30 s plus one check duration `GET /ready` returns `503` with `reasons[0] = { clause: "R-0117-d", reason: "durability_off", detail: { setting: "synchronous_commit", value: "off" } }`, `echo.create` returns `host_not_ready { kind: "not_ready" }`, and `GET /health` stays `200`.  
**And when** the harness runs `ALTER ROLE <serving> RESET synchronous_commit` and sends `SIGHUP` to the host  
**Then** within 1 s of the in-flight check completing an `instance_check { trigger: "signal" }` record is emitted with R-0117-d `met`, the pool is rebuilt — the held connection is closed on return rather than reused, and every connection handed out afterwards shows `synchronous_commit` ≠ `off` — and `GET /ready` returns `200`. [R-0117-d, R-0118-a/-c/-d, R-0120-a]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Over-privileged, owning or over-membered serving role — refused, not served

**Given** the gated binary `tests/instance_check_privileged.rs` (the only reader of `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL`) spawning the host with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` naming the instance's superuser over `verify-full` TLS  
**When** the host starts  
**Then** `GET /ready` returns `503` with `reasons[0] = { clause: "R-0117-c", reason: "role_overprivileged", detail: { attribute: "superuser" } }`, no MCP verb is served, the process stays alive; `mnemra check-db` against the same URL prints the same clause and exits `1`.  
**And given**, each created through the superuser URL by the test as a separate seeded case: a role with `BYPASSRLS`; a role with `CREATEDB`; a role that owns one table in the mnemra schema; a role granted membership of the run's owner role; a role granted `pg_read_server_files`; a role granted `pg_execute_server_program`; a role granted `pg_write_server_files`  
**Then** the reasons are, respectively, `role_overprivileged { attribute: "bypassrls" }`, `role_overprivileged { attribute: "createdb" }`, `role_owns_relation { table }`, `role_membership { role: <owner> }`, `role_membership { role: "pg_read_server_files" }`, `role_membership { role: "pg_execute_server_program" }`, `role_membership { role: "pg_write_server_files" }` — one failing case per rule — and the run-scoped serving role reports `met`. [R-0117-c, R-0118-d, R-0120-a]  
*(Amended 2026-10-20, #4119: "the only reader of `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL`" reads: the only holder of `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_URL` and `MNEMRA_TEST_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD_FILE`, read through its accessor module (R-0120-a(1) as amended). The spawned host receives the superuser URL, which carries no password, as `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` and the superuser password file as `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` — R-0119's inputs unchanged — from `env_clear()`, so the host holds neither `MNEMRA_TEST_*` variable.)*

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Non-local TCP without certificate verification — refused on configuration, before any connection

**Given** `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL=postgres://mnemra@db.example.internal:5432/m?sslmode=require` with a password file, then the same with host `127.0.0.1`  
**When** the host starts  
**Then** in both cases no connection attempt is made (no `R-0117-i` result; a packet capture or the driver's connect hook shows zero attempts), `GET /ready` returns `503` with `reasons[0] = { reason: "config_rejected", detail: { reason: "sslmode_below_floor" } }`, and the host stays alive — loopback buys no exemption.  
**And given** `…?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/etc/mnemra/db-ca.pem` against an instance whose certificate chains to that CA  
**Then** the check reports R-0117-f `met` with `pg_stat_ssl.ssl = true` for the host's backend.  
**And given** a unix-socket URL and a socket pre-created by the test in a directory owned by another uid  
**Then** the host sends zero bytes and reports `transport_unauthenticated { reason: "peer_uid_mismatch" }`.  
**And given** a unix-socket URL and a socket directory with a group write bit  
**Then** the host reports `transport_unauthenticated { reason: "socket_dir_writable" }` and sends zero bytes; the same directory under `/tmp` (sticky, root-owned) fails the owner-uid match with `reason: "peer_uid_mismatch"`.  
**And given** a fake server on `verify-full` TLS that answers the startup message with `AuthenticationCleartextPassword`, and a socket whose peer uid changes between the pre-connection check and the driver's connect  
**Then** the two pinned tests assert the host sends no password (`auth_failed { detail: "cleartext_refused" }`) and refuses the swapped peer on the same connection — both listed as named **expected failures** with their exit conditions, printed as `GATE test XFAIL …` lines (R-0120-h), never `#[ignore]`d and never a red gate. [R-0117-f, R-0119-c/-d, R-0120-h]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Password in the URL — refused; password file — read, reloaded on `SIGHUP`, never logged

**Given**, one seeded case each: `postgres://mnemra:hunter2@127.0.0.1/m`; `postgres://mnemra@127.0.0.1/m?password=hunter2`; `…?hostaddr=10.0.0.9`; `…?options=-c%20work_mem%3D1`; `postgres://mnemra@h1,h2/m`; `…?frobnicate=1`; and a spawn environment carrying `PGPASSWORD=hunter2` and `PGHOST=evil`  
**When** the host starts  
**Then** each URL case yields `503 /ready` with `detail.reason` ∈ `{ "password_in_url", "query_key_forbidden", "multi_host" }` naming the key, no connection is attempted, and the string `hunter2` appears in no emitted record; in the environment case the host's own environment carries no `PG*` variable after start and the connection goes to the configured host.  
**And given** `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL=postgres://mnemra@127.0.0.1/m?sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/etc/mnemra/db-ca.pem` with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` naming a `0600` host-owned file holding the serving role's password, and a seeded `~/.pgpass` line for the same host and user with a **different** password  
**When** the host starts, then the harness sets a new password with `\password` for the serving role, writes it to the file and sends `SIGHUP`  
**Then** the host served before the rotation using the file's password (not `.pgpass`'s); the next `instance_check` reports R-0117-i `met`; the pool is rebuilt and its new connections authenticate; neither password appears in any emitted record.  
**And given** the same file at mode `0644`, then a symlink at the path, then a root-owned `0440` file in a group the host holds  
**Then** the first two yield `503 /ready` with `reason: "credential_file_rejected"` (`detail.reason` `"mode"` and `"symlink"`), the process alive, `guidance` naming `chmod 0600` and the Kubernetes `defaultMode`, and after a rejected read following an earlier good one the pool's next attempt carries no password (the old secret was dropped); the third is accepted and the host serves. [R-0119-a/-b/-e, R-0118-b]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Schema missing — not ready with guidance; `mnemra init` applies it; host becomes ready on its next retry

**Given** a fresh database with `pgvector` installed and no mnemra schema, and the host already running against it  
**When** `GET /ready` is read  
**Then** it returns `503` with `reasons[0] = { clause: "R-0117-h", reason: "schema_missing", guidance: "run mnemra init" }`, and the host has issued no DDL.  
**And when** `mnemra init --dry-run` is run with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` naming the owner role  
**Then** it lists every pending migration with the backup warning, exits `0`, and the database is byte-unchanged.  
**And when** two `mnemra init` processes are started together as the owner role  
**Then** one runs the R-0117 owner-session clauses first, applies the migrations under the advisory lock, creates the `default` workspace, runs the final check as the serving role and exits `0`; the other exits `3` with `init_in_progress`; the migrations table holds each migration once; and the running host's next scheduled check reports R-0117-h `met` and readiness `200` with no host restart.  
**And given** a database migrated by a newer binary  
**Then** the older host's readiness is `503` with `reason: "schema_newer_than_binary"` and the guidance names running the newer binary or restoring the pre-migration backup. [R-0117-h, R-0119-g, R-0013-a as amended]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Liveness is independent of the instance; a wedged runtime is detected

**Given** a serving host, then the instance stopped for ten minutes  
**When** `GET /health` is probed every 10 s  
**Then** every probe returns `200 { "alive": true }` and the handler issues no SQL (SQL-observation seam).  
**And given** a `test-hooks` build with an injection that parks the main runtime  
**When** `GET /health` is probed  
**Then** it returns `503 { "alive": false }` within 1 s. [R-0118-e]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Stored context survives a clean stop and a hard kill of the host — restart acceptance

**Given** the production binary spawned with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` pointing at a fixture-provisioned per-run database, an MCP client over its stdio, `N` acknowledged `echo.create` writes and a captured pre-stop read set  
**When (A)** the host receives SIGTERM (or its stdin reaches EOF), drains and exits, and the binary is spawned again against the same URL  
**Then (A)** `GET /ready` returns `503 { "draining" }` during the drain and `/health` `200` until exit; the second start's check reports every hard clause `met` and R-0117-h `met` (no migration ran), and every acknowledged write reads back equal under R-0121-a's rule.  
**And when (B)** the host receives SIGKILL and is spawned again  
**Then (B)** the same read-back holds; an unacknowledged in-flight write MAY be absent. [R-0121-a/-c, R-0118-f]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): `mnemra check-db` as the operator's pre-flight — exit codes and printed SQL

**Given** the admin CLI and a fresh instance with no mnemra role  
**When** the operator runs `mnemra check-db --setup-sql`, applies its output as the instance's superuser, then runs `mnemra check-db` with `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` naming the new role  
**Then** the printed SQL creates the owner role and a `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS NOCREATEROLE NOCREATEDB NOREPLICATION` serving role, both `PASSWORD NULL`, grants `CONNECT` and schema `USAGE` to both, DML and `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` to the serving role, contains no `PASSWORD '` literal, and ends with the `\password` instruction; after `\password` for both roles, `check-db` as the serving role prints one line per R-0117 clause and exits `1` (R-0117-h `schema_missing`); after `mnemra init` as the owner role it exits `0`; against a stopped instance it exits `2`, and against a stopped instance with an over-privileged role it still exits `2` (precedence); `mnemra check-db --obligations` prints exactly the twelve R-0117-j items under the "Not verified by mnemra" heading and the docs test holds them equal to the deployment document; the printed SQL contains `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC` and `CREATE SCHEMA mnemra AUTHORIZATION`; `mnemra ready` with no listener bound exits `2` within 2 s. [R-0118-d, R-0117-j]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Graceful drain — in-flight verbs finish, new calls refused, bounded exit

**Given** a serving host with one in-flight verb that takes 3 s and an MCP client over its stdio  
**When** the host receives SIGTERM  
**Then** `GET /ready` returns `503 { "reasons": [ { "reason": "draining" } ] }` at once while `GET /health` stays `200`; a new `echo.get` returns `host_not_ready { kind: "draining" }`; the 3 s verb returns its result; a `SIGHUP` during the drain produces no `instance_check` record; the host exits `0` within 10 s.  
**And given** an in-flight verb that never completes  
**Then** the host exits non-zero at the 10 s deadline with an R-0004-a `outcome = "error"` metric for that verb; a second SIGTERM during the drain exits non-zero within 1 s. [R-0118-l]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): The checker stops reporting — readiness withdrawn on staleness

**Given** a serving host and a `test-hooks` injection that parks the check task for 80 s  
**When** `GET /ready` is read at 75 s  
**Then** it returns `503` with `reasons[0].reason = "stale_check"` and `age_s ≥ 70`, `GET /health` stays `200`, and `seconds_since_last_ok` on the metrics surface is ≥ 70; when the injection lifts, the next completed check restores `200`.  
**And given** a host whose password file is wrong, held for 120 s (the `auth_failed` backoff reaches 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64 s)  
**When** `GET /ready` is read every 5 s throughout  
**Then** every response is `503` with `reasons[0].reason = "auth_failed"` and `next_retry_s` in the future, and none reports `stale_check` — the staleness bound tracks the current backoff (R-0118-f).  
**And given** a `test-hooks` injection that panics the check task once  
**Then** an `instance_check` record with every clause `unverifiable { error: "check_task_restarted" }` is emitted, readiness is `503` for one interval, and the following scheduled run reports `met`. [R-0118-a/-f]

### Scenario (Amended 2026-10-19): Instance lost mid-write — unacknowledged, distinct error, recovery without restart

**Given** a serving host and a `test-hooks` injection that closes every pooled connection while an `echo.create` is between its `INSERT` and its `COMMIT`  
**When** the injection fires  
**Then** the caller receives `storage_unavailable` (not `host_not_ready`, not a generic error) within 5 s, the row is absent (the transaction never committed), an `instance_check { trigger: "error" }` record follows within 1 s, and readiness goes `503` with `R-0117-i unreachable` if the probe also fails; when connections are restored the host serves again on the second consecutive `met` with its pid unchanged, and a re-issued `echo.create` succeeds exactly once. [R-0118-m, R-0121-d]

## Data Model

### Content substrate — per-artifact-type tables (C1 single-document layout)

**Entity: artifact table (generalized pattern — each plugin owns its concrete table)**

| Field | Type | Constraints | Notes |
|-------|------|-------------|-------|
| `id` | `TEXT` | `PRIMARY KEY` | ULID-from-source-id |
| `workspace_id` | `UUID` | `NOT NULL`, indexed | Tenant scoping key; RLS column-shape at V0. UUID enforced at Postgres layer. See P-0001 amendment 2026-05-24. |
| `type` | `TEXT` | `NOT NULL` | Artifact type constant for this table |
| `frontmatter` | `JSONB` | `NOT NULL`, `CHECK (frontmatter ? 'id')`, `CHECK (frontmatter ? 'frontmatter_version')` | Source frontmatter stored literally; queryable structured fields |
| `body` | `TEXT` | nullable | Narrative content |
| `frontmatter_version` | `BIGINT` | `GENERATED ALWAYS AS ((frontmatter->>'frontmatter_version')::bigint) STORED` | No-drift projection; authoritative value lives in the `frontmatter` JSONB (interchange format is self-describing) |
| `migrated_from` | `TEXT` | nullable | System field; not in frontmatter JSONB |
| `migrated_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | nullable | System field; not in frontmatter JSONB |
| `created_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | `NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` | |
| `updated_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | `NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` | |

Expression indexes declared on: `(frontmatter->>'status')`, `(frontmatter->>'priority')`, `(frontmatter->>'project_id')`, `(frontmatter->>'parent_id')`.

**V0.1+ non-breaking column additions (not in `0.1.0`):** `embedding vector(1536)` (pgvector), `search_tsv tsvector GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED`.

**Entity: artifact history table (shadow table per artifact table)**

| Field | Type | Constraints | Notes |
|-------|------|-------------|-------|
| `id` | `BIGSERIAL` | `PRIMARY KEY` | |
| `artifact_id` | `TEXT` | `NOT NULL` | FK to artifact table's `id` |
| `workspace_id` | `UUID` | `NOT NULL` | Denormalized for isolation. UUID per P-0001 amendment 2026-05-24. |
| `operation` | `TEXT` | `NOT NULL` | `'UPDATE'` only (INSERT is the artifact row; DELETE is separate) |
| `old_frontmatter` | `JSONB` | `NOT NULL` | Prior row frontmatter value, byte-for-byte |
| `old_body` | `TEXT` | nullable | Prior row body value |
| `changed_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | `NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` | |

Populated by trigger on UPDATE; projections do not read from history tables.

**Entity: admin_tokens**

| Field | Type | Constraints | Notes |
|-------|------|-------------|-------|
| `id` | `UUID` | `PRIMARY KEY` | token_id; used in WorkspaceCtx for write attribution |
| `token_hash` | `BYTEA` | `NOT NULL UNIQUE` | `BLAKE3(token_bytes)`; authenticated by unique-hash lookup |
| `workspace_id` | `UUID` | `NOT NULL` | NOT NULL enforced; absence is a schema violation |
| `scopes` | `TEXT[]` | `NOT NULL` | Valid values: `["admin"]`, `["read_observer"]` |
| `created_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | `NOT NULL` | |
| `rotated_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | nullable | Set on rotation; cleared on regeneration |

**Emission record: per-verb metric (OTel/stdout — NOT an in-app table at V0)**

*Re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability): this is an emission shape, not a stored hypertable. The server emits these records to the OTel/stdout surface; where they land is the operator's choice behind the generation⊥storage separation. No in-app metrics hypertable, partition key, retention policy, or continuous aggregate exists at V0.*

| Field | Type | Notes |
|-------|------|-------|
| `workspace_id` | `UUID` | Tenant dimension; present on every metric record |
| `verb` | `TEXT` | e.g., `"task.list"`, `"task.create"` |
| `outcome` | `TEXT` | `"ok"` or `"error"` or `"timeout"` |
| `duration_ms` | `INT` | Wall-clock milliseconds (p50/p95/p99 derivable at the sink) |
| `recorded_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | Emission timestamp |

**Emission record: event (OTel/structured — NOT an in-app table at V0)**

| Field | Type | Notes |
|-------|------|-------|
| `workspace_id` | `UUID` | Tenant dimension |
| `event_type` | `TEXT` | Versioned; registry at Spec implementation stage |
| `event_version` | `INT` | DEFAULT 1; bumped on breaking event-type schema changes |
| `token_id` | `UUID` | nullable; per-token write attribution; derived by host from `WorkspaceCtx.token_id`; NOT plugin-supplied. NULL only for system-generated events with no token context. |
| `agent_id` | `UUID` | nullable; not all events are agent-originated |
| `session_id` | `UUID` | nullable |
| `payload` | `JSON` | Typed per `event_type`; never contains artifact bodies |
| `recorded_at` | `TIMESTAMPTZ` | Emission timestamp |

**Entity: WorkspaceCtx (runtime struct — Rust)**

| Field | Type | Notes |
|-------|------|-------|
| `workspace_id` | `Uuid` | From `admin_tokens.workspace_id`; NOT NULL |
| `role` | `Role` | Derived from `admin_tokens.scopes`; `Role::Admin` or `Role::ReadObserver` |
| `token_id` | `Uuid` | For per-token write attribution; maps to `admin_tokens.id` |

## API Contract

### Pagination return record (`artifact-page`)

*(Added 2026-06-29 per R-0020.)* The list direction returns a shared WIT record on both the host-fn import (`artifact.list`) and the guest export (`content.list`). It replaces the prior `list<string>` / `[{ id, frontmatter }]` list return — the return **narrows to ids-only** (no inline frontmatter; a caller resolves frontmatter via `artifact.get` per id, an accepted N+1 fan-out characterized on R-0020-b). The ids-only record is the shape locked by **intake Success criterion 1** (host-fn) and **Success criterion 2** (guest export); Frame D4 understated the import baseline (it modeled the prior return as `list<string>`, but the host-fn baseline was `[{ id, frontmatter }]` — so the import side genuinely drops inline frontmatter).

| Field | WIT type | Notes |
|-------|----------|-------|
| `ids` | `list<string>` | The page's artifact ULIDs, in `id`-ascending (creation) order |
| `has-more` | `bool` | `true` iff a further page exists; `true` **iff** `next-cursor = some` (R-0020-b invariant) |
| `next-cursor` | `option<string>` | When `some`: the `id` of the last returned row (the next call's `cursor`). `none` when `has-more = false` |

The paging input parameters added to both directions are `limit: u32` and `cursor: option<string>` (typed WIT params per R-0012-f / R-0019-b — not folded into `filters`). Host-side clamp: `effective_limit = (limit == 0 ? 100 : min(limit, 500))` (R-0020-c/-f).

### Host-fn ABI

The plugin-facing host-fn ABI. All host-fns take `WorkspaceCtx` as the first parameter (not shown in each signature below; it is structurally required). `workspace_id` does not appear as an explicit parameter on write paths.

**Required host-fns (all `core: true` plugins):**

| Function | Parameters | Return | Notes |
|----------|------------|--------|-------|
| `artifact.create` | `type: str, frontmatter: JSON, body: str?` | `id: str` | ULID assigned by host |
| `artifact.update` | `id: str, frontmatter_patch: JSON, body: str?` | `()` | Patch, not replace |
| `artifact.get` | `id: str` | `(frontmatter: JSON, body: str?)` | Scoped to `ctx.workspace_id` |
| `artifact.list` | `type: str, filters: JSON, limit: u32, cursor: str?` | `artifact-page { ids: [str], has-more: bool, next-cursor: str? }` | Keyset (cursor) pagination per R-0020. WHERE-clause mandatory on workspace; host-side clamp default 100 / cap 500; `statement_timeout` scan-cost backstop. *(Amended 2026-06-29 (R-0020): paging params + `artifact-page` return replace the prior `[{ id, frontmatter }]` shape — return narrows to ids-only; resolve frontmatter via `artifact.get`.)* |
| `metrics.record` | `verb: str, duration_ms: u64, outcome: str` | `()` | Emits a per-verb metric record (OTel/stdout); not an in-app table write |
| `log.emit` | `level: str, message: str, context: JSON?` | `()` | Emits a structured log record to stdout |
| `event.emit` | `event_type: str, event_version: u16, payload: JSON` | `()` | Emits a structured event record (OTel/stdout); not an in-app table write |
| `projection.emit` | `projection_name: str, data: JSON` | `()` | workspace_id from ctx |

**Opt-in host-fns (declared in `host_fns.required` or `host_fns.optional` as appropriate):**

| Function | Parameters | Return | Notes |
|----------|------------|--------|-------|
| `artifact.delete` | `id: str` | `()` | Must be explicitly declared; destructive |
| `sampling.request` | `prompt: str, context_ids: [str]` | `completion: str` | Content IDs only in prompt args, not bodies |
| `secrets.get` | `key: str` | `value: str` | Read-only; no write path |

### Plugin export / invocation ABI

The plugin-side export ABI the host invokes per authenticated verb (the **export** direction; the Host-fn ABI above is the **import** direction). Per [P-0013](../src/adrs/P-0013-plugin-invocation-model.md) and R-0019: a fixed typed `content` interface every content plugin exports (at V0 the substrate loads only `core: true` plugins per R-0005-g); resolved statically (plain `wit_bindgen`, no runtime registry). The retired `run(input: string) -> string` export is shown for contrast only — it SHALL NOT exist on the V0 surface.

| Export | Parameters | Return | Notes |
|--------|------------|--------|-------|
| `content.create` | `type: str, frontmatter: JSON, body: str?` | `id: str` | Host invokes per authenticated `*.create` verb |
| `content.get` | `id: str` | `option<...>` | Single artifact by id; `option` for not-found |
| `content.list` | `type: str, filters: JSON, limit: u32, cursor: str?` | `artifact-page` | Keyset pagination per R-0020. Paging params typed (no `ctx` — the host carries `WorkspaceCtx` across the export boundary); workspace-scoped via `WorkspaceCtx`. *(Amended 2026-06-29 (R-0019-a / R-0020): paging params + `artifact-page` return replace the prior `list<...>` shape.)* |
| `content.update` | `id: str, frontmatter_patch: JSON, body: str?` | `()` | Patch, not replace |
| `content.delete` | `id: str` | `()` | Destructive; gated per manifest |
| ~~`run`~~ | ~~`input: str`~~ | ~~`str`~~ | RETIRED (R-0019-a) — string-dispatch export; no string-based verb resolution at V0 |

*(Concrete typed WIT signatures for the `content` interface are a plan-tier artifact, authored with the implementation; this contract pins the interface shape and the export direction. The MCP-verb → `content`-method mapping rule is not yet pinned and is a forthcoming plan/implementation concern, not fixed by this spec — R-0019-c.)*

### Plugin manifest TOML schema (schema_version: 1)

Documented in [P-0003](../src/adrs/P-0003-plugin-manifest.md). The `[signature]` section is populated at build time by the signing chain. The runtime verifies the signature synchronously at load time.

### MCP transport

**Protocol:** MCP specification 2025-06-18  
**Transport at V0:** stdio  
**Verb namespace pattern:** `"<plugin>.<verb>"` (e.g., `"task.create"`)  
**Authentication:** Bearer token in the MCP handshake; verified on every request  
**Error codes:**  

| Error class | JSON-RPC error code | Notes |
|-------------|---------------------|-------|
| Authentication failure | Distinguished code (not -32600 / -32601 / -32602) | Not conflated with parse/method/params errors |
| Verb not found | `-32601` (method not found) | MCP-standard |
| Parameter invalid | `-32602` (invalid params) | MCP-standard |
| Plugin execution timeout (`plugin_execution_timeout`) | Custom code | Returned on epoch-deadline limit breach |
| Plugin resource exhausted (`plugin_resource_exhausted`) | Custom code | Returned on fuel-exhaustion limit breach (distinct from timeout: a compute-budget breach, not a wall-clock timeout) |
| Query scan timeout (`query_scan_timeout`) | Custom code | Returned on host read-path Postgres `statement_timeout` cancellation (R-0020-d) — a **DB scan-cost** timeout; **distinct from** `plugin_execution_timeout` (the guest epoch-deadline timeout) and `-32602` (parameter invalid); error body carries no DB/schema internals (R-0020-e no-leak posture, applied to a distinct class) |
| Permission denied | Custom code | Returned on role check failure |
| Host not ready (`host_not_ready`) *(Amended 2026-10-19 r2, #4018)* | Custom code | Returned on every verb-dispatching request while readiness is false or the host is draining (R-0118-h/-l); `data` carries **only** `kind: "not_ready" \| "draining"` and `next_retry_s` — no clause, reason or detail, because the caller is unauthenticated while the instance is unreachable; the detail is on `GET /ready` and `mnemra check-db`. Distinct from authentication failure, verb-not-found and the timeout classes. `initialize` and `tools/list` succeed |
| Storage unavailable (`storage_unavailable`) *(Amended 2026-10-19 r2, #4018)* | Custom code | Returned when a pool acquire (5 s bound) or driver call fails on a call the host had accepted while ready (R-0118-m); the write is unacknowledged; distinct from `host_not_ready` (the host was ready when it accepted the call) and from `query_scan_timeout` |

*(Amended 2026-06-19: §434 split the single plugin-execution error code into distinct epoch (`plugin_execution_timeout`) and fuel (`plugin_resource_exhausted`) caller codes; maintainer-approved, ratifies the Task-22 trap-recovery behavior.)*
*(Amended 2026-06-29 per R-0020-d: added the `query_scan_timeout` row — the host read-path `statement_timeout` cancellation gets a caller-facing code distinct from the guest-execution `plugin_execution_timeout` and from `-32602` parameter-invalid, per R-0010-f no-conflation. Triple-convergent Stage-3 review finding.)*

### Health endpoint

**Path:** `/health`  
**Methods:** GET  
**Transport:** Dedicated loopback-only TCP listener; binds to `127.0.0.1` only; port configurable via `MNEMRA_HEALTH_PORT` env var (default: `8877`). This is NOT the MCP stdio transport.  
**Gate:** The loopback-only bind IS the gate at V0 — every caller that can reach the listener is on loopback, so the detail body is served to every caller the listener can serve; there is no admin-token gating at V0. *(Named tripwire: if the listener ever binds a non-loopback interface, admin-token gating on the detail body becomes required — unauthenticated callers then receive a status-code-only `200`/`503` and only admin-token callers receive the detail body.)*  
**Response (HTTP `200` healthy / `503` when a dependency is down):**

```json
{
  "postgres": true,
  "pgvector": true,
  "workspace_default": true,
  "overall": "ok"
}
```

`"overall"` values: `"ok"` (all deps reachable), `"degraded"` (partial), `"down"` (Postgres unreachable). *(Re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability): the `timescaledb` field is removed — TimescaleDB is not a V0 substrate dependency (D8). The body reports the substrate dependencies the standalone binary owns. The health endpoint is the first API, started before config load — R-0004-g.)*

### Liveness and readiness endpoints (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018 — supersedes the single `/health` body above per R-0118)

**Transport:** the same dedicated loopback-only listener (`127.0.0.1`, `MNEMRA_HEALTH_PORT`, default `8877`), bound first; exactly two routes.

| Route | Question | `200` body | `503` body | Depends on the instance? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /health` | Is the process alive and not wedged? | `{ "alive": true, "pid": int, "uptime_s": int }` | `{ "alive": false }` (runtime did not answer an in-process ping within 1 s) | **Never** (R-0118-e) |
| `GET /ready` | Can the host serve a call now? | `{ "ready": true, "last_run_at", "age_s", "checks": [ … ] }` | `{ "ready": false, "reasons": [ { clause, status, reason, detail?, guidance? } ], "next_retry_s": int, "last_run_at", "age_s", "checks": [ … ] }`; `reasons` carries `{ "reason": "starting" }` before `mark_ready`, `{ "reason": "stale_check" }` when the last completed check is older than 2 × the current scheduled interval + 10 s (70 s while serving; tracks the backoff while not ready), and `{ "reason": "draining" }` after SIGTERM | Yes — by design (R-0118-f) |

`checks` is the most recent completed `instance_check` result list (R-0118-a): one entry per R-0117 clause, `status ∈ { met, unmet, unverifiable, not_applicable }`. There is no `degraded` key at V0 (R-0118-i). Probe mapping (R-0118-g): liveness → `/health`; readiness → `/ready`; startup → `/health` or none; pre-flight and diagnostic → `mnemra check-db` (never a probe). The listener stays loopback-only, so an orchestrator uses exec probes at V0 (`mnemra health`, `mnemra ready`: client timeout 2 s; exit `0` on `200`, `1` on `503`, `2` when the listener refuses or times out; the orchestrator's probe timeout is set `≥ 3 s`). Metrics (R-0118-j): `host_ready{clause}`, `instance_check_total{trigger,status}`, `instance_check_duration_seconds`, `seconds_since_last_ok`; documented alert `host_ready == 0` for 5 min.

### Connection configuration (per R-0119; Amended 2026-10-19 r2, #4018)

| Surface | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `MNEMRA_DATABASE_URL` (env) | `postgres://` URL from the **closed allowlist grammar** of R-0119-a, **without a password** | Parsed by the host, never by the driver; options built field by field. Accepted: user; one literal address, hostname or `host=/socket/dir`; port; database; query keys `host`, `port`, `sslmode`, `sslrootcert` only. Refused before any connection, without logging the value: password in userinfo, `password=`, `hostaddr=`, `options=`, `passfile=`, `sslkey=`/`sslcert=`, any unknown key, multi-host. *(Amended 2026-10-25, #4119: also refused, a query key that appears more than once — `duplicate_key`, naming the key (R-0119-a as amended).)* Unset → `connection_not_configured`. All are not-ready reasons, not exits (R-0118-b) |
| `PG*` environment | scrubbed at start | `PGPASSWORD`, `PGPASSFILE`, `PGHOST`, `PGHOSTADDR`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGSSLMODE`, `PGSSLROOTCERT`, `PGOPTIONS`, `PGSERVICE`, `PGSERVICEFILE` and the rest of the libpq set are removed from the host's own environment before any option is built; `~/.pgpass` is made inert (R-0119-e) |
| Transport floor (R-0119-d) | socket → peer only; TCP → `verify-full` + `sslrootcert`, loopback included | Password only over `verify-full` TCP: socket + password file → `password_over_socket`; TCP without `verify-full` → `sslmode_below_floor`; `verify-full` without `sslrootcert` → `sslrootcert_missing`; TCP without a password file → `connection_not_configured { missing: "password_file" }`. Peer-uid and socket-directory check before the first byte (R-0117-f) |
| `MNEMRA_DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` (env) | path; default `~/.config/mnemra/db-password` | Opened `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` on the descriptor on **every** read (boot, `SIGHUP`, `check-db`): regular file, no group/world write, no world read, owner = host uid or root-owned and readable via a group the host holds (the Kubernetes Secret shape, `defaultMode: 0440` + `fsGroup`). Failure → not-ready `credential_file_rejected { path, reason, guidance }` with the old secret dropped — **never an exit** (ruling 7762). The primary shape for containers |
| `MNEMRA_DB_POOL_SIZE` (env) | integer, default `8`, range `1..=64` | Serving pool maximum; the host's connection budget is pool + 1 (R-0119-f); acquire timeout 5 s (R-0118-m) |
| `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_URL`, `MNEMRA_DATABASE_OWNER_PASSWORD_FILE` (env) | same grammar and file rules; a **different role** | Read by `mnemra init` only; the serving host ignores them; owner user = serving user → `config_rejected { reason: "owner_equals_serving" }` (R-0119-g) |
| `SIGHUP` (signal) | — | Forced retry: re-read the password file, re-run the instance check (coalesced with an in-flight run), rebuild the serving pool, reset the backoff (R-0118-d); handler installed before the listener binds; ignored during a drain |
| `mnemra check-db [--setup-sql \| --obligations]` (CLI) | exit precedence `2` (unverifiable / unreachable) > `1` (unmet) > `0` | The operator's pre-flight and diagnostic, never a probe; `--setup-sql` prints the two-role setup SQL with no password literal and the `\password` instruction; `--obligations` prints the twelve R-0117-j items under "Not verified by mnemra" |
| `mnemra init [--dry-run]` (CLI) | exit `0` / non-zero; `3` = `init_in_progress` | Owner credential; advisory lock; `--dry-run` lists pending migrations with the backup warning and changes nothing (R-0117-h) |
| `mnemra ready`, `mnemra health` (CLI) | exit `0` on `200`, `1` on `503`, `2` on no listener / timeout (2 s) | Wrappers over the two routes; read `MNEMRA_HEALTH_PORT` (R-0011-d as amended) |
| `instance_check` (log record) | `{ run_at, completed_at, trigger: "boot" \| "schedule" \| "signal" \| "error" \| "cli", results: [ { clause, status, detail?, guidance? } ] }` | One per check run, on the structured-log sink (R-0118-a); readiness transitions logged at `INFO` once per transition (R-0118-j) |

### Data directory (per R-0116)

> ⛔ **SUPERSEDED (2026-10-19, #4018)** with R-0116: no surface in this table exists under the operator-run instance. Retained as the embedded-engine record.

The operator-facing configuration surface for where stored context lives, how the host reaches the cluster, and the startup errors it can raise. Startup errors are the host's own structured errors (a non-zero exit with a structured record on stdout, in the R-0005-f "refuse to start" shape), not MCP JSON-RPC codes; each carries `data_dir`, and those an operator must act on carry a one-sentence `guidance`.

| Surface | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR` (env) | absolute path | Takes precedence when set and non-empty; honored wherever it points (network-backed mounts are the operator's choice); relative values refused. Default when unset: Linux `$XDG_DATA_HOME/mnemra` (fallback `$HOME/.local/share/mnemra`), macOS `$HOME/Library/Application Support/mnemra`; no Windows default (R-0116-b) |
| `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT` (env) | exactly `1` | Set by the operator or a future image; the host refuses unless the data directory is a readable mount table's mount point (R-0116-i) |
| `<data_dir>/pg` | directory `0700` | `PGDATA`; `initdb` runs here on a first run only (R-0116-c) |
| `<data_dir>/initialized` | marker file, written last | Presence = completed first run; `pg/` without it = `data_dir_incomplete_init` (R-0116-c) |
| `<data_dir>/host.lock` | `flock`-held file, never unlinked by the host | Holder record: one UTF-8 JSON object on a single line, `{ "hostname": string, "boot_id": string, "pid": int, "pid_start_time": int (unix seconds), "engine_major": int }`, rewritten in place and `fsync`ed on reclaim; empty or unparsable → `data_dir_in_use { holder: null }` (R-0116-g) |
| `<data_dir>/sock` (or a `0700` hashed directory under the OS temp dir when the path is too long) | socket directory `0700` | The only path to the cluster; `listen_addresses = ''`; peer auth with an ident map; no role has a password (R-0116-e) |
| `mnemra --version` | prints `engine_major` | Lets an operator learn a binary's engine major before pointing it at a store (R-0116-f) |
| `data_dir_resolved` (log record) | `{ path, source: "env" \| "default", initialized: "first_run" \| "existing", socket_dir }` | Emitted before any postmaster start (R-0116-b) |
| `data_dir_rejected` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, reason: "relative" \| "symlink" \| "ownership" \| "permissions", guidance }` | Path or integrity check failed; `guidance` names the path and the one-line fix (R-0116-b) |
| `data_dir_unwritable` (startup error) | `{ data_dir }` | Cannot be created or is not writable by the host uid (R-0116-b) |
| `data_dir_incomplete_init` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, guidance }` | `pg/` present, marker absent; nothing touched (R-0116-c) |
| `data_dir_durability_off` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, setting, value }` | The host's own `SHOW` check found a durability setting weakened (R-0116-d) |
| `data_dir_version_mismatch` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, found: int \| null, engine_major, guidance }` | `PG_VERSION` major differs from the engine pin, or is unparsable; raised before any postmaster start (R-0116-f) |
| `data_dir_in_use` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, holder }` | Lock held, or the holder record is live or foreign (R-0116-g) |
| `data_dir_postmaster_ambiguous` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, pid, guidance }` | `postmaster.pid` names a live process that is not a postmaster for this directory; not signalled (R-0116-g) |
| `data_dir_not_mounted` (startup error) | `{ data_dir, reason: "not_mounted" \| "bad_value" \| "undeterminable", guidance }` | `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT` set and the directory is not a mount point, the value is not exactly `1`, or the mount table cannot be read — fails closed (R-0116-i) |

## Constraints

- Must use PostgreSQL as the sole substrate, behind an engine-agnostic swappable `Storage` trait (Postgres the only implementation), via the **embedded Postgres engine** with bundled `pgvector` (per [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md)). *(Amended 2026-06-08: TimescaleDB is no longer a V0 substrate extension — D8 demotes it off the V0 stack. Re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability): observability is emitted (stdout/OTel), not stored in-app; there is no in-app observability hypertable at V0. The observability storage backend is deferred behind the generation⊥storage separation — see R-0004, R-0013-c.)* *(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: "via the embedded Postgres engine with bundled `pgvector`" becomes "via a **separate, vanilla PostgreSQL instance the operator installs and runs**, with `pgvector` installed by the operator and verified by the host" — P-0010 §Amendment 2026-10-19. The substrate (D1), the trait (D5) and the A1-clean stack (D2) are unchanged.)*
- Must use Wasmtime for plugin execution; no alternative WASM runtime.
- Must use MCP specification 2025-06-18; no other agent-facing protocol.
- Rust-default toolchain; non-Rust paths require justification.
- License: Apache-2.0 with future-relicense clause.
- No compile-time asset embedding (project default G-0002); assets served at runtime.
- No new dependencies without license-tier review (Green/Yellow/Red tier model).
- Architecture MUST NOT be schedule-pressured; marketing-tier dates are not architectural inputs.
- **Deployment pre-condition (amended 2026-06-08 per [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md)):** The V0 engine is **embedded Postgres** (`postgresql_embedded`) shipping with the single self-hosted binary, with **`pgvector` bundled/compiled** (`pgvector_compiled`). There is NO operator-provisioned external Postgres server and NO OS-package-manager extension-install step at V0 — `mnemra init` runs `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgvector` against the embedded engine's bundled extension. TimescaleDB is not a V0 extension (D8); observability is emitted (stdout/OTel), not stored in-app, with the storage backend deferred behind the generation⊥storage separation (re-derived 2026-06-09 per [observability baseline](../src/architecture/overview.md#observability); see R-0004, R-0013-c). *(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018 — superseded by the next bullet.)*
- **Deployment pre-condition — operator-run instance (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018; per [P-0010](../src/adrs/P-0010-storage-substrate-engine.md) §Amendment 2026-10-19):** The V0 engine is a separate PostgreSQL 16 instance the operator installs and runs, with `pgvector` installed by the operator in the host's database; the host reaches it by R-0119's connection configuration and verifies R-0117 before serving. The one-time operator steps are: install Postgres 16 and pgvector; create the database; apply `mnemra check-db --setup-sql` as a privileged user; `CREATE EXTENSION vector` in the database; run `mnemra init`. There is NO embedded engine, NO first-run download, NO host-run `initdb`, `pg_ctl` or `CREATE EXTENSION`, and NO `~/.theseus` directory. How Postgres, pgvector and the host binary reach the operator's machine (packages, images, first-run install experience) is #4019's scope, not this spec's. *(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: "a separate PostgreSQL 16 instance" and "install Postgres 16 and pgvector" read: a separate PostgreSQL instance, 16 or later, on a major the host supports and at or above that major's floor (R-0117-a as amended), with pgvector at or above the host's floor (R-0117-b as amended). **Only the majors the deployment document lists are supported**; the host refuses an instance on any other major (`pg_version_unsupported`). **Upgrade order when a host release raises a floor:** patch PostgreSQL, or pgvector, to at least the new floor first, then upgrade the host; a host upgraded first refuses the instance and stays not ready until the patch lands. The deployment document SHALL state this order in its upgrade procedure, and SHALL state the supported majors, each major's floor and the pgvector floor. **The version-statement test:** a unit test in the `mnemra-host` library SHALL assert that statement equal to R-0117-a's table (each key with its floor) and R-0117-b's pgvector floor, and SHALL compare nothing else. It SHALL fail `deployment_doc_missing`, naming the path, when `docs/src/operations/deployment.md` does not exist or holds no version statement: a missing document is a failure, never a skip. It is one of the tests R-0120-c (8) as amended pins by name in `verify-test`, so it runs in the required "CI gates" check. It lands in the same change as R-0117-a's constant, so the document exists, with at least its version statement, from that change on, which may be earlier than the next constraint requires the rest of it. This spec carries none of the values. *(Binary-observable: a seeded change to a floor in the constant alone, with no document edit, turns `verify-test` red naming the version-statement test; so does a seeded change to the pgvector floor alone; with `docs/src/operations/deployment.md` removed, `verify-test` is red with `deployment_doc_missing`, not skipped and not green; the document's upgrade procedure names the patch-first order.)* [V-Consistency — the shape the obligations list already takes; P-GuaranteeByMechanism; P-TrustworthySignal — a missing input fails, never skips])*
- **Operator-run instance — the obligations the host cannot verify (Amended 2026-10-19, #4018; R-0117-j):** An operator-facing deployment document, `docs/src/operations/deployment.md`, SHALL exist by the time the R-0121-c restart acceptance lands and SHALL carry, verbatim and asserted equal to the binary's list by a docs test: the twelve R-0117-j obligations under the heading "Not verified by mnemra"; the R-0118-g probe mapping with the orchestrator probe timeout (`≥ 3 s`); the instruction that a client-side `host_not_ready` is diagnosed with `mnemra ready` and then `mnemra check-db` on the host (R-0118-h); `MNEMRA_CREDENTIAL_MOUNT=1` in the container section only (R-0119-b); the owner credential supplied to `mnemra init` on the operator's session and removed before serving (R-0119-g); the two supervision models and the daemon model's unsupported status (R-0118-k); the compose snippet the CI fresh-machine job runs (R-0120-g); the `host_ready` alert rule (R-0118-j); the one-time setup steps above including `\password` for both roles; the two local test-substrate paths (R-0120-d); the rotation procedure (R-0119-b), with the container Secret-mount shape and the bare-host peer shape each presented first in its own section; and the upgrade procedure (`init --dry-run`, backup, `init`, restore-from-backup as the way back; rolling upgrades unsupported — R-0117-h). It replaces the embedded design's `deployment.md` content (NFS guidance, `host.lock` failover, `--version` engine major, mount requirement), none of which has an object. *(Amended 2026-10-29, #4291: the document also carries the version statement of the deployment pre-condition as amended 2026-10-29 (the supported majors, each major's floor and the pgvector floor) and the patch-first upgrade order, asserted by the version-statement test that pre-condition names.)*
- **Data location and durability (per R-0116; maintainer ruling 2026-09-15):** *(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018 — SUPERSEDED with R-0116; retained as the record. The durability requirement lives in R-0121 and R-0117-d; the data-placement obligation is R-0117-j item 1, binding on the operator's instance.)* the embedded cluster's data directory lives outside the executable and its image at the operator-configured location (`MNEMRA_DATA_DIR`, or the OS per-user app-data default) — never a temporary directory, never the executable's own directory, never a container's writable layer. The production start path does not use the engine crate's temporary-directory mode, does not pass `-F` to the postmaster, and reaches the cluster only over a private unix socket by peer authentication. **Network-backed data directories are the operator's choice and the operator's responsibility to configure correctly**: the PostgreSQL 16 documentation requires an NFS data directory to be mounted `hard` (not `soft`) and exported with `sync` (not `async`), and notes that block-level storage is often preferable to NFS for a data directory. The host does not verify any of this at startup — it logs the resolved path (`data_dir_resolved`) so the operator can — and it does not rely on the mount's lock semantics to refuse a host on another machine (R-0116-g refuses on the holder record's foreign identity). An operator-facing deployment document, `docs/src/operations/deployment.md`, SHALL exist by the time the restart acceptance lands and SHALL carry: this NFS guidance verbatim; the failover manual step (confirm the previous host is stopped, delete `<data_dir>/host.lock`); the version-mismatch way out (run the previous binary; `mnemra --version` shows a binary's engine major); and the container mount requirement.

---

## Verify Contract

Fields `/verify` consumes from this spec:

- **requirements** — the RFC-2119 requirement list (R-IDs R-0001 through R-0020, plus R-0116 — numbered in the project's global `R-NNNN` series, which other specs had advanced to R-0115 before this requirement was authored), including SHALL NOT / MUST NOT prohibitions. *(Amended 2026-10-19, #4018: plus R-0117 to R-0121, assigned after `grep -rhoE 'R-0[0-9]{3}' docs/specs/ | sort -u | tail -1` returned `R-0116` at `57cfeca`; R-0116 is superseded and retained. The scenario count (ten superseded, ten added) and the out-of-scope count (two added) are reconciled at spec-exit.)*
- **scenarios** — the Given/When/Then set (41 scenarios: 9 happy path + 21 edge case + 11 failure path). Happy paths (9): the 7 prior plus stored-context-survives-clean-stop-and-restart (R-0116) and stored-context-survives-replacing-the-executable (R-0116-j). Edge cases (21): cross-workspace ABI prevention (build-time test), read-observer role denial, build-host key-on-disk trip-wire, admin CLI schema-driven generation, token rotation event ordering, artifact-list pagination DoS bounds (size cap + scan-cost timeout), artifact-list tenant isolation under pagination, artifact-list breaking return-type ABI evolution, artifact-list cursor/`limit` boundary semantics, artifact-list paging-param telemetry exclusion, artifact-list empty type (N=0), artifact-list exact-last-page boundary, artifact-list page-size cap boundary (limit 499/500/501), artifact-list foreign-but-valid cursor, artifact-list concurrent-insert-during-walk (forward-non-losing keyset), artifact-list concurrent cursor churn under pool pressure, artifact-list malformed cursor (invalid base32 alphabet), second host on an owned data directory refused (R-0116-g), stale ownership state — foreign holder / reused pid / earlier boot (R-0116-g), data-directory resolution — default / rejected incl. `permissions` / unwritable (R-0116-b), mount requirement — missing mount refused, check fails closed (R-0116-i). Failure paths (11): signing verification failure, resource limit epoch breach, admin token mismatch 401, `/health` degraded and down states, plugin fuel exhaustion breach, artifact-list scan-cost `statement_timeout` cancellation (caller-visible `query_scan_timeout`), artifact-list degraded-DB-connectivity mid-walk (structured error, resumable), stored-context-survives-hard-kill (host SIGKILL with orphaned postmaster + postmaster SIGKILL with stale `postmaster.pid`; R-0116), engine major-version mismatch refusal incl. unparsable `PG_VERSION` (R-0116-f), interrupted first run refusal (R-0116-c), durability setting weakened — host refuses to serve (R-0116-d). *(The artifact-list keyset-pagination end-to-end walk is the +1 happy-path scenario added 2026-06-29 per R-0020; the 2026-06-29 Stage-3 review fold added 9 scenarios — 7 edge + 2 failure — and corrected the failure-path membership: the prior enumeration named a non-existent "epoch-tick thread death" scenario and omitted the present `/health` degraded/down scenario. R-0116 contributes 10 scenarios — 2 happy + 4 edge + 4 failure.)*
- **out-of-scope** — the explicit prohibition boundary (29 items: one — password-based cluster access and rotation — is a recorded rejected alternative rather than a deferral, and one — the container image contract — is parked on the named trigger of the first `Dockerfile` landing)
- **data_model** — schema tables (artifact rows, admin_tokens, events, metrics) for `/verify` to validate against the running system *(the R-0020 `artifact-page` is a WIT interface return record, carried under api_contract — not a stored table; the R-0116 data-directory layout is a filesystem contract, carried under api_contract)*
- **api_contract** — host-fn ABI signatures (import direction, including the R-0020 keyset-pagination `artifact.list` signature + `artifact-page` return), the typed `content` export / invocation ABI (export direction, R-0019, including the R-0020 `content.list` paging signature), MCP transport shape, and the R-0116 data-directory surface (`MNEMRA_DATA_DIR`, `MNEMRA_DATA_DIR_REQUIRE_MOUNT`, the `<data_dir>` layout, the `data_dir_*` startup errors, the `data_dir_resolved` log record, `mnemra --version`)

**Restart-acceptance placement (planning note):** R-0116-h places the restart scenarios in the out-of-process `verify-smoke` gate so they restart the real host process and construct no engine in test code; the committed-tier plan SHALL NOT relocate them into a fixture-consuming member or add an R-0032 exception for them. The observables that read cluster state (`system_identifier`, the `SHOW` values, `pg_authid`) are read by the harness over the cluster's unix socket as the same OS user (peer authentication), with the socket path taken from the child's `data_dir_resolved` record — not through a host-side test seam; the host's own R-0116-d startup check is the in-process half of the same assertion. **Known platform gap, recorded:** CI runs Linux only (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, three `ubuntu-latest` jobs), so R-0116-b's macOS default is asserted by the injected-platform unit test only, and R-0116-g's `flock`, `kern.boottime` and `sun_path`-length branches are unexercised in CI on macOS; they are exercised on the maintainer's macOS dogfood host, which announces a failure on first use.

**White-box AC residual (RED-phase planning note, added 2026-06-29):** R-0020-a, R-0020-c, and R-0020-f carry **white-box** acceptance criteria that assert the *emitted SQL structure* — "the query emits `ORDER BY id` and `LIMIT $effective_limit + 1`" (R-0020-a), "no read path emits a `LIMIT`-less query" (R-0020-c), and "`limit = 0` emits `LIMIT 101`, never `LIMIT NULL` / `LIMIT NULLIF`-zeroed" (R-0020-f). These are **not reachable by a black-box test** of the page contract — they require a **SQL-observation seam** (a query-log capture or a `#[cfg(test)]`-gated SQL-emission hook). The RED phase SHALL provision that seam; the SQL-structure assertions are owned at **code-level review** (the security/correctness reviewer), not by the black-box scenario set. This note flags the seam so the committed-tier plan provisions it.

Per-task acceptance criteria, test expectations, and dependency declarations are **plan-tier** artifacts and live with the committed-tier plan document (authored separately when this feature moves from `designed` → `committed`). They are NOT in scope for `/verify`-at-spec-stage.
